## Dedication / 献词
===Original===

Ditchingham, 1918.

My dear Curzon,

More than thirty years ago you tried to protect me, then a stranger to you, from one of the falsest and most malignant accusations ever made against a writer.

So complete was your exposure of the methods of those at work to blacken a person whom they knew to be innocent, that, as you will remember, they refused to publish your analysis which destroyed their charges and, incidentally, revealed their motives.

Although for this reason vindication came otherwise, your kindness is one that I have never forgotten, since, whatever the immediate issue of any effort, in the end it is the intention that avails.

Therefore in gratitude and memory I ask you to accept this romance, as I know that you do not disdain the study of romance in the intervals of your Imperial work.

The application of its parable to our state and possibilities﻿—beneath or beyond these glimpses of the moon﻿—I leave to your discernment.

Believe me,

Ever sincerely yours,

H. Rider Haggard

To

**The Earl Curzon of Kedleston, K.G.**

===Chinese===

迪钦厄姆，1918年。

亲爱的寇松：

三十多年前，您曾试图保护当时与您素昧平生的我，使我免受作家所曾遭遇的最虚假、最恶毒的指控之一。

您对那伙人污蔑一个他们明知清白之人的手段的揭露，彻底到——如您记得的——他们拒不肯发表您那篇分析，因为它摧毁了他们的指控，还顺带揭穿了他们的居心。

虽然正因如此，洗雪另有他途，但您的这番善意，我从未忘怀；因为无论一时成败如何，终究是初衷最见分量。

所以，怀着感激与纪念，谨请笑纳这部传奇——我知道，在帝国的公务之余，您并不鄙薄传奇的研读。

至于书中的寓言如何映照我们的处境与前程——在这浮光掠影的尘世之下或之外——则留待您明鉴。

请相信我是

您永远的诚挚的，

H·赖德·哈格德

献给

**克尔勋爵的寇松伯爵（The Earl Curzon of Kedleston），嘉德勋章得主（K.G.）**

## I / 第一章
===Original===

Arbuthnot Describes Himself

I suppose that I, Humphrey Arbuthnot, should begin this history in which Destiny has caused me to play so prominent a part, with some short account of myself and of my circumstances.

I was born forty years ago in this very Devonshire village in which I write, but not in the same house. Now I live in the Priory, an ancient place and a fine one in its way, with its panelled rooms, its beautiful gardens where, in this mild climate, in addition to our own, flourish so many plants which one would only expect to find in countries that lie nearer to the sun, and its green, undulating park studded with great timber trees. The view, too, is perfect; behind and around the rich Devonshire landscape with its hills and valleys and its scarped faces of red sandstone, and at a distance in front, the sea. There are little towns quite near too, that live for the most part on visitors, but these are so hidden away by the contours of the ground that from the Priory one cannot see them. Such is Fulcombe where I live, though for obvious reasons I do not give it its real name.

Many years ago my father, the Rev. Humphrey Arbuthnot, whose only child I am, after whom also I am named Humphrey, was the vicar of this place with which our family is said to have some rather vague hereditary connection. If so, it was severed in the Carolian times because my ancestors fought on the side of Parliament.

My father was a recluse, and a widower, for my mother, a Scotswoman, died at or shortly after my birth. Being very High Church for those days he was not popular with the family that owned the Priory before me. Indeed its head, a somewhat vulgar person of the name of Enfield who had made money in trade, almost persecuted him, as he was in a position to do, being the local magnate and the owner of the rectorial tithes.

===Chinese===

阿巴思诺特自述

我想，我，汉弗莱·阿巴思诺特（Humphrey Arbuthnot），既然被命运推上前台，在这段历史里扮演了如此显要的角色，就该从对自己、对自身境况的简短交代写起。

四十年前，我出生在德文郡的这个村子，也就是眼下我写作的地方，只是不在同一座宅子。如今我住在"修道院旧宅"（the Priory），一处古老而自有其气派的宅邸：房间装着护壁板；园子漂亮，这地方气候温和，除了我们本土的花木，还繁茂地长着许多按理只该在更靠近太阳的国度才能见到的植物；另有绿茵起伏的草场，点缀着参天大树。景致也称得上十全：屋后屋侧，是德文郡丰饶的乡野，丘陵、谷地，红砂岩削出的崖壁；前方远处，则是大海。附近也有几座小镇，居民多半靠游客过活，但它们全被地势的起伏藏得严严实实，从"修道院旧宅"望出去，一座也看不见。这就是我居住的富尔科姆（Fulcombe），不过理由显而易见，我没有说出它的真名。

多年前，我的父亲汉弗莱·阿巴思诺特牧师——我是他唯一的孩子，名字也随了他，也叫汉弗莱——做过这里的教区牧师。据说我们家与这地方有些含糊不清的世袭渊源；若真有，到查理王朝的时代也断了线，因为我的先辈站在国会一边作战。

父亲是个隐士，又是个鳏夫：我母亲是苏格兰人，在我出生之时或出生后不久便去世了。在那个年头，他的高教会派做派很重，先于我拥有"修道院旧宅"的那户人家因此不喜欢他。那家家主名叫恩菲尔德（Enfield），靠经商发了财，人有些庸俗，几乎是刻意作践他——他也有能力这样做：他既是当地的豪绅，又是教士什一税的所有者。

===Original===

I mention this fact because owing to it as a boy I made up my mind that one day I would buy that place and sit in his seat, a wild enough idea at the time. Yet it became engrained in me, as do such aspirations of our youth, and when the opportunity arose in after years I carried it out. Poor old Enfield! He fell on evil fortunes, for in trying to bolster up a favourite son who was a gambler, a spendthrift, and an ungrateful scamp, in the end he was practically ruined and when the bad times came, was forced to sell the Fulcombe estate. I think of him kindly now, for after all he was good to me and gave me many a day’s shooting and leave to fish for trout in the river.

By the poor people, however, of all the district round, for the parish itself is very small, my father was much beloved, although he did practise confession, wear vestments and set lighted candles on the altar, and was even said to have openly expressed the wish, to which however he never attained, that he could see a censer swinging in the chancel. Indeed the church which, as monks built it, is very large and fine, was always full on Sundays, though many of the worshippers came from far away, some of them doubtless out of curiosity because of its papistical repute, also because, in a learned fashion, my father’s preaching was very good indeed.

For my part I feel that I owe much to these High-Church views. They opened certain doors to me and taught me something of the mysteries which lie at the back of all religions and therefore have their home in the inspired soul of man whence religions are born. Only the pity is that in ninety-nine cases out of a hundred he never discovers, never even guesses at that entombed aspiration, never sinks a shaft down on to this secret but most precious vein of ore.

===Chinese===

我提这桩旧事，是因为正由于它，我小时候便打定主意：将来有一天要买下那座宅子，坐上他的位子——在当时，这念头足够狂妄。可它偏偏在我心里扎了根，少年人的心气往往如此；多年之后机会一到，我果真如愿。可怜的老恩菲尔德！他时运不济：为了撑持一个宠儿——那儿子嗜赌、挥霍、忘恩负义——到头来他几乎倾家荡产，世道一坏，不得不卖掉富尔科姆的产业。如今我想起他，心怀好感：毕竟他待我不薄，给过我许多打猎的日子，还准我在河里钓鳟鱼。

不过，在周围一带的穷苦人当中——本教区自身很小——我父亲深得人心，尽管他的确听取忏悔、穿着祭衣、在祭坛上点上蜡烛，甚至据说公开表示过巴望看见圣坛里香炉摇曳的心愿，虽然这心愿他从未实现。说真的，那座教堂既出自修士之手，宏大而精美，每逢礼拜日必定坐得满满当当，尽管许多礼拜者来自远方，其中一些无疑出于好奇，慕的是它"教皇派"的名声；另外也因为，父亲讲道学养深厚，的确讲得极好。

就我自己而言，我觉得这些高教会派的观点让我受益良多。它们为我打开了几扇门，让我略略窥见一切宗教背后的奥秘；那奥秘居于人那受神启的灵魂之中，宗教正是从那里诞生。可惜的是，一百个人里倒有九十九个从不发现那被活埋的渴望，连猜也不去猜，从不曾钻井下探这条隐秘却最珍贵的矿脉。

===Original===

I have said that my father was learned; but this is a mild description, for never did I know anyone quite so learned. He was one of those men who is so good all round that he became preeminent in nothing. A classic of the first water, a very respectable mathematician, an expert in theology, a student of sundry foreign languages and literature in his lighter moments, an inquirer into sociology, a theoretical musician though his playing of the organ excruciated most people because it was too correct, a really first-class authority upon flint instruments and the best grower of garden vegetables in the county, also of apples﻿—such were some of his attainments. That was what made his sermons so popular, since at times one or the other of these subjects would break out into them, his theory being that God spoke to us through all of these things.

But if I began to drift into an analysis of my father’s abilities, I should never stop. It would take a book to describe them. And yet mark this, with them all his name is as dead to the world today as though he had never been. Light reflected from a hundred facets dissipates itself in space and is lost; that concentrated in one tremendous ray pierces to the stars.

Now I am going to be frank about myself, for without frankness what is the value of such a record as this? Then it becomes simply another convention, or rather conventional method of expressing the octoroon kind of truths with which the highly civilised races feed themselves, as fastidious ladies eat cakes and bread from which all but the smallest particle of nourishment has been extracted.

The fact is, therefore, that I inherited most of my father’s abilities, except his love for flint instruments which always bored me to distraction, because although they are by association really the most human of things, somehow to me they never convey any idea of humanity. In addition I have a practical side which he lacked; had he possessed it surely he must have become an archbishop instead of dying the vicar of an unknown parish. Also I have a spiritual sense, mayhap mystical would be a better term, which with all this religion was missing from my father’s nature.

===Chinese===

我说过父亲博学，可这话还说轻了，我从来不曾认识谁有他那样博学。他是那样一种人：样样都好，好到反倒在任何一样上都成不了第一家。一流的古典学者、相当可观的数学家、神学行家、闲时涉猎多国语言文学的学生、社会学的探究者、理论上的音乐家——虽然他弹管风琴时因过于工整而叫大多数人如坐针毡——燧石器具真正的一流权威，全郡种菜种得最好的人，苹果也种得最好：这些只是他造诣中的一部分。他的布道所以受人欢迎，正缘于此：这些学问时不时便有一门涌进布道词里，他的理论是，上帝借这一切向我们说话。

不过，倘若由着我剖析父亲的本领，怕要没完没了，那得另写一本书。然而请记住这一点：本领虽多，他的名字今日在世上却已死去，仿佛此人从未存在。从一百个棱面反射的光，在空中散逸而逝；凝聚成一道巨光，方能刺透星辰。

现在我要坦白地谈自己，因为缺乏坦白，这样的记录还有什么价值？否则它不过又是一种陈规，毋宁说又一套程式，用来表白那种掺薄到八分之一的"真相"——高度文明的种族靠这类东西滋养自己，一如讲究的太太们吃着蛋糕与面包，里头除了最微末的一点养分，早已被提炼干净。

事实是，我承袭了父亲的大部分才干，唯独没有他对燧石器具的爱好——那总叫我烦闷得发疯，因为虽说就联想而言它们实在是最富人性的东西，它们却不知怎的从没向我传达过半点人气。此外，我还有他所欠缺的实干一面；他若有这一面，想必早做了大主教，不至于以一个无名教区牧师的身份离世。我还有一种灵性的感觉——或许称它神秘倾向更妥——父亲天性里宗教尽管多，却独独缺了这样东西。

===Original===

For I think that notwithstanding his charity and devotion he never quite got away from the shell of things, never cracked it and set his teeth in the kernel which alone can feed our souls. His keen intellect, to take an example, recognised every one of the difficulties of our faith and flashed hither and thither in the darkness, seeking explanation, seeking light, trying to reconcile, to explain. He was not great enough to put all this aside and go straight to the informing Soul beneath that strives to express itself everywhere, even through those husks which are called the World, the Flesh and the Devil, and as yet does not always quite succeed.

It is this boggling over exteriors, this peering into pitfalls, this desire to prove that what such senses as we have tell us is impossible, is in fact possible, which causes the overthrow of many an earnest, seeking heart and renders its work, conducted on false lines, quite nugatory. These *will* trust to themselves and their own intelligence and not be content to spring from the cliffs of human experience into the everlasting arms of that Infinite which are stretched out to receive them and to give them rest and the keys of knowledge. When will man learn what was taught to him of old, that faith is the only plank wherewith he can float upon this sea and that his miserable works avail him nothing; also that it is a plank made of many sorts of wood, perhaps to suit our different weights?

So to be honest, in a sense I believe myself to be my father’s superior, and I know that he agreed with me. Perhaps this is owing to the blood of my Scotch mother which mixed well with his own; perhaps because the essential spirit given to me, though cast in his mould, was in fact quite different﻿—or of another alloy. Do we, I wonder, really understand that there are millions and billions of these alloys, so many indeed that Nature, or whatever is behind Nature, never uses the same twice over? That is why no two human beings are or ever will be quite identical. Their flesh, the body of their humiliation, is identical in all, any chemist will prove it to you, but that which animates the flesh is distinct and different because it comes from the home of that infinite variety which is necessary to the ultimate evolution of the good and bad that we symbolise as heaven and hell.

===Chinese===

因为我想，尽管父亲仁厚虔诚，他终究没能完全挣脱事物的外壳，不曾敲破外壳、把牙齿咬进那唯一能喂养灵魂的果仁。举例说，他敏锐的智力认得出我们信仰中的每一处难关，在黑暗里四处闪击，寻求解释，寻求光明，力图调和，力图说明。他还不够伟大，不能把这一切搁在一边，径直走向底下那个赋灵的"大灵"——它力图在处处表达自己，甚至借那些名为"世界""肉体""魔鬼"的空壳来表达，只是至今还不总能如愿。

正是这种对表皮的畏葸，这种朝陷阱里的窥探，这种非要证明我们那点感官断为不可能之事实则可能的执念，颠覆了一颗颗热切求索的心，叫它们的事业循着错误的路线进行，终归全然无用。这样的人*偏要*信任自己、信任自己的智力，不肯纵身从人类经验的悬崖跃入那"无限"伸出来承接他们的永恒臂膀——那臂膀本要赐他们安息，赐他们知识的钥匙。人要到几时才肯学会古时便教给他的道理：信心是他浮在这片海上的唯一木板，他那点可怜的造作于事无补；还得懂得，这木板由好些木料拼成，或许正是为了配上我们各不相同的体重。

所以老实说，在某种意义上我自认胜过父亲，我也知道他同意这一点。也许这得力于我苏格兰母亲的血，它同他的血融合得很好；也许因为赋予我的那点本质精神虽铸在他的模子里，实际却全然不同——或是另一种合金。我们可曾真正明白，这样的合金有千种万种、亿万种，多到大自然——或大自然背后的那个什么——从不把同一种用上第二回？正因如此，世上没有两个人是、也永远不会完全相同。他们的肉体、那"蒙羞之身"，人人无异，随便哪位化学家都能向你证明；但那赋予肉体生命的东西却各个不同，因为它来自"无限多样"的故乡——而那无限多样，正是我们所象征为天堂与地狱的善与恶得以终极演化的必要条件。

===Original===

Further, I had and to a certain extent still have another advantage over my father, which certainly came to me from my mother, who was, as I judge from all descriptions and such likenesses as remain of her, an extremely handsome woman. I was born much better looking. He was small and dark, a little man with deep-set eyes and beetling brows. I am also dark, but tall above the average, and well made. I do not know that I need say more about my personal appearance, to me not a very attractive subject, but the fact remains that they called me “handsome Humphrey” at the University, and I was the captain of my college boat and won many prizes at athletic sports when I had time to train for them.

Until I went up to Oxford my father educated me, partly because he knew that he could do it better than anyone else, and partly to save school expenses. The experiment was very successful, as my love of all outdoor sports and of any small hazardous adventure that came to my hand, also of associating with fisherfolk whom the dangers of the deep make men among men, saved me from becoming a milksop. For the rest I learned more from my father, whom I always desired to please because I loved him, than I should have done at the best and most costly of schools. This was shown when at last I went to college with a scholarship, for there I did very well indeed, as search would still reveal.

Here I had better set out some of my shortcomings, which in their sum have made a failure of me. Yes, a failure in the highest sense, though I trust what Stevenson calls “a faithful failure.” These have their root in fastidiousness and that lack of perseverance, which really means a lack of faith, again using the word in its higher and wider sense. For if one had real faith one would always persevere, knowing that in every work undertaken with high aim, there is an element of nobility, however humble and unrecognised that work may seem to be. God after all is the God of Work, it is written large upon the face of the Universe. I will not expand upon the thought; it would lead me too far afield, but those who have understanding will know what I mean.

===Chinese===

此外，我还有——在某种程度上如今仍有——一样胜过父亲的长处，它无疑得自母亲。从种种描述和她仅存的几幅肖像判断，母亲是位极其俊美的女子，我生来便比他好看得多。他又矮又黑，个子小，眼窝深陷，眉毛浓重隆起。我也黑，但比常人高，体格匀称。关于我的相貌，本不必多说，这话题于我并无吸引力，但事实终究是事实：在大学里人家叫我"英俊汉弗莱"，我当过学院划船队的队长，腾得出工夫训练时，还在各项体育运动里得过不少奖。

进牛津之前，一直由父亲亲自教我，一半因为他知道自己教得比谁都好，一半为了省下上学的花销。这场试验十分成功：我热爱一切户外运动，热爱随手可得的任何小小的冒险，也爱与渔人往来——深海的危险使他们成为人中之杰——这救了我，没让我变成奶油小生。至于学问，我从一个因为我爱他而总想讨他欢心的人身上学到的东西，比在最贵最好的学校里学到的还多。这一点，在我终于带着奖学金进了大学时得到证明：我在那里确实成绩斐然，如今查考，仍可复按。

在此我最好摆一摆自己的短处，它们加在一起，把我造成了一个失败者。是的，最高意义上的失败，不过我但愿自己算得上史蒂文森（Stevenson）所谓"忠实的失败者"。这些短处植根于苛求，以及缺乏毅力——后者实际意味着缺乏信仰，仍是就这词更高、更广的意义而说。因为人若有真信仰，总归会坚持到底，深知凡怀着高尚目标从事的工作，无论显得多么卑微、多么无人赏识，其中必有一点高贵的成分。上帝终究是"工作"的上帝，这话大书特书在宇宙的面上。这层意思我不展开了，那会岔得太远，但有悟性的人自会明白我的意思。

===Original===

As regards what I interpret as fastidiousness, this is not very easy to express. Perhaps a definition will help. I am like a man with an over-developed sense of smell, who when walking through a foreign city, however clean and well kept, can always catch the evil savours that are inseparable from such cities. More, his keen perception of them interferes with all other perceptions and spoils his walks. The result is that in after years, whenever he thinks of that beautiful city, he remembers, not its historic buildings or its wide boulevards, or whatever it has to boast, but rather its ancient, fish-like smell. At least he remembers that first owing to this defect in his temperament.

So it is with everything. A lovely woman is spoiled for such a one because she eats too much or has too high a voice; he does not care for his shooting because the scenery is flat, or for his fishing because the gnats bite as well as the trout. In short he is out of tune with the world as it is. Moreover, this is a quality which, where it exists, cannot be overcome; it affects day-labourers as well as gentlemen at large. It is bred in the bone.

Probably the second failure-breeding fault, lack of perseverance, has its roots in the first, at any rate in my case. At least on leaving college with some reputation, I was called to the Bar where, owing to certain solicitor and other connections, I had a good opening. Also, owing to the excellence of my memory and powers of work, I began very well, making money even during my first year. Then, as it happened, a certain case came my way and, my leader falling ill suddenly after it was opened, was left in my hands. The man whose cause I was pleading was, I think, one of the biggest scoundrels it is possible to conceive. It was a will case and if he won, the effect would be to beggar two most estimable middle-aged women who were justly entitled to the property, to which end personally I am convinced he had committed forgery; the perjury that accompanied it I do not even mention.

===Chinese===

至于我所谓苛求者，不太容易说清，或许一个比方有用。我像极了嗅觉过分发达的人：走过任何一座异乡城市，无论它多么洁净、打理得多么好，他总能嗅出这类城市摆脱不掉的恶浊气味。还不止此，对这些气味的敏锐察觉干扰了他其余的一切感知，毁了他散步的兴致。结果是，多年之后，每当想起那座美丽的城市，他记住的不是它的历史建筑、宽阔的林荫道，不是它任何值得夸口的东西，而是那股陈年的鱼腥气。至少，缘着他性情的这个缺陷，他最先想起的便是这个。

凡事皆然。对这样的人，可爱的女人也会毁了：只因她吃得太多，或嗓门太高；打猎没了味道，只因风景平淡；钓鱼没了味道，只因蚊子同鳟鱼一样咬人。一句话，他同这个现实的世界合不上调。况且，这是一种既已存在便无法克服的品性；它缠住绅士，也一样缠住卖力气打短工的人。它长在骨头里。

至于第二个招致失败的毛病，缺乏毅力，多半植根于头一个毛病，至少在我身上如此。至少，我离开学院时小有名声，取得了律师资格，凭着几位诉状律师和其他一些关系，在那一行里开局很好。加上记性出众、办事得力，我开头极顺，头一年便赚了钱。后来恰巧一桩案子落到我手里：开庭之后，我上头的首席律师突然病倒，案子便留在我手上。我为之辩护的那位当事人，我想，是想象得出的最大的无赖之一。那是一桩遗嘱案，他若胜诉，后果便是叫两位极可敬的中年妇人倾家荡产，而那产业理应归她们所有；为了这个目的，我本人确信他伪造了文书，相随而来的伪证更不必提了。

===Original===

Well, he did win, thanks to me, and the estimable middle-aged ladies were beggared, and as I heard afterwards, driven to such extremities that one of them died of her misery and the other became a lodging-house keeper. The details do not matter, but I may explain that these ladies were unattractive in appearance and manner and broke down beneath my cross-examination which made them appear to be telling falsehoods, whereas they were only completely confused. Further, I invented an ingenious theory of the facts which, although the judge regarded it with suspicion, convinced an unusually stupid jury who gave me their verdict.

Everybody congratulated me and at the time I was triumphant, especially as my leader had declared that our case was impossible. Afterwards, however, my conscience smote me sorely, so much so that arguing from the false premise of this business, I came to the conclusion that the practice of the Law was not suited to an honest man. I did not take the large view that such matters average themselves up and that if I had done harm in this instance, I might live to do good in many others, and perhaps become a just judge, even a great judge. Here I may mention that in after years, when I grew rich, I rescued that surviving old lady from her lodging-house, although to this day she does not know the name of her anonymous friend. So by degrees, without saying anything, for I kept on my chambers, I slipped out of practice, to the great disappointment of everybody connected with me, and took to authorship.

A marvel came to pass, my first book was an enormous success. The whole world talked of it. A leading journal, delighted to have discovered someone, wrote it up; other journals followed suit to be in the movement. One of them, I remember, which had already dismissed it with three or four sneering lines, came out with a second and two-column notice. It sold like wildfire and I suppose had some merits, for it is still read, though few know that I wrote it, since fortunately it was published under a pseudonym.

===Chinese===

结果他果真胜诉，多亏了我；两位可敬的中年妇人倾了家，后来我听说，她们被逼到山穷水尽：一个在愁苦中死去，另一个做了公寓老板娘。细节无关紧要，但我可以说明：这两位妇人生相与举止都不讨人喜欢，在我的盘问之下垮了，显得满口谎言，其实她们不过是彻底乱了方寸。再有，我就案情编出一套精巧的理论，法官虽以疑虑的眼光看它，却说服了一个格外愚蠢的陪审团，他们把裁决给了我。

人人都来道贺，当时我意气昂扬，何况首席律师原说过这案子毫无指望。然而过后，良心痛责于我，痛得厉害，以致我从这桩买卖的错误前提出发，得出结论：操律师这一行，不适合一个诚实的人。我没有从大处着眼——此类事情自会扯平，这一回我作了恶，或许往后能在许多别的事上行善，说不定还能做个公正的法官，甚至伟大的法官。这里不妨一提：多年之后我发了财，把那位尚在人世的老妇人从她的公寓里赎了出来，她至今不知道这位匿名恩人的姓名。于是我逐渐地、不动声色地退出了执业——事务所照旧留着——惹得一切与我相干的人大失所望，我则改行去做了作家。

奇迹出现了：我的第一本书大获成功，全世界都在谈论它。一家大报因为发掘出个新人而欣喜若狂，连篇累牍地捧；其他报纸纷纷跟进，不肯落在这个运动之外。我记得其中一家，先前只用三四行讥诮的字句把它打发掉，这时又登出一篇二次评论，占了整整两栏。书像野火一样卖开去，我想总有些长处，至今仍有人读，虽然极少有人知道作者是我——幸好，它出版时用的是笔名。

===Original===

Again I was much elated and set to work to write another and, as I believe, a much better book. But jealousies had been excited by this leaping into fame of a totally unknown person, which were, moreover, accentuated through a foolish article that I published in answer to some criticisms, wherein I spoke my mind with an insane freedom and biting sarcasm. Indeed I was even mad enough to quote names and to give the example of the very powerful journal which at first carped at my work and then gushed over it when it became the fashion. All of this made me many bitter enemies, as I found out when my next book appeared.

It was torn to shreds, it was reviled as subversive of morality and religion, good arrows in those days. It was called puerile, half-educated stuff﻿—I half-educated! More, an utterly false charge of plagiarism was cooked up against me and so well and venomously run that vast numbers of people concluded that I was a thief of the lowest order. Lastly, my father, from whom the secret could no longer be kept, sternly disapproved of both these books which I admit were written from a very radical and somewhat anti-church point of view. The result was our first quarrel and before it was made up, he died suddenly.

---

Now again fastidiousness and my lack of perseverance did their work, and solemnly I swore that I would never write another book, an oath which I have kept till this moment, at least so far as publication is concerned, and now break only because I consider it my duty so to do and am not animated by any pecuniary object.

Thus came to an end my second attempt at carving out a career. By now I had grown savage and cynical, rather revengeful also, I fear. Knowing myself to possess considerable abilities in sundry directions, I sat down, as it were, to think things over and digest my past experiences. Then it was that the truth of a very ancient adage struck upon my mind, namely, that money is power. Had I sufficient money I could laugh at unjust critics for example; indeed they or their papers would scarcely dare to criticise me for fear lest it should be in my power to do them a bad turn. Again I could follow my own ideas in life and perhaps work good in the world, and live in such surroundings as commended themselves to me. It was as clear as daylight, but﻿—how to make the money?

===Chinese===

我又一次大为得意，动手去写另一本书，而且我相信，比头一本好得多。可是，一个全然无名的人这样一跃成名，早已惹起妒火；我又发表一篇愚蠢的文章回敬几条批评，火上浇油——文中我以疯狂的放肆和刻薄的讥讽直抒胸臆，竟疯到点名道姓，还举了那家权势熏天的大报做例子：它起先挑剔我的作品，等作品成了风尚，又对它大肆吹捧。这一切给我结下许多死敌，下一本书问世时我便知道了。

它被撕成碎片，被痛詈为伤风败俗、颠覆宗教——在那个年头，这两支箭最好使。它被称作幼稚、半吊子的货色——我半吊子！更有甚者，有人给我罗织了一桩彻头彻尾虚假的剽窃罪名，运作得又周到又恶毒，以致无数人断定我是个下三滥的文贼。最后，父亲——那秘密再也瞒他不住了——对这两本书严词不悦；我承认，这两本书确实写得十分激进，观点也颇有些反教会。结果是我们头一次争吵，还没和好，他便猝然去世了。

---

于是，苛求与缺乏毅力这两个毛病又故技重施，我郑重发誓再不写书。这誓言我守到此刻——至少就出版而论——如今打破它，只因我认为这是我的本分，绝非受任何图利之心的驱使。

就这样，我第二次开凿前程的尝试告了终。到这时，我已变得粗野而愤世，恐怕还有几分记仇。自知在好几个方面颇有才干，我便坐下来——可以说——把事情细细想了一遍，消化过往的经历。就在这时，一句极古老的格言的真理击中了我的心：金钱就是权力。假使我有足够的钱，比方说，对不公的批评家尽可付之一笑；实则他们或他们的报纸几乎不敢再批评我，生怕我有本事叫他们吃不了兜着走。再者，我可以在人生中走自己的路子，或许还能在世上做点好事，住进自己中意的环境里。道理明如白昼，可是——钱从哪里来？

===Original===

I had some capital as the result of my father’s death, about £8,000 in all, plus a little more that my two books had brought in. In what way could I employ it to the best advantage? I remembered that a cousin of my father and therefore my own, was a successful stockbroker, also that there had been some affection between them. I went to him, he was a good, easy-natured man who was frankly glad to see me, and offered to put £5,000 into his business, for I was not minded to risk everything I had, if he would give me a share in the profits. He laughed heartily at my audacity.

“Why, my boy,” he said, “being totally inexperienced at this game, you might lose us more than that in a month. But I like your courage, I like your courage, and the truth is that I do want help. I will think it over and write to you.”

He thought it over and in the end offered to try me for a year at a fixed salary with a promise of some kind of a partnership if I suited him. Meanwhile my £5,000 remained in my pocket.

I accepted, not without reluctance since with the impatience of youth I wanted everything at once. I worked hard in that office and soon mastered the business, for my knowledge of figures﻿—I had taken a first-class mathematical degree at college﻿—came to my aid, as in a way did my acquaintance with Law and Literature. Moreover I had a certain aptitude for what is called high finance. Further, Fortune, as usual, showed me a favourable face.

In one year I got the partnership with a small share in the large profits of the business. In two the partner above me retired, and I took his place with a third share of the firm. In three my cousin, satisfied that it was in able hands, began to cease his attendance at the office and betook himself to gardening which was his hobby. In four I paid him out altogether, although to do this I had to borrow money on our credit, for by agreement the title of the firm was continued. Then came that extraordinary time of boom which many will remember to their cost. I made a bold stroke and won. On a certain Saturday when the books were made up, I found that after discharging all liabilities, I should not be worth more than £20,000. On the following Saturday but two when the books were made up, I was worth £153,000! *L’appétit vient en mangeant.* It seemed nothing to me when so many were worth millions.

===Chinese===

父亲身后给我留下一笔本金，合计约八千镑，外加两本书挣来的一点小钱。怎样用它才最合算？我想起父亲的一位堂兄弟——自然也是我的堂叔——是个很得法的股票经纪人，他们之间也还有些情分。我去找他。他是个好心、好脾气的人，见了我坦率地高兴。我提出把五千镑投入他的买卖——我不打算把全部身家押上——条件是让我分一份利润。听我这样大胆，他放声大笑。

"哎呀，我的孩子，"他说，"你在这门生意里毫无经验，弄不好一个月就能叫我们亏掉不止这个数。不过我喜欢你的胆量，我喜欢你的胆量。老实说，我也确实需要帮手。让我想想，再写信给你。"

他想了想，最后答应按固定薪金试用我一年，倘若我合他的意，再许我某种合伙的名分。与此同时，那五千镑依然躺在我口袋里。

我接受了，虽不无勉强，因为怀着年轻人的性急，我恨不得事事一步到位。我在那间行号里苦干，很快摸透了生意：我精通数字——在大学拿过一等数学学位——帮了大忙，对法律与文学的熟稔多少也帮了忙。此外，对所谓的高等金融，我颇有一点天分。再者，命运女神照例向我露出了好脸色。

一年之后，我拿到了合伙人的身份，在这笔大生意的利润里分得一小股。两年之后，我上头那位合伙人退休，我接了他的位子，占有行号三分之一的股份。三年之后，堂叔眼见生意交在能人手里放了心，渐渐不大到行里来，一门心思扑在他的爱好——园艺上。四年之后，我把他全数付清、买了出来；为此我不得不凭行号的信用举债，因为照约定，行号的字号仍沿用下去。接着便来了那段异乎寻常的景气岁月，许多人至今想起犹有余痛。我放手一搏，赢了。某个星期六结账，我发现清偿一切债务之后，我的身家不过两万镑。第二个星期六再结账时，我已值十五万三千镑！*L'appétit vient en mangeant.*（胃口越吃越大。）当那么多的人身家以百万计，这点钱在我眼里简直不算什么。

===Original===

For the next year I worked as few have done, and when I struck a balance at the end of it, I found that on the most conservative estimate I was the owner of a million and a half in hard cash, or its equivalent. I was so tired out that I remember this discovery did not excite me at all. I felt utterly weary of all wealth-hunting and of the City and its ways. Moreover my old fastidiousness and lack of perseverance reasserted themselves. I reflected, rather late in the day perhaps, on the ruin that this speculation was bringing to thousands, of which some lamentable instances had recently come to my notice, and once more considered whether it were a suitable career for an upright man. I had wealth; why should I not take it and enjoy life?

Also﻿—and here my business acumen came in, I was sure that these times could not last. It is easy to make money on a rising market, but when it is falling the matter is very different. In five minutes I made up my mind. I sent for my junior partners, for I had taken in two, and told them that I intended to retire at once. They were dismayed both at my loss, for really I was the firm, and because, as they pointed out, if I withdrew all my capital, there would not be sufficient left to enable them to carry on.

One of them, a blunt and honest man, said to my face that it would be dishonourable of me to do so. I was inclined to answer him sharply, then remembered that his words were true.

“Very well,” I said, “I will leave you £600,000 on which you shall pay me five percent interest, but no share of the profits.”

On these terms we dissolved the partnership and in a year they had lost the £600,000, for the slump came with a vengeance. It saved them, however, and today they are earning a reasonable income. But I have never asked them for that £600,000.

===Chinese===

接下来那一年，我苦干得少有人及；年终一结账，我发现，即便按最保守的估算，我也已拥有整整一百五十万镑的现金或等值资产。我累得厉害，记得这一发现竟不曾叫我有半分激动。我对猎取财富、对伦敦城和它的那一套，已经厌倦到了极点。何况旧日的苛求与缺乏毅力又回过头来作祟。我想起——也许想得晚了——这场投机正把成千上万的人推向毁灭，近来就有几桩惨事进了我的耳目；我又一次掂量，这行当究竟适不适合一个正直的人。我已有钱，何不拿了它去享受人生？

再者——这里我的生意眼光派上了用场——我确信这样的年月长不了。市面上涨时赚钱容易；市面一下跌，可就完全是另一回事了。不出五分钟我便拿定了主意。我把手下的两个少壮合伙人——我后来添了两个——召来，告诉他们我打算即刻引退。他们大为沮丧，一则痛惜我抽身——说真的，我就是这间行号——二则因为，他们指出，倘若我撤走全部资本，剩下来的本钱不足以让他们把生意撑下去。

其中一个，人直率也诚实，当面对我说，我这样做有失体面。我本想抢白他一句，随即想起他说的是实话。

"那好，"我说，"我给你们留下六十万镑，你们按五厘付我利息，利润就不必分给我了。"

我们便在这样的条件下散了伙。一年之内，他们把那六十万亏了个干净，因为暴跌来势汹汹。不过这一跌倒救了他们，如今他们挣着一份还过得去的进项。但那六十万镑，我从来没有向他们讨还。

## II / 第二章
===Original===

Bastin and Bickley

Behold me once more a man without an occupation, but now the possessor of about £900,000. It was a very considerable fortune, if not a large one in England; nothing like the millions of which I had dreamed, but still enough. To make the most of it and to be sure that it remained, I invested it very well, mostly in large mortgages at four percent which, if the security is good, do not depreciate in capital value. Never again did I touch a single speculative stock, who desired to think no more about money. It was at this time that I bought the Fulcombe property. It cost me about £120,000 of my capital, or with alterations, repairs, etc., say £150,000, on which sum it may pay a net two and a half percent, not more.

This £3,700 odd I have always devoted to the upkeep of the place, which is therefore in first-rate order. The rest I live on, or save.

These arrangements, with the beautifying and furnishing of the house and the restoration of the church in memory of my father, occupied and amused me for a year or so, but when they were finished time began to hang heavy on my hands. What was the use of possessing about £20,000 a year when there was nothing upon which it could be spent? For after all my own wants were few and simple and the acquisition of valuable pictures and costly furniture is limited by space. Oh! in my small way I was like the weary King Ecclesiast. For I too made me great works and had possessions of great and small cattle (I tried farming and lost money over it!) and gathered me silver and gold and the peculiar treasure of kings, which I presume means whatever a man in authority chiefly desires, and so forth. But “behold all was vanity and vexation of spirit, and there was no profit under the sun.”

===Chinese===

巴斯廷与比克利

请看我，又一次成了无业之人，如今却坐拥约九十万镑。这份家财即便在英格兰算不得巨大，也已是相当可观；远不如我梦寐以求的数百万，可也够了。为使它尽量生利、并确保不再缩水，我把它投资得很稳妥，多半换成四厘的大宗抵押贷款——只要抵押品可靠，本金不会贬值。此后我再没碰过一分钱的投机股票，因为我已不愿再为金钱操心。就在这时，我买下了富尔科姆的产业。本金花了约十二万镑，连上改建、修缮等等，算是十五万镑；这笔数目，每年能有净两厘五的收益，至多如此。

这三千七百镑上下，我一向用来维持这处产业的开销，因此宅子始终井井有条。其余的，我便用来过活，或者存起来。

这些安排，连同宅子的美化陈设、为纪念父亲而重修的教堂，占去我一年左右的光阴，也给了我消遣；可等一切完工，日子又渐渐变得难熬。每年有近两万镑的进项，却无处可花，这笔钱有什么用？毕竟我自身的欲求少而简单，而收藏名画贵器，又受着空间的限制。唉！小小的我，活像传道书里那位厌倦的君王。因为我也是大兴土木，也拥有大大小小的牲畜（我试着经营过农庄，赔了钱！），也积攒金银与"君王特别的财宝"——我猜这指的是当权者心里最想要的东西——如此等等。然而"看哪，一切都是虚空，都是捕风，日光之下并无益处"。

===Original===

So, notwithstanding my wealth and health and the deference which is the rich man’s portion, especially when the limit of his riches is not known, it came about that I too “hated life,” and this when I was not much over thirty. I did not know what to do; for Society as the word is generally understood, I had no taste; it bored me; horse-racing and cards I loathed, who had already gambled too much on a big scale. The killing of creatures under the name of sport palled upon me, indeed I began to doubt if it were right, while the office of a junior county magistrate in a place where there was no crime, only occupied me an hour or two a month.

Lastly my neighbours were few and with all due deference to them, extremely dull. At least I could not understand them because in them there did not seem to be anything to understand, and I am quite certain that they did not understand me. More, when they came to learn that I was radical in my views and had written certain “dreadful” and somewhat socialistic books in the form of fiction, they both feared and mistrusted me as an enemy to their particular section of the race. As I had not married and showed no inclination to do so, their womenkind also, out of their intimate knowledge, proclaimed that I led an immoral life, though a little reflection would have shown them that there was no one in the neighbourhood which for a time I seldom left, who could possibly have tempted an educated creature to such courses.

Terrible is the lot of a man who, while still young and possessing the intellect necessary to achievement, is deprived of all ambition. And I had none at all. I did not even wish to purchase a peerage or a baronetcy in this fashion or in that, and, as in my father’s case, my tastes were so many and so catholic that I could not lose myself in any one of them. They never became more than diversions to me. A hobby is only really amusing when it becomes an obsession.

At length my lonesome friendlessness oppressed me so much that I took steps to mitigate it. In my college life I had two particular friends whom I think I must have selected because they were so absolutely different from myself.

===Chinese===

于是，尽管我富有、康健，享着富人应得的那份恭维——尤其当人家并不知道他的财产究竟有多厚——我也照样"恨恶生命"，而那时我刚过三十岁不多。我不知道该做什么。至于一般意义上的社交界，我没有兴趣，它叫我厌烦；赛马和牌戏我憎恶，我已在大规模赌博上下过太多的注。以运动之名杀戮生灵，也叫我兴味索然，说真的，我开始怀疑这是否正当；而在一个并无罪案的地方担任基层治安法官，每月不过占去我一两个钟头。

还有，我的邻人不多，而且恕我直言，都乏味得很。至少我理解不了他们，因为他们身上似乎没有任何可以理解的东西；我同样敢肯定，他们理解不了我。再者，当他们得知我观点激进，还写过某些"可怕"的、多少带社会主义色彩的小说时，他们便又怕又疑，把我当作他们那一族群的敌人。由于我未娶，也毫无娶的意思，他们的女眷们便凭着亲身的见识，宣称我过着不道德的生活，虽然稍一思索本该叫她们明白：在这个我一度很少离开的地方，根本没有谁可能引诱一个有教养的生灵走上那条路。

一个正当年富、又有成事所需智力的人，却被剥夺了一切抱负，他的境遇是可怕的。而我恰恰毫无抱负。我甚至不想通过这种那种方式买个贵族头衔或从男爵名分；又像我父亲那样，我的趣味五花八门、包罗万象，不能让我在其中任何一样里忘我沉潜。它们对我至多是消遣。只有当爱好变成痴迷，它才真正有趣。

大学时代我有两位特别的朋友，我想我选中他们，正因为他们跟我截然不同。

===Original===

They were named Bastin and Bickley. Bastin﻿—Basil was his Christian name﻿—was an uncouth, shock-headed, flat-footed person of large, rugged frame and equally rugged honesty, with a mind almost incredibly simple. Nothing surprised him because he lacked the faculty of surprise. He was like that kind of fish which lies at the bottom of the sea and takes every kind of food into its great maw without distinguishing its flavour. Metaphorically speaking, heavenly manna and decayed cabbage were just the same to Bastin. He was not fastidious and both were mental pabulum﻿—of a sort﻿—together with whatever lay between these extremes. Yet he was good, so painfully good that one felt that without exertion to himself he had booked a first-class ticket straight to Heaven; indeed that his guardian angel had tied it round his neck at birth lest he should lose it, already numbered and dated like an identification disc.

I am bound to add that Bastin never went wrong because he never felt the slightest temptation to do so. This I suppose constitutes real virtue, since, in view of certain Bible sayings, the person who is tempted and would like to yield to the temptation, is equally a sinner with the person who does yield. To be truly good one should be too good to be tempted, or too weak to make the effort worth the tempter’s while﻿—in short not deserving of his powder and shot.

I need hardly add that Bastin went into the Church; indeed, he could not have gone anywhere else; it absorbed him naturally, as doubtless Heaven will do in due course. Only I think it likely that until they get to know him he will bore the angels so much that they will continually move him up higher. Also if they have any susceptibilities left, probably he will tread upon their toes﻿—an art in which I never knew his equal. However, I always loved Bastin, perhaps because no one else did, a fact of which he remained totally unconscious, or perhaps because of his brutal way of telling one what he conceived to be the truth, which, as he had less imagination than a dormouse, generally it was not. For if the truth is a jewel, it is one coloured and veiled by many different lights and atmospheres.

===Chinese===

他们名叫巴斯廷（Bastin）和比克利（Bickley）。巴斯廷——他的教名是巴兹尔——是个粗笨的家伙，头发蓬乱，平足，身架高大粗壮，为人也如身材一般粗直方正，头脑简单得叫人难以置信。什么都不曾让他吃惊，因为他缺少吃惊的能力。他像海底那种鱼：趴在洋底，不分滋味，什么食物都吞进那张大嘴里。打个比方，天降的吗哪和烂白菜，在巴斯廷嘴里是一个味道。他不挑剔，两样都是精神食粮——算是一种食粮——连同介于这两极之间的一切。然而他人好，好得让人心疼，让人觉得他不费吹灰之力，就订好了一张直达天堂的头等车票；实在说，他的守护天使在他出生时就把它挂上了他的脖子，免得他丢失——票已编号印期，像一块身份牌。

我必须补一句：巴斯廷从不出错，因为他从没感到过丝毫作错的诱惑。我想这构成了真正的德行，因为照某些圣经章句的说法，受诱惑而想屈从的人，与屈从的人同样是罪人。要真正地善，就该善到不受诱惑，或者弱到不值得诱惑者在他身上耗费火药铅弹——总之，不配他那一番工夫。

不必说，巴斯廷进了教会；他实在也去不了别处，教会自然而然地吸收了他，正如到了时候天堂无疑也会这样做。只是我想，在天使们了解他之前，他多半会把他们烦得不住把他往上挪。再有，倘若天使们还剩几分敏感，他多半还要踩他们的脚趾——这门手艺，我从没见过他的对手。可我一向喜欢巴斯廷，也许因为没有别人喜欢他——这一点他自己全然不觉；也许因为他那种不管不顾、直陈他所谓真理的莽劲，而由于他的想象力还不及一只睡鼠，那通常并非真理。因为真理若是一颗宝石，它也是被种种不同的光线与氛围染色、遮蔽的宝石。

===Original===

It only remains to add that he was learned in his theological fashion and that among his further peculiarities were the slow, monotonous voice in which he uttered his views in long sentences, and his total indifference to adverse argument however sound and convincing.

My other friend, Bickley, was a person of a quite different character. Like Bastin, he was learned, but his tendencies faced another way. If Bastin’s omnivorous throat could swallow a camel, especially a theological camel, Bickley’s would strain at the smallest gnat, especially a theological gnat. The very best and most upright of men, yet he believed in nothing that he could not taste, see or handle. He was convinced, for instance, that man is a brute-descended accident and no more, that what we call the soul or the mind is produced by a certain action of the grey matter of the brain; that everything apparently inexplicable has a perfectly mundane explanation, if only one could find it; that miracles certainly never did happen, and never will; that all religions are the fruit of human hopes and fears and the most convincing proof of human weakness; that notwithstanding our infinite variations we are the subjects of Nature’s single law and the victims of blind, black and brutal chance.

Such was Bickley with his clever, well-cut face that always reminded me of a cameo, and thoughtful brow; his strong, capable hands and his rather steely mouth, the mere set of which suggested controversy of an uncompromising kind. Naturally as the Church had claimed Bastin, so medicine claimed Bickley.

Now as it happened the man who succeeded my father as vicar of Fulcombe was given a better living and went away shortly after I had purchased the place and with it the advowson. Just at this time also I received a letter written in the large, sprawling hand of Bastin from whom I had not heard for years. It went straight to the point, saying that he, Bastin, had seen in a Church paper that the last incumbent had resigned the living of Fulcombe which was in my gift. He would therefore be obliged if I would give it to him as the place he was at in Yorkshire did not suit his wife’s health.

===Chinese===

尚须一提的是，他在神学的意义上也有学问；他的别的怪癖里，还有两样：一是用缓慢、平板的声调，把他的见解装进长句子里说出来；二是不管驳论多么确凿有力，他一概全然不理会。

我的另一位朋友比克利，性情却全然是另一路人。同巴斯廷一样，他也博学，可他的倾向朝着另一个方向。巴斯廷那张无所不吞的喉咙能吞下骆驼——尤其是一头神学的骆驼；比克利却要滤出最小的蠓虫，尤其是神学的蠓虫。他是顶好顶正直的人，却什么都不信，除非尝得到、看得见、摸得着。比如，他确信人不过是兽类血统的一场偶然，仅此而已；确信我们所谓的灵魂或心智，不过是脑灰质某种活动的产物；确信一切看似无法解释的东西都有一桩完全世俗的解释，只要找得到；确信神迹从来不曾发生，也永远不会发生；确信一切宗教都是人类的希望与恐惧结出的果子，是人类软弱的最有力证明；确信尽管我们变化无穷，我们仍受制于大自然唯一的定律，是盲目、黑暗、残暴的偶然的牺牲品。

比克利就是这副样子：他那张聪明的、轮廓利落的脸总叫我想起浮雕玉石，还有沉思的额头；一双强健能干的手，一张近乎钢铁的嘴——单看那嘴形，就预示着不妥协的论战。教会既然收纳了巴斯廷，医学自然收纳了比克利。

说来凑巧，就在我买下富尔科姆、连同它的圣职推荐权之后不久，接替我父亲做教区牧师的那位得到了更好的职位，迁走了。恰在此时，我又收到一封信，是巴斯廷写来的，字体又大又潦草，我已多年没有他的音讯。信直奔主题，说教会的报纸上登了消息，上一位在任者辞去了富尔科姆的圣职，而圣职归我推荐；因此，若蒙把这个职位给他，他将不胜感激，因为他在约克郡的那个地方不合他太太的健康。

===Original===

Here I may state that afterwards I learned that what did not suit Mrs. Bastin was the organist, who was pretty. She was by nature a woman with a temperament so insanely jealous that actually she managed to be suspicious of Bastin, whom she had captured in an unguarded moment when he was thinking of something else and who would as soon have thought of even looking at any woman as he would of worshipping Baal. As a matter of fact it took him months to know one female from another. Except as possible providers of subscriptions and props of Mothers’ Meetings, women had no interest for him.

To return﻿—with that engaging honesty which I have mentioned﻿—Bastin’s letter went on to set out all his own disabilities, which, he added, would probably render him unsuitable for the place he desired to fill. He was a High Churchman, a fact which would certainly offend many; he had no claims to being a preacher although he was extraordinarily well acquainted with the writings of the Early Fathers. (What on earth had that to do with the question, I wondered.) On the other hand he had generally been considered a good visitor and was fond of walking (he meant to call on distant parishioners, but did not say so).

Then followed a page and a half on the evils of the existing system of the presentation to livings by private persons, ending with the suggestion that I had probably committed a sin in buying this particular advowson in order to increase my local authority, that is, if I had bought it, a point on which he was ignorant. Finally he informed me that as he had to christen a sick baby five miles away on a certain moor and it was too wet for him to ride his bicycle, he must stop. And he stopped.

There was, however, a P.S. to the letter, which ran as follows:

“Someone told me that you were dead a few years ago, and of course it may be another man of the same name who owns Fulcombe. If so, no doubt the Post Office will send back this letter.”

That was his only allusion to my humble self in all those diffuse pages. It was a long while since I had received an epistle which made me laugh so much, and of course I gave him the living by return of post, and even informed him that I would increase its stipend to a sum which I considered suitable to the position.

===Chinese===

这里不妨交代一句：后来我才知道，不合巴斯廷太太心意的，其实是那位的管风琴师——她长得漂亮。这位太太天性嫉妒成狂，居然连巴斯廷也要疑心——他是她趁他想着别的事情不留神时擒到手的，而他哪怕只想到看一眼随便哪个女人，也会像想到去拜巴力一样不可能。实际上，他得花上几个月工夫才能分辨两个女人谁是谁。除了当作可能捐些款项的来源和"母亲会"的支柱，女人引不起他任何兴趣。

书归正传——巴斯廷带着他那副讨人喜欢的诚实，在信里接着开列自己种种不能胜任之处，还补了一句，这些毛病多半使他不宜担任他所期望的职位。他是高教会派，这一点必定得罪许多人；他算不上布道家，虽然他对早期教父的著作熟得不得了。（我纳闷，这与本题到底有什么相干。）另一方面，人家一般公认他善于探访，而且喜欢走路（他是指打算拜访远处的教民，但没有说明）。

接着又写了一页半，论述由私人推荐圣职的现行制度的种种弊害，末了暗示我买下这份圣职推荐权来扩张自己在当地的势力，恐怕是犯了罪——也就是说，倘若我确实买了它；这一点他并不清楚。最后他通知我，他得去五英里外一处荒原给一个病婴施洗，天太潮，骑不成自行车，只好就此搁笔。他就搁了笔。

不过信末还有一行附言，如下：

"几年前有人告诉我说你死了，当然，拥有富尔科姆的兴许是另一个同名的人。若真如此，想必邮局会把这封信退回。"

在所有这些东拉西扯的纸页里，这是他对区区在下唯一的提及。好久没有人让我笑得这么厉害了，我自然回信把圣职给了他，甚至通知他，我要把年薪提到我认为与这职位相称的数目。

===Original===

About ten days later I received another letter from Bastin which, as a scrawl on the flap of the envelope informed me, he had carried for a week in his pocket and forgotten to post. Except by inference it returned no thanks for my intended benefits. What it did say, however, was that he thought it wrong of me to have settled a matter of such spiritual importance in so great a hurry, though he had observed that rich men were nearly always selfish where their time was concerned. Moreover, he considered that I ought first to have made inquiries as to his present character and attainments, etc., etc.

To this epistle I replied by telegraph to the effect that I should as soon think of making inquiries about the character of an archangel, or that of one of his High Church saints. This telegram, he told me afterwards, he considered unseemly and even ribald, especially as it had given great offence to the postmaster, who was one of the sidesmen in his church.

Thus it came about that I appointed the Rev. Basil Bastin to the living of Fulcombe, feeling sure that he would provide me with endless amusement and act as a moral tonic and discipline. Also I appreciated the man’s blunt candour. In due course he arrived, and I confess that after a few Sundays of experience I began to have doubts as to the wisdom of my choice, glad as I was to see him personally. His sermons at once bored me, and, when they did not send me to sleep, excited in me a desire for debate. How could he be so profoundly acquainted with mysteries before which the world had stood amazed for ages? Was there nothing too hot or too heavy in the spiritual way for him to dismiss in a few blundering and casual words, as he might any ordinary incident of everyday life, I wondered? Also his idea of High Church observances was not mine, or, I imagine, that of anybody else. But I will not attempt to set it out.

His peculiarities, however, were easy to excuse and entirely swallowed up by the innate goodness of his nature which soon made him beloved of everyone in the place, for although he thought that probably most things were sins, I never knew him to discover a sin which he considered to be beyond the reach of forgiveness. Bastin was indeed a most charitable man and in his way wide-minded.

===Chinese===

十来天后我又收到巴斯廷一封信，信封盖口的潦草字迹告诉我，这信在他口袋里装了一个星期，忘了寄。除了可以意会，信中对我要施的好处没有半句谢意。它倒是说了：这样一件属灵大事，我办得如此仓促，他认为不对；不过他也看出，富人一沾到自己的时间，几乎无不自私。况且，他认为我理应先打听一下他如今的品行与造诣等等、等等。

对这封书信，我拍电报作答，大意是：我宁可去打听一位大天使的品行，或者他哪位高教会派圣徒的品行。这封电报，他后来告诉我，他认为失礼，甚至近于轻薄，尤其因为它大大得罪了邮政局长——他教堂里的一名护堂员。

就这样，我委任巴兹尔·巴斯廷牧师做了富尔科姆的教区牧师，深信他会供给我无穷的笑料，还兼作一副道德补剂和一门训导功课。我也赏识这人的直率坦诚。到时候他来了，我承认，经历几个礼拜日之后，我便开始怀疑这选择是否明智，虽然见到他本人我很高兴。他的布道立刻叫我厌烦，而且，当它们还没把我催入梦乡时，便在我心里激起争辩的欲望。我纳闷，他怎么能在几句蹩脚、随口的闲话里，打发掉让世界惊愕了千秋万代的奥秘——在属灵的事情上，难道就没有一样东西太烫或太重，是他不能像打发一桩日常琐事那样打发的？再有，他对高教会派仪轨的见解与我的不同，或者，与任何别人的都不同。不过我不想一一细数。

然而，他的种种怪癖很容易原谅，完全被他天性里的纯善吞没；这纯善很快叫他在当地人人爱戴，因为虽然他认定多半事情都是罪，我从没见他发现过一桩他认为赦免不了的罪。巴斯廷的确是个极慈善的人，而且就他的方式而言，心胸宽大。

===Original===

The person whom I could not tolerate, however, was his wife, who, to my fancy, more resembled a vessel, a very unattractive vessel, full of vinegar than a woman. Her name was Sarah and she was small, plain, flat, sandy-haired and odious, quite obsessed, moreover, with her jealousies of the Rev. Basil, at whom it pleased her to suppose that every woman in the countryside under fifty was throwing herself.

Here I will confess that to the best of my ability I took care that they did in outward seeming, that is, whenever she was present, instructing them to sit aside with him in darkened corners, to present him with flowers, and so forth. Several of them easily fell into the humour of the thing, and I have seen him depart from a dinner-party followed by that glowering Sarah, with a handful of rosebuds and violets, to say nothing of the traditional offerings of slippers, embroidered markers and the like. Well, it was my only way of coming even with her, which I think she knew, for she hated me poisonously.

So much for Basil Bastin. Now for Bickley. Him I had met on several occasions since our college days, and after I was settled at the Priory from time to time I asked him to stay with me. At length he came, and I found out that he was not at all comfortable in his London practice which was of a nature uncongenial to him; further, that he did not get on with his partners. Then, after reflection, I made a suggestion to him. I pointed out that, owing to its popularity amongst seaside visitors, the neighbourhood of Fulcombe was a rising one, and that although there were doctors in it, there was no really first-class surgeon for miles.

Now Bickley was a first-class surgeon, having held very high hospital appointments, and indeed still holding them. Why, I asked, should he not come and set up here on his own? I would appoint him doctor to the estate and also give him charge of a cottage hospital which I was endowing, with liberty to build and arrange it as he liked. Further, as I considered that it would be of great advantage to me to have a man of real ability within reach, I would guarantee for three years whatever income he was earning in London.

===Chinese===

然而，我容忍不了的那个人，是他的太太。在我眼里，她与其说像个女人，不如说像个器皿——一只盛满醋的、很不讨人喜欢的器皿。她名叫萨拉，个子小，相貌平平，扁平，头发灰黄，可憎得很；而且满脑子都是她对巴兹尔牧师的嫉妒，自以为乡下五十岁以下的女人个个都乐意往他身上扑。

这里我要坦白：我尽力让她们至少在表面上像她猜想的那样——凡她在场的时候——我吩咐她们坐到昏暗角落里挨着他，送他鲜花，如此等等。好几位轻易上了这个趣味的道，我见过他在宴会后告辞，身后跟着怒目而视的萨拉，手里攥着一把蔷薇花蕾和紫罗兰，还不算拖鞋、绣花书签之类传统供品。唉，这是我同她扯平的唯一办法，我想她也明白，因为她恨我入骨。

巴斯廷就说到这里。再说比克利。大学时代之后，我又见过他几面；我定居修道院旧宅之后，时不时请他来住。末了他来了，我发现他在伦敦的行医很不对他的脾胃，那种行医的性质同他格格不入；再说，他同合伙人相处不好。于是我思索之后向他提了个建议。我指出，富尔科姆一带在海滨游客中很受欢迎，是个正在兴起的地区；虽有几名医生，方圆多少英里却没有一位真正第一流的外科医生。

而比克利正是第一流的外科医生，他在大医院里任过极高的职位，实际当时还任着。我问，他为什么不来这里自立门户？我可以聘他做庄园的医生，还让他主持一家我出资兴办的乡村医院，院舍如何建造布置，随他的意思。再者，我认为身边有一位真正有才干的人，对我大有益处，我可以担保三年，他在伦敦挣多少，这里照付多少。

===Original===

He thanked me warmly and in the end acted on the idea, with startling results so far as his prospects were concerned. Very soon his really remarkable skill became known and he was earning more money than as an unmarried man he could possibly want. Indeed, scarcely a big operation took place at any town within twenty miles, and even much farther away, at which he was not called in to assist.

Needless to say his advent was a great boon to me, for as he lived in a house I let him quite near by, whenever he had a spare evening he would drop in to dinner, and from our absolutely opposite standpoints we discussed all things human and divine. Thus I was enabled to sharpen my wits upon the hard steel of his clear intellect which was yet, in a sense, so limited.

I must add that I never converted him to my way of thinking and he never converted me to his, any more than he converted Bastin, for whom, queerly enough, he had a liking. They pounded away at each other, Bickley frequently getting the best of it in the argument, and when at last Bastin rose to go, he generally made the same remark. It was:

“It really is sad, my dear Bickley, to find a man of your intellect so utterly wrongheaded and misguided. I have convicted you of error at least half a dozen times, and not to confess it is mere pigheadedness. Good night. I am sure that Sarah will be sitting up for me.”

“Silly old idiot!” Bickley would say, shaking his fist after him. “The only way to get him to see the truth would be to saw his head open and pour it in.”

Then we would both laugh.

Such were my two most intimate friends, although I admit it was rather like the equator cultivating close relationships with the north and south poles. Certainly Bastin was as far from Bickley as those points of the earth are apart, while I, as it were, sat equally distant between the two. However, we were all very happy together, since in certain characters, there are few things that bind men more closely than profound differences of opinion.

---

===Chinese===

他热诚地道了谢，末了依计行事，就他的前程而言，结果惊人。他那份确实出众的本领很快传开，他挣的钱，对一个单身汉来说绰绰有余。实际上，方圆二十英里内任何城镇，甚至远得多的地方，凡有大手术，几乎没有不请他去协助的。

不用说，他的到来对我是一大恩惠。他住的房子是我租给他的，离得很近；每逢晚上有空，他便过来吃饭，我们从截然相反的立场出发，把人间与神界的一切都争论一番。这样，我便得以在他清明智力的硬钢上磨砺我的锋刃——那智力虽然清明，某种意义上却颇有局限。

必须补一句：我从不曾把他改造成我的想法，他也从不曾把我改造成他的，正如他从不曾改造巴斯廷——说来也怪，他却挺喜欢巴斯廷。两人你来我往地锤打，比克利在论战里常占上风；末了巴斯廷起身告辞，他总要说同一句话。那话是：

"实在可叹，亲爱的比克利，你这样有智力的人，竟这样执迷不悟、误入歧途。我至少已经六次指明你的错误，你不肯承认，纯粹是顽固不化。晚安。萨拉一定在等我呢。"

"老蠢货！"比克利朝着他的背影挥着拳头说。"要叫这家伙看见真理，只有把他的脑壳锯开，把真理灌进去。"

于是我们俩都笑了。

这便是我最亲密的两位朋友，虽然我承认，这有点像赤道同南北两极搞亲密关系。巴斯廷离比克利，确实同地球上那两点一样远；而我，可以说，不偏不倚地坐在两者中间。不过，我们在一起很快活，因为在某些性格里，再没有什么比意见的深刻分歧更能把人拴在一起的了。

---

===Original===

Now I must turn to my more personal affairs. After all, it is impossible for a man to satisfy his soul, if he has anything of the sort about him which in the remotest degree answers to that description, with the husks of wealth, luxury and indolence, supplemented by occasional theological and other arguments between his friends. Becoming profoundly convinced of this truth, I searched round for something to do and, like Noah’s dove on the waste of waters, found nothing. Then I asked Bickley and Bastin for their opinions as to my best future course. Bickley proved a barren draw. He rubbed his nose and feebly suggested that I might go in for “research work,” which, of course, only represented his own ambitions. I asked him indignantly how I could do such a thing without any scientific qualifications whatever. He admitted the difficulty, but replied that I might endow others who had the qualifications.

“In short, become a milch cow for sucking scientists,” I replied, and broke off the conversation.

Bastin’s idea was, first, that I should teach in a Sunday School; secondly, that if this career did not satisfy all my aspirations, I might be ordained and become a missionary.

On my rejection of this brilliant advice, he remarked that the only other thing he could think of was that I should get married and have a large family, which might possibly advantage the nation and ultimately enrich the Kingdom of Heaven, though of such things no one could be quite sure. At any rate, he was certain that at present I was in practice neglecting my duty, whatever it might be, and in fact one of those cumberers of the earth who, he observed in the newspaper he took in and read when he had time, were “very happily named﻿—the idle rich.”

“Which reminds me,” he added, “that the clothing-club finances are in a perfectly scandalous condition; in fact, it is £25 in debt, an amount that as the squire of the parish I consider it incumbent on you to make good, not as a charity but as an obligation.”

“Look here, my friend,” I said, ignoring all the rest, “will you answer me a plain question? Have you found marriage such a success that you consider it your duty to recommend it to others? And if you have, why have *you* not got the large family of which you speak?”

===Chinese===

现在我必须转向更切身的私事。说到底，一个身上若还有哪怕一丁点儿配得上"灵魂"二字的东西的人，是不可能靠财富、奢侈和安逸这些空壳，再加上朋友之间偶尔的神学与别样论战，来喂饱他的灵魂的。深切地相信了这个道理之后，我便四下寻找可做的事，却像挪亚的鸽子在浩渺的水面上一样，一无所获。于是我向比克利和巴斯廷请教，问我将来走哪条路最好。比克利那边颗粒无收。他搓着鼻子，有气无力地建议我去做"研究工作"，这自然只是他自己的抱负的投影。我愤然问他，我毫无一点科学资历，怎么做这样的事。他承认了困难，却答道，我可以出钱资助有资历的人。

"一句话，叫我去给嗷嗷待哺的科学家们当奶牛，"我答道，就此打住了谈话。

巴斯廷的主义是：第一，我该去主日学校教书；第二，倘若这个行当不能满足我全部的抱负，我可以受按立，做传教士。

我拒绝了这番高明建议，他便说，他所能想到的唯一另外一条路，是我结婚，生一大群孩子，这或许于国家有利，最终还能充实天国的库藏，虽然这类事谁也说不准。不管怎样，他肯定我眼下实际上在怠工，怠的是什么工且不论，而且属于那种"地上的累赘"——他翻开他订的那份报纸、抽空读到的时候注意到，报上给这类人起了个"十分贴切的诨名——游手好闲的有钱人"。

"这倒提醒了我，"他补了一句，"制衣会的账目糟得一塌糊涂；实际上负债二十五镑。作为本教区的乡绅，我认为你有责任补上这个亏空——不是当作施舍，是当作义务。"

"听着，我的朋友，"我说，把其余的话一概置之不理，"你肯不肯老老实实回答我一个问题？你发现婚姻如此成功，以至于认定有责任向别人推荐吗？若真如此，为什么*你*没有你说的那一大群孩子？"

===Original===

“Of course not,” he replied with his usual frankness. “Indeed, it is in many ways so disagreeable that I am convinced it must be right and for the good of all concerned. As regards the family I am sure I do not know, but Sarah never liked babies, which perhaps has something to do with it.”

Then he sighed, adding, “You see, Arbuthnot, we have to take things as we find them in this world and hope for a better.”

“Which is just what I am trying to do, you unilluminating old donkey!” I exclaimed, and left him there shaking his head over matters in general, but I think principally over Sarah.

By the way, I think that the villagers recognised this good lady’s vinegary nature. At least, they used to call her “Sour Sal.”

===Chinese===

"当然没有，"他以一贯的坦率回答。"说实话，婚姻在许多方面这样叫人讨厌，我倒确信它一定是正当的，对一切相关的人都有好处。至于孩子，我说不上来，不过萨拉从来不喜欢婴孩，也许有点关系。"

说完他叹了口气，又道："你瞧，阿巴思诺特，在这个世界上，我们只好有什么认什么，再指望一个更好的。"

"这恰恰是我在尽力做的事呀，你这只不开窍的老驴！"我叫道，把他撂在那里，让他在种种事情上摇头——不过我想，摇的主要是萨拉。

顺带说一句，我想村里人认得透这位太太的酸劲儿。至少，他们惯常叫她"酸萨拉"。

## III / 第三章
===Original===

Natalie

Now what Bastin had said about marriage stuck in my mind as his blundering remarks had a way of doing, perhaps because of the grain of honest truth with which they were often permeated. Probably in my position it was more or less my duty to marry. But here came the rub; I had never experienced any leanings that way. I was as much a man as others, more so than many are, perhaps, and I liked women, but at the same time they repelled me.

My old fastidiousness came in; to my taste there was always something wrong about them. While they attracted one part of my nature they revolted another part, and on the whole I preferred to do without their intimate society, rather than work violence to this second and higher part of me. Moreover, quite at the beginning of my career I had concluded from observation that a man gets on better in life alone, rather than with another to drag at his side, or by whom perhaps he must be dragged. Still true marriage, such as most men and some women have dreamed of in their youth, had always been one of my ideals; indeed it was on and around this vision that I wrote that first book of mine which was so successful. Since I knew this to be unattainable in our imperfect conditions, however, notwithstanding Bastin’s strictures, again I dismissed the whole matter from my mind as a vain imagination.

As an alternative I reflected upon a parliamentary career which I was not too old to begin, and even toyed with one or two opportunities that offered themselves, as these do to men of wealth and advanced views. They never came to anything, for in the end I decided that Party politics were so hateful and so dishonest, that I could not bring myself to put my neck beneath their yoke. I was sure that if I tried to do so, I should fail more completely than I had done at the Bar and in Literature. Here, too, I am quite certain that I was right.

The upshot of it all was that I sought refuge in that last expedient of weary Englishmen, travel, not as a globetrotter, but leisurely and with an inquiring mind, learning much but again finding, like the ancient writer whom I have quoted already, that there is no new thing under the sun; that with certain variations it is the same thing over and over again.

===Chinese===

娜塔莉

巴斯廷关于婚姻的那番话萦绕在我心头，他那些莽撞的话向来有此功效，也许因为其中常常渗透着一粒老实的真理。就我的处境而言，成家多半或多或少是我的本分。可难处也在这里：我从未体验过那方面的倾向。我同别人一样是个男人，也许比许多人更其是个男人，我喜欢女人，可与此同时她们又令我却步。

我那老毛病——苛求——又来作祟；依我看，她们身上总有不对劲的地方。她们吸引我天性的一部分，又触犯另一部分，两相权衡，我宁可不要她们的亲密陪伴，也不愿对我这更高一层的部分施暴。况且，在我事业的开头，我便从观察中得出结论：人单身度日，在人生路上走得更顺，好过身旁拖着一个人，或被那个人拖着。然而真正的婚姻——多数男人和一些女人年轻时梦想过的那种——始终是我的理想之一；我那本大获成功的处女作，写的就是这一愿景，或萦绕这一愿景的东西。可是既然我知道，在我们这不完善的境况里它无法企及，那么不管巴斯廷如何数落，我再一次把这桩事整个抛出脑海，当作一枕黄粱。

作为替代，我想过进议会从政——以我的年纪起步还不算太迟——甚至摆弄过一两个送上门来的机会，有钱有见地的人向来不缺这种机会。它们从没成过事，因为我最终认定党派政治如此可憎、如此不诚实，我无法让自己把脖子伸进它的轭下。我确信，若真去尝试，我会败得比在律师界和文学界更彻底。这一点，我至今同样确信自己没有想错。

这一切的归结是：我逃进了倦怠的英国人的最后一条退路——旅行。不是走马观花式的环球旅游，而是从容不迫、带着探究之心的漫游。我学到了许多，却再一次像前面引过的那位古代作者一样发现，日光之下并无新事；除去若干变奏，一切都是同一出戏，一遍又一遍地重演。

===Original===

No, I will make an exception, the East did interest me enormously. There it was, at Benares, that I came into touch with certain thinkers who opened my eyes to a great deal. They released some hidden spring in my nature which hitherto had always been striving to break through the crust of our conventions and inherited ideas. I know now that what I was seeking was nothing less than the Infinite; that I had “immortal longings in me.” I listened to all their solemn talk of epochs and years measureless to man, and reflected with a thrill that after all man might have his part in every one of them. Yes, that bird of passage as he seemed to be, flying out of darkness into darkness, still he might have spread his wings in the light of other suns millions upon millions of years ago, and might still spread them, grown radiant and glorious, millions upon millions of years hence in a time unborn.

If only I could know the truth. Was Life (according to Bickley) merely a short activity bounded by nothingness before and behind; or (according to Bastin) a conventional golden-harped and haloed immortality, a word of which he did not in the least understand the meaning?

Or was it something quite different from either of these, something vast and splendid beyond the reach of vision, something God-sent, beginning and ending in the Eternal Absolute and at last partaking of His attributes and nature and from aeon to aeon shot through with His light? And how was the truth to be learned? I asked my Eastern friends, and they talked vaguely of long ascetic preparation, of years upon years of learning, from whom I could not quite discover. I was sure it could not be from them, because clearly they did not know; they only passed on what they had heard elsewhere, when or how they either could not or would not explain. So at length I gave it up, having satisfied myself that all this was but an effort of Oriental imagination called into life by the sweet influences of the Eastern stars.

===Chinese===

不，我要破个例：东方叫我极感兴趣。正是在贝拿勒斯，我接触到某些思想家，他们为我打开了眼界。他们松开了我天性里一根隐秘的弹簧，那弹簧一直在力图冲破我们的成规与因袭的硬壳。我如今知道，我寻求的正是"无限"，别无其他；我知道我"胸怀着不朽的渴望"。我听他们庄重地谈论人类的度量测不尽的纪元与年代，心头一震，想到人毕竟可能在每一个纪元里都占有一份。是啊，这个看似匆匆过客的生命，从黑暗飞向黑暗，却也许在千百万年之前，已在别的太阳的光里展开过翅膀；也许在千百万年之后，在尚未降生的时代里，还要展开那双翅膀，披上光辉与荣耀。

只要我能知道真相便好。生命（照比克利的说法）是不是仅仅一场短暂的活动，前后都以虚无为界？还是（照巴斯廷的说法）一种程式化的、竖着金色竖琴、顶着光环的不朽——一个他压根儿不懂其含义的词？

或者，它全然不同于这两者，是某种辽阔辉煌、目力不及的东西，是某种上帝所赐、在永恒的绝对中开始并终结、最终分有祂的属性与本质、从万古到万古被祂的光穿透的东西？而这真理要到哪里去学？我问我的东方朋友们，他们含糊地谈到长期的苦修，谈到经年累月的学习——跟谁学，我却怎么也弄不清。我确信不会是跟他们学，因为显然他们自己并不知道；他们只是转述从别处听来的东西，至于是何时、如何听来的，他们要么说不出，要么不肯说。于是末了我放弃了，心里认定，这一切不过是东方想象力的产物，由东方星辰温柔的感应唤醒罢了。

===Original===

I gave it up and went away, thinking that I should forget. But I did not forget. I was quick with a new hope, or at any rate with a new aspiration, and that secret child of holy desire grew and grew within my soul, till at length it flashed upon me that this soul of mine was itself the hidden Master from which I must learn my lesson. No wonder that those Eastern friends could not give his name, seeing that whatever they really knew, as distinguished from what they had heard, and it was little enough, each of them had learned from the teaching of his own soul.

Thus, then, I too became a dreamer with only one longing, the longing for wisdom, for that spirit touch which should open my eyes and enable me to see.

Yet now it happened strangely enough that when I seemed within myself to have little further interest in the things of the world, and least of all in women, I, who had taken another guest to dwell with me, those things of the world came back to me and in the shape of Woman the Inevitable. Probably it was so decreed since is it not written that no man can live to himself alone, or lose himself in watching and nurturing the growth of his own soul?

---

It happened thus. I went to Rome on my way home from India, and stayed there a while. On the day after my arrival I wrote my name in the book of our Minister to Italy at that time, Sir Alfred Upton, not because I wished him to ask me to dinner, but for the reason that I had heard of him as a man of archeological tastes and thought that he might enable me to see things which otherwise I should not see.

As it chanced he knew about me through some of my Devonshire neighbours who were friends of his, and did ask me to dinner on the following night. I accepted and found myself one of a considerable party, some of them distinguished English people who wore Orders, as is customary when one dines with the representative of our Sovereign. Seeing these, and this shows that in the best of us vanity is only latent, for the first time in my life I was sorry that I had none and was only plain Mr. Arbuthnot who, as Sir Alfred explained to me politely, must go in to dinner last, because all the rest had titles, and without even a lady as there was not one to spare.

===Chinese===

我放弃了，走了，心想我会忘掉。可我没有忘掉。我怀着一种新的希望，至少是一种新的渴望，这个圣洁愿望的秘密孩子在灵魂里越长越大，直到末了，一道闪光击中我：原来我这灵魂本身，就是那位隐秘的宗师，我该向它学我的功课。难怪那些东方朋友说不出他的名字——因为他们真正知道的（有别于听来的，而那实在少得可怜），各人都学自各自灵魂的教导。

就这样，我也成了一个做梦的人，只怀着一个渴望：渴望智慧，渴望那一触开启我眼睛、使我得以看见的灵的触摸。

然而说来奇怪，正当我似乎对世间之事——尤其是女人——再无多少兴趣，正当我已接了另一位房客与我同住的时候，世间之事却回到我身上，而且是以那个无可回避的女人——"必然之女"——的模样。这多半是天意，既然书上不是写着：没有人能只为自己活着，也没有人能在看顾和滋养自己灵魂的生长中忘掉世界？

---

事情是这样发生的。我从印度返国的途中到了罗马，在那里盘桓了一阵。到达的第二天，我把名字写进了我国驻意大利公使阿尔弗雷德·厄普顿爵士（Sir Alfred Upton）的来客簿——不是想让他请我吃饭，而是听说此人雅好考古，想着他或许能让我看到一些别处看不到的东西。

碰巧，他通过我在德文郡的几位邻人——他们也是他的朋友——听说过我，果然在第二天晚上请了我。我去了，发现自己置身于一场不小的宴会，其中有几位佩戴着勋章的英国名流，在君主代表的席上用膳，佩章是惯例。见到这些勋章——这也说明连最好的人身上，虚荣也不过是蛰伏着——我有生以来第一次懊恼自己竟一个也没有，只是个光杆的阿巴思诺特先生；而且，正如阿尔弗雷德爵士客气地向我解释的，我得最后一个进餐，因为其余的人全有头衔；连一位挽臂的女士也没有，因为没有富余。

===Original===

Nor was my lot bettered when I got there, as I found myself seated between an Italian countess and a Russian prince, neither of whom could talk English, while, alas, I knew no foreign language, not even French in which they addressed me, seeming surprised that I did not understand them. I was humiliated at my own ignorance, although in fact I was not ignorant, only my education had been classical. Indeed I was a good classic and had kept up my knowledge more or less, especially since I became an idle man. In my confusion it occurred to me that the Italian countess might know Latin from which her own language was derived, and addressed her in that tongue. She stared, and Sir Alfred, who was not far off and overheard me (he also knew Latin), burst into laughter and proceeded to explain the joke in a loud voice, first in French and then in English, to the assembled company, who all became infected with merriment and also stared at me as a curiosity.

Then it was that for the first time I saw Natalie, for owing to a mistake of my driver I had arrived rather late and had not been introduced to her. As her father’s only daughter, her mother being dead, she was seated at the end of the table behind a fan-like arrangement of white Madonna lilies, and she had bent forward and, like the others, was looking at me, but in such a fashion that her head from that distance seemed as though it were surrounded and crowned with lilies. Indeed the greatest art could not have produced a more beautiful effect which was, however, really one of naked accident.

An angel looking down upon earth through the lilies of Heaven﻿—that was the rather absurd thought which flashed into my mind. I did not quite realise her face at first except that it seemed to be both dark and fair; as a fact her waving hair which grew rather low upon her forehead, was dark, and her large, soft eyes were grey. I did not know, and to this moment I do not know if she was really beautiful, but certainly the light that shone through those eyes of hers and seemed to be reflected upon her delicate features, was beauty itself. It was like that glowing through a thin vase of the purest alabaster within which a lamp is placed, and I felt this effect to arise from no chance, like that of the lily-setting, but, as it were, from the lamp of the spirit within.

===Chinese===

到了席上，我的处境也不见改善：我被安排在一位意大利伯爵夫人和一位俄国亲王中间，两人都不会讲英语；而唉，我不通任何外国语，连他们用来同我讲话的法语也不懂，他们似乎还很奇怪我竟听不明白。我为自己的无知感到羞辱，虽然实际上我并非无知，只是我的教养是古典的。我确实是个不错的古典学者，也或多或少维持着所学，尤其自从成了闲人之后。慌乱之中，我想到那位意大利伯爵夫人或许懂拉丁文——她的母语便出自拉丁文——便用那种语言同她攀谈。她瞪住了我；近旁的阿尔弗雷德爵士听见了（他也懂拉丁文），放声大笑，先用法语、再用英语，高声向满座宾朋解释这个笑话，于是人人传染了笑意，又都把我当作一件奇物瞪着看。

就在这时，我第一次看见了娜塔莉，因为司机弄错了路，我到得颇迟，没有人给我介绍过她。作为她父亲唯一的女儿——母亲已去世——她坐在长桌末端，身后是一架扇形的白百合；她探身向前，同别人一样望着我，但从那个距离看去，她的头仿佛被百合簇拥着、戴着一顶百合的冠冕。的确，最高的艺术也造不出比这更美的效果，而这效果实际上纯属赤裸裸的偶然。

一位天使透过天堂的百合俯瞰尘世——一闪念间，这个相当荒唐的念头掠过我的心头。起初我看不真切她的脸，只觉得她又深又亮；实际上，她起伏的头发长势颇低，覆着额头，是深色的，她那双大而温柔的眼睛是灰色的。我不知道——直到此刻我也不知道——她是否真算得上美，但透过那双眼睛照耀出来、又仿佛映在她娇嫩眉目上的那道光，本身就是美。它像灯光透过一只薄薄的最纯的雪花石膏瓶放射出来的光，而我感到这效果绝非百合陈设那类偶然，可以说，是发自内里那盏灵的灯。

===Original===

Our eyes met, and I suppose that she saw the wonder and admiration in mine. At any rate her amused smile faded, leaving the face rather serious, though still sweetly serious, and a tinge of colour crept over it as the first hue of dawn creeps into a pearly sky. Then she withdrew herself behind the screen of lilies and for the rest of that dinner which I thought was never coming to an end, practically I saw her no more. Only I noted as she passed out that although not tall, she was rounded and graceful in shape and that her hands were peculiarly delicate.

Afterwards in the drawing-room her father, with whom I had talked at the table, introduced me to her, saying:

“My daughter is the real archaeologist, Mr. Arbuthnot, and I think if you ask her, she may be able to help you.”

Then he bustled away to speak to some of his important guests, from whom I think he was seeking political information.

“My father exaggerates,” she said in a soft and very sympathetic voice, “but perhaps”﻿—and she motioned me to a seat at her side.

Then we talked of the places and things that I more particularly desired to see and, well, the end of it was that I went back to my hotel in love with Natalie; and as she afterwards confessed, she went to bed in love with me.

It was a curious business, more like meeting a very old friend from whom one had been separated by circumstances for a score of years or so than anything else. We were, so to speak, intimate from the first; we knew all about each other, although here and there was something new, something different which we could not remember, lines of thought, veins of memory which we did not possess in common. On one point I am absolutely clear: it was not solely the everyday and ancient appeal of woman to man and man to woman which drew us together, though doubtless this had its part in our attachment as under our human conditions it must do, seeing that it is Nature’s bait to ensure the continuance of the race. It was something more, something quite beyond that elementary impulse.

===Chinese===

我们的目光相遇了，我想她看见了我眼中的惊异与叹赏。无论如何，她那含笑的意趣褪去了，脸上变得相当严肃，虽然仍是甜甜的严肃，并且，一抹红晕悄悄爬上双颊，像黎明的第一线色泽爬进珍珠色的天空。随后她退到百合的屏障后面，那一顿我简直以为永远不会散的晚餐，余下的时间我再没怎么见到她。只在她退席经过时，我注意到她虽不高，身形却丰满窈窕，一双手出奇地纤巧。

后来在客厅里，她父亲——席间我同他谈过话——把我介绍给她，说道：

"我女儿才是真正的考古学家，阿巴思诺特先生；我想，您若去问她，她也许能帮上您的忙。"

说完他便匆匆走开，去招呼他的几位要紧客人，我想他是在向他们打探政治消息。

"我父亲言过其实了，"她用柔和而极富同情的嗓音说，"不过也许——"她示意我在她身旁坐下。

于是我们谈起我尤其想看的地方和东西。嗯，结果是我回到旅馆时，已经爱上了娜塔莉；而她后来也承认，她上床时已经爱上了我。

这事透着蹊跷，与其说像别的什么，不如说像重逢一位因际遇暌隔了二十年上下的老友。可以说，我们自一开始便亲熟；我们彼此无所不知，虽然这里或那里总有点新东西，有点不同、记不起来的东西，是彼此不曾共有的思路，是不曾共享的记忆的脉络。在一点上我全然明白：把我们拉到一起的，并非仅仅是那种日常而古老的、女对男男对女的吸引，虽然毫无疑问，这份吸引在我们的眷爱中也有一份，按我们人的境况它必然如此，因为那是大自然确保种族延续的诱饵。那是某种更多的东西，某种远远超出这最初冲动的东西。

===Original===

At any rate we loved, and one evening in the shelter of the solemn walls of the great Coliseum at Rome, which at that hour were shut to all except ourselves, we confessed our love. I really think we must have chosen the spot by tacit but mutual consent because we felt it to be fitting. It was so old, so impregnated with every human experience, from the direst crime of the tyrant who thought himself a god, to the sublimest sacrifice of the martyr who already was half a god; with every vice and virtue also which lies between these extremes, that it seemed to be the most fitting altar whereon to offer our hearts and all that caused them to beat, each to the other.

So Natalie and I were betrothed within a month of our first meeting. Within three we were married, for what was there to prevent or delay? Naturally Sir Alfred was delighted, seeing that he possessed but small private resources and I was able to make ample provision for his daughter who had hitherto shown herself somewhat difficult in this business of matrimony and now was bordering on her twenty-seventh year. Everybody was delighted, everything went smoothly as a sledge sliding down a slope of frozen snow and the mists of time hid whatever might be at the end of that slope. Probably a plain; at the worst the upward rise of ordinary life.

That is what we thought, if we thought at all. Certainly we never dreamed of a precipice. Why should we, who were young, by comparison, quite healthy and very rich? Who thinks of precipices under such circumstances, when disaster seems to be eliminated and death is yet a long way off?

And yet we ought to have done so, because we should have known that smooth surfaces without impediment to the runners often end in something of the kind.

I am bound to say that when we returned home to Fulcombe, where of course we met with a great reception, including the ringing (out of tune) of the new peal of bells that I had given to the church, Bastin made haste to point this out.

===Chinese===

总之我们相爱了。一天晚上，在罗马大斗兽场庄严围墙的荫蔽里——那时辰墙内只许我们两个——我们坦白了爱情。我真的以为，我们是凭着无言却彼此心照的默契选中那个地方的，因为我们觉得它配得上。它是那样古老，浸透了人类的一切经历：从那个自以为神、犯下滔天大罪的暴君，到那半个身子已在神里、献上最崇高牺牲的殉道者；还有介于两极之间的一切恶与善——它似乎是最相宜的祭坛，好在上面献上我们的心，和那使两颗心互为对方跳动的一切。

于是，娜塔莉同我在初遇之后不满一个月订了婚。不满三个月，我们结了婚，因为有什么可阻止、可延宕的呢？阿尔弗雷德爵士自然喜出望外，眼见他私蓄无多，而我竟能为他的女儿备下丰厚的安排——她此前在这桩婚姻的买卖上颇为挑剔，如今已快满二十七岁。人人都欢喜，事事顺遂，像雪橇滑下一道冻雪的长坡；时光的雾霭遮住了坡底可能有什么。多半是一片平原；最坏，也不过是寻常生活的上坡。

我们就是这样想的，如果说我们想过的话。我们当然梦不到悬崖。为什么要梦到呢？我们相对年轻、十分康健、又非常富有。在这种时候——灾难似乎已被排除，死亡还远在天边——谁会想到悬崖呢？

然而我们本该想到，因为我们本该知道：毫无障碍的光滑滑道，常常正以这类东西收尾。

我必须说，当我们回到富尔科姆——自然少不了一场盛大的迎接，还包括我赠给教堂的那套新钟（走了调地）齐鸣——巴斯廷赶忙把这一点指了出来。

===Original===

“Your wife seems a very nice and beautiful lady, Arbuthnot,” he reflected aloud after dinner, when Mrs. Bastin, glowering as usual, though what at I do not know, had been escorted from the room by Natalie, “and really, when I come to think of it, you are an unusually fortunate person. You possess a great deal of money, much more than you have any right to; which you seem to have done very little to earn and do not spend quite as I should like you to do, and this nice property, that ought to be owned by a great number of people, as, according to the views you express, I should have thought you would acknowledge, and everything else that a man can want. It is very strange that you should be so favoured and not because of any particular merits of your own which one can see. However, I have no doubt it will all come even in the end and you will get your share of troubles, like others. Perhaps Mrs. Arbuthnot will have no children as there is so much for them to take. Or perhaps you will lose all your money and have to work for your living, which might be good for you. Or,” he added, still thinking aloud after his fashion, “perhaps she will die young﻿—she has that kind of face, although, of course, I hope she won’t,” he added, waking up.

I do not know why, but his wandering words struck me cold; the proverbial funeral bell at the marriage feast was nothing to them. I suppose it was because in a flash of intuition I knew that they would come true and that he was an appointed Cassandra. Perhaps this uncanny knowledge overcame my natural indignation at such super-gaucherie of which no one but Bastin could have been capable, and even prevented me from replying at all, so that I merely sat still and looked at him.

But Bickley did reply with some vigour.

“Forgive me for saying so, Bastin,” he said, bristling all over as it were, “but your remarks, which may or may not be in accordance with the principles of your religion, seem to me to be in singularly bad taste. They would have turned the stomachs of a gathering of early Christians, who appear to have been the worst mannered people in the world, and at any decent heathen feast your neck would have been wrung as that of a bird of ill omen.”

“Why?” asked Bastin blankly. “I only said what I thought to be the truth. The truth is better than what you call good taste.”

===Chinese===

"你的太太似乎是位很可爱、很漂亮的夫人，阿巴思诺特，"饭后他自言自语地沉吟道——那时娜塔莉已把巴斯廷太太领出餐厅，她照例怒目而视，虽然冲着什么我不知道——"实在说，仔细一想，你是个出奇走运的人。你拥有大量钱财，多得你没有权利拥有的那么多；你好像没费什么事就挣到了它，花起来又不合我的心意；你还拥有这份产业——照你自己发表的高见，我本想你该承认，它应当归许许多多人共有——还拥有一个男人想要的一切。你这样受眷顾实在奇怪，而且并不是因为你自己有什么看得见的特殊德行。不过，我毫不怀疑，到头来一切都会扯平，你会像别人一样分到你那份苦难。也许阿巴思诺特太太不会有孩子，因为留给他们拿的东西太多了。或者，你会把钱财赔光，不得不做工糊口，那对你也许有好处。或者，"他照他的老习惯继续想出声来，"也许她会早死——她长着那样一张脸；当然，我希望她不会。"他惊醒过来，补上这一句。

不知为什么，他那游移不定的话叫我遍体生寒；婚宴上那口人尽皆知的丧钟，同它们相比也算不了什么。我想这是因为灵光一闪，我知道这些话会应验，知道他是一位奉了差遣的卡珊德拉。也许正是这神秘的知识压住了我对这等超级拙劣——除了巴斯廷没人做得出——理应起的义愤，甚至使我一言不答，只管坐着望着他。

可是比克利答了，答得颇有力。

"恕我直言，巴斯廷，"他说，浑身像刺猬似的竖了起来，"你这番话——合不合你们宗教的教义我不敢说——依我看，趣味坏得出奇。早年的基督徒听了也要翻胃，虽然他们似乎是世上最没规矩的一群人；而在任何一场像样的异教宴席上，你的脖子早被人拧断了，像拧一只报丧的鸟。"

"为什么？"巴斯廷茫然地问。"我不过说了我以为是真话的东西。真理总比你们所谓的好趣味强。"

===Original===

“Then I will say what I think also to be the truth,” replied Bickley, growing furious. “It is that you use your Christianity as a cloak for bad manners. It teaches consideration and sympathy for others of which you seem to have none. Moreover, since you talk of the death of people’s wives, I will tell you something about your own, as a doctor, which I can do as I never attended her. It is highly probable, in my opinion, that she will die before Mrs. Arbuthnot, who is quite a healthy person with a good prospect of life.”

“Perhaps,” said Bastin. “If so, it will be God’s will and I shall not complain” (here Bickley snorted), “though I do not see what you can know about it. But why should you cast reflections on the early Christians who were people of strong principle living in rough times, and had to wage war against an established devil-worship? I know you are angry because they smashed up the statues of Venus and so forth, but had I been in their place I should have done the same.”

“Of course you would, who doubts it? But as for the early Christians and their iconoclastic performances﻿—well, curse them, that’s all!” and he sprang up and left the room.

I followed him.

Let it not be supposed from the above scene that there was any ill-feeling between Bastin and Bickley. On the contrary they were much attached to each other, and this kind of quarrel meant no more than the strong expression of their individual views to which they were accustomed from their college days. For instance Bastin was always talking about the early Christians and missionaries, while Bickley loathed both, the early Christians because of the destruction which they had wrought in Egypt, Italy, Greece and elsewhere, of all that was beautiful; and the missionaries because, as he said, they were degrading and spoiling the native races and by inducing them to wear clothes, rendering them liable to disease. Bastin would answer that their souls were more important than their bodies, to which Bickley replied that as there was no such thing as a soul except in the stupid imagination of priests, he differed entirely on the point. As it was quite impossible for either to convince the other, there the conversation would end, or drift into something in which they were mutually interested, such as natural history and the hygiene of the neighbourhood.

===Chinese===

"那么我也说一句我以为是真话的话，"比克利答道，怒气上来了。"我说的就是：你拿你的基督教当无礼的斗篷。它教人体恤他人、同情他人，你似乎一样也没有。再者，既然你谈起人家太太的死，那我就以医生的身份，也讲讲你太太的事——这个身份我够格，因为我从没给她看过病。照我看，极可能她比阿巴思诺特太太死得早，后者是个相当健康的人，寿数还长着呢。"

"也许，"巴斯廷说。"若是那样，那是上帝的旨意，我不会抱怨"（比克利在这里哼了一声），"虽然我不明白你对这会有什么可知道的。可是你为什么要诋毁早年的基督徒？他们是原则坚定的人，活在粗粝的年代，不得不同一个根深蒂固的魔鬼崇拜作战。我知道你生气，因为他们砸碎了维纳斯之类的雕像；可我若处在他们的位置，也会照样做。"

"你当然会，谁怀疑呢？可至于早年的基督徒和他们的砸像伟业——哼，咒诅他们，如此而已！"他一跃而起，冲出了房间。

我跟了出去。

不要因为上面那一幕，便以为巴斯廷同比克利之间存着什么芥蒂。恰恰相反，两人感情很深；这类争吵，无非是各自观点的激烈表达，从大学时代起他们便惯于此道。比如，巴斯廷开口闭口总是早年的基督徒和传教士，比克利对两样都深恶痛绝：恨早年的基督徒，是因为他们在埃及、意大利、希腊等地毁灭了一切美的东西；恨传教士，是因为——照他的说法——他们在败坏、糟蹋土著种族，引得他们穿上衣服，反而容易染病。巴斯廷会回答说，灵魂比肉体要紧；比克利便答道，既然根本没有灵魂这回事——除了神甫们的蠢想头——他在这一点上全然异议。既然谁也说服不了谁，谈话便到此为止，或者岔到他们彼此都有兴趣的事情上去，比如博物学和本地的卫生。

===Original===

Here I may state that Bickley’s keen professional eye was not mistaken when he diagnosed Mrs. Bastin’s state of health as dangerous. As a matter of fact she was suffering from heart disease that a doctor can often recognise by the colour of the lips, etc., which brought about her death under the following circumstances:

Her husband attended some ecclesiastical function at a town over twenty miles away and was to have returned by a train which would have brought him home about five o’clock. As he did not arrive she waited at the station for him until the last train came in about seven o’clock﻿—without the beloved Basil. Then, on a winter’s night she tore up to the Priory and begged me to lend her a dogcart in which to drive to the said town to look for him. I expostulated against the folly of such a proceeding, saying that no doubt Basil was safe enough but had forgotten to telegraph, or thought that he would save the sixpence which the wire cost.

Then it came out, to Natalie’s and my intense amusement, that all this was the result of her jealous nature of which I have spoken.
