Expression after expression flitted across the features; indeed these seemed to change so much from moment to moment that they might have belonged to several different individuals, though each was beautiful. The fact of these remarkable changes with the suggestion of multiform personalities which they conveyed impressed both Bickley and myself very much indeed. Then the breast heaved tumultuously; it even appeared to struggle. Next the eyes opened. They were full of wonder, even of fear, but oh! what marvelous eyes. I do not know how to describe them, I cannot even state their exact colour, except that it was dark, something like the blue of sapphires of the deepest tint, and yet not black; large, too, and soft as a deer’s. They shut again as though the light hurt them, then once more opened and wandered about, apparently without seeing.

===Chinese===

我们回来时，那人已跪在棺旁——他还站不住——发光的眼睛紧紧锁住棺中睡者的脸，长臂在她上方挥动。

"催眠把戏！不知灵不灵，"比克利耳语道。随后他举起注射器，探询地望着那人；那人摇头，继续他的迷离手法。

我绕到他身后，站在睡者的头旁，好守望她的脸——那张脸值得守望——比克利带着他的药守在她脚边，我想是在某种酒精或酸里给注射器消毒。我料他正要试用注射器，忽然间，仿佛受催眠手法的驱遣，闪光夫人的脸上起了变化。此前，虽然美丽，那是一张死人的脸——尽管是一个几小时前、至多一天多以前，在圆满的健康与活力中骤然被斩断的人的脸。此刻它开始活过来；仿佛灵魂正从远方归来，且归得不易、历尽艰辛。

一种神情又一种神情掠过那五官；实在说，它们时时刻刻变换，仿佛分属好几个不同的人，虽然个个都美。这些显著的变换，连同它们所暗示的多重人格，给比克利和我都留下极深的印象。随后胸口剧烈起伏；它甚至像在挣扎。接着眼睛睁开了。里面盛满惊奇，甚至恐惧，可是哦！多么奇妙的眼睛。我不知道怎样形容，连它们的准确颜色也说不清，只知是深的，近似最深色的蓝宝石的蓝，却不是黑；而且大，柔得像鹿的眼睛。它们又阖上，仿佛光刺得疼，然后再次睁开，四下里游移，分明视而不见。

===Original===

At length they found my face, for I was still bending over her, and, resting there, appeared to take it in by degrees. More, it seemed to touch and stir some human spring in the still-sleeping heart. At least the fear passed from her features and was replaced by a faint smile, such as a patient sometimes gives to one known and well loved, as the effects of chloroform pass away. For a while she looked at me with an earnest, searching gaze, then suddenly, for the first time moving her arms, lifted them and threw them round my neck.

The old man stared, bending his imperial brows into a little frown, but did nothing. Bickley stared also through his glasses and sniffed as though in disapproval, while I remained quite still, fighting with a wild impulse to kiss her on the lips as one would an awakening and beloved child. I doubt if I could have done so, however, for really I was immovable; my heart seemed to stop and all my muscles to be paralysed.

I do not know for how long this endured, but I do know how it ended. Presently in the intense silence I heard Bastin’s heavy voice and looking round, saw his big head projecting into the sepulchre.

“Well, I never!” he said, “you seem to have woke them up with a vengeance. If you begin like *that* with the lady, there will be complications before you have done, Arbuthnot.”

Talk of being brought back to earth with a rush! I could have killed Bastin, and Bickley, turning on him like a tiger, told him to be off, find wood and light a large fire in front of the statue. I think he was about to argue when the Ancient gave him a glance of his fierce eyes, which alarmed him, and he departed, bewildered, to return presently with the wood.

But the sound of his voice had broken the spell. The Lady let her arms fall with a start, and shut her eyes again, seeming to faint. Bickley sprang forward with his sal volatile and applied it to her nostrils, the Ancient not interfering, for he seemed to recognise that he had to deal with a man of skill and one who meant well by them.

===Chinese===

末了它们找到我的脸——我仍俯身在她上方——便停在那里，似乎一层一层地把她领会进去。不止如此，它仿佛触动了仍在沉睡的心里某一根人性的弦。至少，恐惧从她五官上退去，换上一个淡淡的微笑，像病人有时对相识而深爱的人报以的那种微笑，当氯仿的余力散尽的时候。有一阵，她以热切而探索的目光望着我，随后突然——头一回挪动手臂——抬起双臂，环住我的脖颈。

老人瞪着眼，把帝王气象的浓眉蹙成小小的怒容，却没有动。比克利也瞪着眼，隔着眼镜嗅了一声，似乎不以为然；我则僵立不动，同狂野的冲动搏斗——想吻她的唇，像吻一个苏醒的、亲爱的孩子。然而我疑心我吻不成，因为我真的动弹不得；我的心似乎停了，全身的肌肉都瘫了。

不知这状态延续了多久，但我确知它怎样收场。极度的寂静里，我忽然听见巴斯廷沉重的嗓音，回头一看，他的大脑袋正探进墓室来。

"哎呀，了不得！"他说，"看来你们把他们弄醒得可不轻。倘若你开头就对这位夫人来*这一手*，阿巴思诺特，事情没完就会生出麻烦来。"

说起猛地被拽回尘世！我简直能杀死巴斯廷；比克利像老虎似的朝他转过身去，叫他滚，去找柴，在雕像前面生一堆大火。我想他还想争辩，古代人猛地给他一个凶眼，把他吓住，他便晕头转向地走了，不久抱柴回来。

可他嗓音的响动已经破了魔。夫人一惊，双臂落下，重新闭眼，似乎昏了过去。比克利抢步上前，把嗅盐凑到她鼻孔下；古代人没有干涉，因为他似乎认出，这是一个有本领的人，一个对他们存好意的人。

===Original===

In the end we brought her round again and, to omit details, Bickley gave her, not coffee and brandy, but a mixture he compounded of hot water, preserved milk and meat essence. The effect of it on her was wonderful, since a few minutes after swallowing it she sat up in the coffin. Then we lifted her from that narrow bed in which she had slept for﻿—ah! how long? and perceived that beneath her also were crystal boxes of the radiant, heat-giving substance. We sat her on the floor of the sepulchre, wrapping her also in a blanket.

Now it was that Tommy, after frisking round her as though in welcome of an old friend, calmly established himself beside her and laid his black head upon her knee. She noted it and smiled for the first time, a marvelously sweet and gentle smile. More, she placed her slender hand upon the dog and stroked him feebly.

Bickley tried to make her drink some more of his mixture, but she refused, motioning him to give it to Tommy. This, however, he would not do because there was but one cup. Presently both of the sleepers began to shiver, which caused Bickley anxiety. Abusing Bastin beneath his breath for being so long with the fire, he drew the blankets closer about them.

Then an idea came to him and he examined the glowing boxes in the coffin. They were loose, being merely set in prepared cavities in the crystal. Wrapping our handkerchiefs about his hand, he took them out and placed them around the wakened patients, a proceeding of which the Ancient nodded approval. Just then, too, Bastin returned with his first load of firewood, and soon we had a merry blaze going just outside the sepulchre. I saw that they observed the lighting of this fire by means of a match with much interest.

===Chinese===

末了我们把她弄醒过来；细节从略，比克利给她的不是咖啡和白兰地，而是他调的一种合剂：热水、炼乳和肉汁。它对她的效力奇妙，咽下几分钟，她便在棺中坐了起来。随后我们从她睡过——啊！睡了多久？——的那张窄床上把她抱出来，发觉她身下也有盛着那种放光发热之物的水晶匣。我们让她坐在墓室地上，也用毯子裹住她。

这时，汤米绕着她欢蹦，仿佛欢迎一位旧友，随后安然在她身旁安顿下来，把黑脑袋搁在她膝上。她注意到了，破题儿第一遭微笑，一个无比甜美温柔的微笑。不止如此，她把纤手放在狗身上，无力地抚摩。

比克利想让她再喝些他的合剂，她不肯，示意他给汤米。可他不肯，因为只有一只杯。不久两位睡者都开始发抖，这叫比克利着急起来。他低声咒骂巴斯廷生火生得太久，把毯子往他们身上又拢紧了些。

随后一个念头来了，他去查验棺中的发光匣。匣子是松的，不过嵌在水晶上备好的凹窝里。他用手帕裹住手，把它们取出来，安置在苏醒的病人四周；对这番举动，古代人点头赞许。恰在此时，巴斯廷抱着第一批柴回来了，不久我们在墓室外燃起一团欢腾的火。我看见，他们带着很大的兴趣观察我用火柴点火的动作。

===Original===

Now they grew warm again, as indeed we did also﻿—too warm. Then in my turn I had an idea. I knew that by now the sun would be beating hotly against the rock of the mount, and suggested to Bickley, that, if possible, the best thing we could do would be to get them into its life-giving rays. He agreed, if we could make them understand and they were able to walk. So I tried. First I directed the Ancient’s attention to the mouth of the cave which at this distance showed as a white circle of light. He looked at it and then at me with grave inquiry. I made motions to suggest that he should proceed there, repeating the word “Sun” in the Orofenan tongue. He understood at once, though whether he read my mind rather than what I said I am not sure. Apparently the Glittering Lady understood also and seemed to be most anxious to go. Only she looked rather pitifully at her feet and shook her head. This decided me.

I do not know if I have mentioned anywhere that I am a tall man and very muscular. She was tall, also, but as I judged not so very heavy after her long fast. At any rate I felt quite certain that I could carry her for that distance. Stooping down, I lifted her up, signing to her to put her arms round my neck, which she did. Then calling to Bickley and Bastin to bring along the Ancient between them, with some difficulty I struggled out of the sepulchre, and started down the cave. She was more heavy than I thought, and yet I could have wished the journey longer. To begin with she seemed quite trustful and happy in my arms, where she lay with her head against my shoulder, smiling a little as a child might do, especially when I had to stop and throw her long hair round my neck like a muffler, to prevent it from trailing in the dust.

===Chinese===

此刻他们重新暖和起来——我们也一样，甚至太暖。随后轮到我出主意了。我知道这会儿太阳正炽热地晒着山的岩体，便向比克利建议：倘若办得到，最好的办法是把他们弄进它赐生命的阳光里去。他赞成——倘若能让他们明白，他们又走得动。于是我试着来。我先引古代人注意洞口——隔着这段距离，它显成一圈白亮的光。他望望它，又庄重地探询地望望我。我打手势，示意他到那里去，并用奥罗费纳语重复"太阳"这个词。他立刻懂了，虽然他究竟读了我的心思，还是听懂了我的话，我不敢断言。显然闪光夫人也懂了，并且似乎极想去。只是她怜悯似的看了看自己的脚，摇头。这让我拿定了主意。

我不知道我在哪里提过没有：我是个高个子，肌肉发达。她个子也高，但据我判断，久绝饮食之后并不很重。无论如何，我有十分的把握，那段路我抱得动她。我俯下身，把她抱起，示意她双臂环住我的脖颈，她照办了。随后我叫比克利和巴斯廷两边架着古代人跟上，我费力地挤出墓室，顺洞穴往下走。她比我想的沉，可我倒宁愿这路再长些。首先，她在我怀里似乎全然信赖而快乐，头靠着我的肩，微微含笑，像个孩子；尤其当我不得不停下来，把她长发绕到我脖子上围成一圈围巾、免得拖进尘土里的时候。

===Original===

A bundle of lavender, or a truss of new-mown hay, could not have been more sweet to carry and there was something electric about the touch of her, which went through and through me. Very soon it was over, and we were out of the cave into the full glory of the tropical sun. At first, that her eyes might become accustomed to its light and her awakened body to its heat, I set her down where shadow fell from the overhanging rock, in a canvas deck chair that had been brought by Marama with the other things, throwing the rug about her to protect her from such wind as there was. She nestled gratefully into the soft seat and shut her eyes, for the motion had tired her. I noted, however, that she drew in the sweet air with long breaths.

Then I turned to observe the arrival of the Ancient, who was being borne between Bickley and Bastin in what children know as a dandy-chair, which is formed by two people crossing their hands in a peculiar fashion. It says much for the tremendous dignity of his presence that even thus, with one arm round the neck of Bickley and the other round that of Bastin, and his long white beard falling almost to the ground, he still looked most imposing.

Unfortunately, however, just as they were emerging from the cave, Bastin, always the most awkward of creatures, managed to leave hold with one hand, so that his passenger nearly came to the ground. Never shall I forget the look that he gave him. Indeed, I think that from this moment he hated Bastin. Bickley he respected as a man of intelligence and learning, although in comparison with his own, the latter was infantile and crude; me he tolerated and even liked; but Bastin he detested. The only one of our party for whom he felt anything approaching real affection was the spaniel Tommy.

We set him down, fortunately uninjured, on some rugs, and also in the shadow. Then, after a little while, we moved both of them into the sun. It was quite curious to see them expand there. As Bickley said, what happened to them might well be compared to the development of a butterfly which has just broken from the living grave of its chrysalis and crept into the full, hot radiance of the light. Its crinkled wings unfold, their brilliant tints develop; in an hour or two it is perfect, glorious, prepared for life and flight, a new creature.

===Chinese===

一束熏衣草，或一捆新割的干草，抱着也不比这更香；她的触觉里有一种电流似的东西，穿透我，再穿透我。路程很快完了，我们出了洞，置身热带太阳的全副辉煌里。起初，为了让她的眼睛习惯于光、她苏醒的身体习惯于热，我把她放在悬岩的影子投下的地方，安置在一把帆布躺椅里——那是马拉玛随其他东西一起送来的——又把毯子披在她身上，挡一挡那点风。她感激地偎进柔软的椅座，闭上眼，因为挪动使她疲乏了。然而我注意到，她长长地、深深地吸着甜美的空气。

随后我转身去看古代人的到达。他正被比克利和巴斯廷架着——架法就是孩子们熟知的"轿杠椅"，由两人以一种特别的方式交叉双手而成。他仪容的威严实在惊人：就这样，一条臂搂着比克利的脖颈，另一条搂着巴斯廷的脖颈，长长的白胡须几乎垂到地上，他看上去仍旧气度凛然。

不幸的是，恰在他们出洞的时候，巴斯廷——天生的最笨手笨脚的家伙——竟用一只手撒了把，害得他的乘客几乎摔在地上。他给巴斯廷的那一眼，我永世难忘。实在说，我想从这一刻起，他恨上了巴斯廷。比克利他是敬重的，当作一个有学识的聪明人——虽然同他自己的相比，那学问幼稚而粗糙；我，他容忍，甚至喜欢；巴斯廷，他憎恶。我们这一伙里，唯一叫他感到近乎真情之爱的，是那只小獚犬汤米。

===Original===

So it was with this pair, from moment to moment they gathered strength and vigour. Nearby to them, as it happened, stood a large basket of the luscious native fruits brought that morning by the Orofenans, and at these the Lady looked with longing. With Bickley’s permission, I offered them to her and to the Ancient, first peeling them with my fingers. They ate of them greedily, a full meal, and would have gone on had not the stern Bickley, fearing untoward consequences, removed the basket. Again the results were wonderful, for half an hour afterwards they seemed to be quite strong. With my assistance the Glittering Lady, as I still call her, for at that time I did not know her name, rose from the chair, and, leaning on me, tottered a few steps forward. Then she stood looking at the sky and all the lovely panorama of nature beneath, and stretching out her arms as though in worship. Oh! how beautiful she seemed with the sunlight shining on her heavenly face!

Now for the first time I heard her voice. It was soft and deep, yet in it was a curious bell-like tone that seemed to vibrate like the sound of chimes heard from far away. Never have I listened to such another voice. She pointed to the sun whereof the light turned her radiant hair and garments to a kind of golden glory, and called it by some name that I could not understand. I shook my head, whereon she gave it a different name taken, I suppose, from another language. Again I shook my head and she tried a third time. To my delight this word was practically the same that the Orofenans used for “sun.”

“Yes,” I said, speaking very slowly, “so it is called by the people of this land.”

She understood, for she answered in much the same language:

“What, then, do you call it?”

“Sun in the English tongue,” I replied.

“Sun. English,” she repeated after me, then added, “How are you named, Wanderer?”

“Humphrey,” I answered.

“Hum-fe-ry!” she said as though she were learning the word, “and those?”

“Bastin and Bickley,” I replied.

Over these patronymics she shook her head; as yet they were too much for her.

“How are you named, Sleeper?” I asked.

“Yva,” she answered.

“A beautiful name for one who is beautiful,” I declared with enthusiasm, of course always in the rich Orofenan dialect which by now I could talk well enough.

===Chinese===

我们把他放在几张毯子上，幸而无伤，也在阴影里。随后过了一小会儿，我们把两位都挪进太阳里。看他们在阳光里舒展开来，实在奇妙。正如比克利所说，他们身上发生的事，恰可比作一只蝴蝶刚破开蛹的活坟、爬进炽热明亮的光辉里。它皱缩的翅膀展开，绚烂的色泽显现；一两个钟头后，它已完满、辉煌，为生命与飞翔作好准备，成了一个新的造物。

这一对正是如此，时时刻刻都在积聚力量与生气。凑巧，他们近旁立着一大篮甜美的本地果品，是那天早晨奥罗费纳人送来的；夫人以渴望的目光望着它。经比克利允许，我取来献给她和古代人，先用手指剥了皮。他们贪馋地吃了一顿饱的，若不是严厉的比克利怕出岔子把篮子撤走，他们还要吃下去。效果又一次奇妙：半小时后，他们似乎已相当有力气。在我的搀扶下，闪光夫人——我仍旧这样叫她，因为那时我还不知道她的名字——从躺椅上立起，靠着我，摇摇晃晃地向前挪了几步。随后她伫立着，仰望天空和天底下自然的一切可爱的全景，伸展双臂，仿佛在礼拜。哦！阳光照着她天上似的脸庞，她多么美啊！

===Original===

She repeated the words once or twice, then of a sudden caught their meaning, for she smiled and even coloured, saying hastily with a wave of her hand towards the Ancient who stood at a distance between Bastin and Bickley, “My father, Oro; great man; great king; great god!”

At this information I started, for it was startling to learn that here was the original Oro, who was still worshipped by the Orofenans, although of his actual existence they had known nothing for uncounted time. Also I was glad to learn that he was her father and not her old husband, for to me that would have been horrible, a desecration too deep for words.

“How long did you sleep, Yva?” I asked, pointing towards the sepulchre in the cave.

After a little thought she understood and shook her head hopelessly, then by an afterthought, she said,

“Stars tell Oro tonight.”

So Oro was an astronomer as well as a king and a god. I had guessed as much from those plates in the coffin which seemed to have stars engraved on them.

At this point our conversation came to an end, for the Ancient himself approached, leaning on the arm of Bickley who was engaged in an animated argument with Bastin.

“For Heaven’s sake!” said Bickley, “keep your theology to yourself at present. If you upset the old fellow and put him in a temper he may die.”

“If a man tells me that he is a god it is my duty to tell him that he is a liar,” replied Bastin obstinately.

“Which you did, Bastin, only fortunately he did not understand you. But for your own sake I advise you not to take liberties. He is not one, I think, with whom it is wise to trifle. I think he seems thirsty. Go and get some water from the rain pool, not from the lake.”

Bastin departed and presently returned with an aluminum jug full of pure water and a glass. Bickley poured some of it into a glass and handed it to Yva who bent her head in thanks. Then she did a curious thing. Having first lifted the glass with both hands to the sky and held it so for a few seconds, she turned and with an obeisance poured a little of it on the ground before her father’s feet.

A libation, thought I to myself, and evidently Bastin agreed with me, for I heard him mutter,

“I believe she is making a heathen offering.”

===Chinese===

此刻我头一回听见她的嗓音。它轻柔而深沉，其中却有一种奇特的钟鸣似的音色，像远处传来的排钟声那样震颤。这样的嗓音我从未听过第二回。她指指太阳——它的光把她的华发和衣裳照成一种金色的荣光——用某个名字称呼它，我听不懂。我摇头，她便换了一个名字——我猜是取自另一种语言。我又摇头，她试了第三次。令我欢喜的是，这个词同奥罗费纳人叫"太阳"的那个词几乎一模一样。

"是的，"我说，说得极慢，"这片土地上的人这样叫它。"

她懂了，因为她用大致相同的语言答道：

"那么，你们叫它什么？"

"英语叫太阳（Sun），"我答。

"太阳。英语，"她跟着我念，又补一句："你叫什么名字，流浪者？"

"汉弗莱，"我答。

"汉-弗-莱！"她说，仿佛在学这个词；"那两位呢？"

"巴斯廷和比克利，"我答。

对这两个姓氏她摇了摇头；它们眼下还太难为她。

"你叫什么名字，睡者？"我问。

"伊娃，"她答。

"美人的美名，"我热忱地宣称，当然始终用的是丰富的奥罗费纳方言——那时我已说得相当好了。

===Original===

Doubtless we were right, for Oro accepted the homage by a little motion of the head. After this, at a sign from him she drank the water. Then the glass was refilled and handed to Oro who also held it towards the sky. He, however, made no libation but drank at once, two tumblers of it in rapid succession.

By now the direct sunlight was passing from the mouth of the cave, and though it was hot enough, both of them shivered a little. They spoke together in some language of which we could not understand a word, as though they were debating what their course of action should be. The dispute was long and earnest. Had we known what was passing, which I learned afterwards, it would have made us sufficiently anxious, for the point at issue was nothing less than whether we should or should not be forthwith destroyed﻿—an end, it appears, that Oro was quite capable of bringing about if he so pleased. Yva, however, had very clear views of her own on the matter and, as I gather, even dared to threaten that she would protect us by the use of certain powers at her command, though what these were I do not know.

While the event hung doubtful Tommy, who was growing bored with these long proceedings, picked up a bough still covered with flowers which, after their pretty fashion, the Orofenans had placed on the top of one of the baskets of food. This small bough he brought and laid at the feet of Oro, no doubt in the hope that he would throw it for him to fetch, a game in which the dog delighted. For some reason Oro saw an omen in this simple canine performance, or he may have thought that the dog was making an offering to him, for he put his thin hand to his brow and thought a while, then motioned to Bastin to pick up the bough and give it to him.

Next he spoke to his daughter as though assenting to something, for I saw her sigh in relief. No wonder, for he was conveying his decision to spare our lives and admit us to their fellowship.

After this again they talked, but in quite a different tone and manner. Then the Glittering Lady said to me in her slow and archaic Orofenan:

“We go to rest. You must not follow. We come back perhaps tonight, perhaps next night. We are quite safe. You are quite safe under the beard of Oro. Spirit of Oro watch you. You understand?”

I said I understood, whereon she answered:

“Goodbye, O Humfe-ry.”

“Goodbye, O Yva,” I replied, bowing.

===Chinese===

她把这个词重复了一两遍，随后猛地抓住了它的意思，因为她笑了，甚至红了脸，匆匆一面说，一面朝站在远处、站在巴斯廷和比克利之间的古代人挥手："我的父亲，奥罗；伟大的人；伟大的王；伟大的神！"

这信息叫我吃了一惊，因为得知眼前这位就是真奥罗——奥罗费纳人至今还在敬拜的那一位，虽然多少万年以来他们对他实际的存在一无所知——实在令人震动。得知他是她的父亲而非她的老丈夫，我又暗自欢喜，因为若是后者，那对我说来就太可怕了，是一种言语无法形容的亵渎。

"你睡了多久，伊娃？"我指着洞穴里那座墓问。

略一思索，她懂了，无可奈何地摇头，随后灵机一动，说：

"星星今晚告诉奥罗。"

原来奥罗不但是王、是神，还是天文学家。这一点我早从棺中那两片刻着星辰似的金属板上猜到几分。

我们的谈话到此为止，因为古代人本人走近前来，扶着比克利的臂——比克利正同巴斯廷争得起劲。

"看在上天的分上！"比克利说，"眼下把你的神学收起来。倘若你把这老家伙搅得动了怒，他会死的。"

"倘若一个人告诉我他是神，我的本分就是告诉他，他说谎，"巴斯廷固执地答。

"你确实告诉了，巴斯廷，幸好他没听懂你。为了你自己，我劝你别放肆。这个人，我想，不是可以随便戏弄的。我看他似乎渴了。去雨水洼里打点水来，别用湖里的。"

巴斯廷去了，不久提回一把铝壶，盛着清水，还带一只玻璃杯。比克利斟了些到杯里，递给伊娃；她低头称谢。随后她做了一件古怪的事：先用双手把杯举向天空，这样捧了几秒钟，然后转过身，行了一礼，在她父亲脚前把一点点水浇在地上。

一奠祭酒，我心里想；巴斯廷显然同我想到一处，因为我听见他咕哝：

"我看她正在行异教的祭礼。"

===Original===

Thereon they turned and refusing all assistance from us, vanished into the darkness of the cave leaning upon each other and walking slowly.

===Chinese===

无疑我们没有看错，因为奥罗以微微点首受了这个敬礼。此后，他一示意，她便饮水。随后杯子又斟满，递给奥罗；他也举向天空，却不奠祭，立刻饮了，接连两大杯。

此刻直射的阳光正从洞口移开；虽然天还够热，两人都微微发抖。他们用一种我们一字听不懂的语言交谈，仿佛在商议他们该采取什么方针。争论漫长而恳切。倘若我们当时知道正在商议的是什么——我后来才知道——准会惶惶不安，因为争的点不是别的，正是：我们应不应该立刻被毁灭——看来，只要奥罗高兴，他完全有能力促成这个结局。然而伊娃在这件事上自有十分明确的主见；据我领会，她甚至敢于威胁说，她将动用她掌握的某种力量保护我们，虽然那是什么力量，我不知道。

胜负未决之际，汤米——对这一场冗长的过程已经厌倦——叼起一根还开满花的树枝——奥罗费纳人照他们雅致的习惯，把这样的花枝搁在一只食篮顶上——把小花枝衔到奥罗脚前放下，无疑是巴望他掷出去好让它衔回，那是它酷爱的游戏。不知为何，奥罗在这桩单纯的犬类把戏里看出了一个预兆，或者他以为狗是在向他献祭，因为他把瘦手放到额上，思索片刻，随后示意巴斯廷拾起花枝递给他。

接着他对女儿说了句什么，像是应允了什么，因为我看见她如释重负地舒了一口气。难怪，因为他传达的决定是：饶我们的性命，纳我们入他们的交谊。

此后他们又交谈，声气与方式全然不同了。随后闪光夫人用她那种缓慢而古奥的奥罗费纳语对我说：

"我们去安歇。你们不可跟随。也许明晚回来，也许后晚回来。我们十分平安。你们在奥罗的胡须底下十分平安。奥罗的灵看顾你们。你们明白？"

我说我明白，她便答道：

"再会，哦，汉弗莱。"

"再会，哦，伊娃，"我答，躬身行礼。

## XII / 第十二章
===Original===

Two Hundred and Fifty Thousand Years!

“You seem to have made the best of your time, old fellow,” said Bickley in rather a sour voice.

“I never knew people begin to call each other by their Christian names so soon,” added Bastin, looking at me with a suspicious eye.

“I know no other,” I said.

“Perhaps not, but at any rate *you* have another, though you don’t seem to have told it to her. Anyway, I am glad they are gone, for I was getting tired of being ordered by everybody to carry about wood and water for them. Also I am terribly hungry as I can’t eat before it is light. They have taken most of the best fruit to which I was looking forward, but thank goodness they do not seem to care for pork.”

“So am I,” said Bickley, who really looked exhausted. “Get the food, there’s a good fellow. We’ll talk afterwards.”

When we had eaten, somewhat silently, I asked Bickley what he made of the business; also whither he thought the sleepers had gone.

“I think I can answer the last question,” interrupted Bastin. “I expect it is to a place well known to students of the Bible which even Bickley mentions sometimes when he is angry. At any rate, they seem to be very fond of heat, for they wouldn’t part from it even in their coffins, and you will admit that they are not quite natural, although that Glittering Lady is so attractive as regards her exterior.”

Bickley waved these remarks aside and addressed himself to me.

“I don’t know what to think of it,” he said; “but as the experience is not natural and everything in the Universe, so far as we know it, has a natural explanation, I am inclined to the belief that we are suffering from hallucinations, which in their way are also quite natural. It does not seem possible that two people can really have been asleep for an unknown length of time enclosed in vessels of glass or crystal, kept warm by radium or some such substance, and then emerge from them comparatively strong and well. It is contrary to natural law.”

“How about microbes?” I asked. “They are said to last practically forever, and they are living things. So in their case your natural law breaks down.”

===Chinese===

二十五万年！

"看来你把时间利用得很好，老伙计，"比克利说，声调相当酸。

"我从不知道有谁这么快便叫起彼此的教名来，"巴斯廷补上一句，狐疑地瞟着我。

"我没有别的名字，"我说。

"也许没有，可无论如何，*你*另有称呼，虽然你似乎没告诉她。不管怎样，我很高兴他们走了，我正腻烦了人人支使我给他们搬柴提水。还有，我饿得厉害，天亮前我吃不下东西。我最盼着的那些好果子大半叫他们拿走了，但谢天谢地，他们似乎不碰猪肉。"

"我也是，"比克利说，他看上去真是筋疲力尽。"把吃的拿来，乖伙计。话咱们回头再说。"

我们吃完了——吃得相当沉默——我问比克利对这件事怎么看，还问那些睡者去了哪里。

"末一个问题我想我能答，"巴斯廷插话。"我料想是去了一个研读圣经的人熟知的去处，连比克利发火时也偶尔提它。无论如何，他们似乎极爱热，连躺在棺材里也不肯离开热；你会承认，他们不大自然，虽然那位闪光夫人论外表倒很动人。"

比克利挥手把这些话挥开，对我说：

"我不知道该怎么想，"他说；"但既然这经历不自然，而宇宙中一切——照我们所知——都有自然的解释，我倾向于认为我们害了幻觉，幻觉本身也全然自然。两个人真能在玻璃或水晶的容器里，靠镭或类似之物保温，沉睡一段不知多长的时间，然后出来还相当健旺，这似乎不可能。这违背自然律。"

"那微生物呢？"我问。"据说它们实际上能存活到永远，它们是活物。所以在它们身上，你的自然律失效了。"

===Original===

“That is true,” he answered. “Some microbes in a sealed tube and under certain conditions do appear to possess indefinite powers of life. Also radium has an indefinite life, but that is a mineral. Only these people are not microbes nor are they minerals. Also, experience tells us that they could not have lived for more than a few months at the outside in such circumstances as we seemed to find them.”

“Then what do you suggest?”

“I suggest that we did not really find them at all; that we have all been dreaming. You know that there are certain gases which produce illusions, laughing gas is one of them, and that these gases are sometimes met with in caves. Now there were very peculiar odours in that place under the statue, which may have worked upon our imaginations in some such way. Otherwise we are up against a miracle, and, as you know, I do not believe in miracles.”

“*I* do,” said Bastin calmly. “You’ll find all about it in the Bible if you will only take the trouble to read. Why do you talk such rubbish about gases?”

“Because only gas, or something of the sort, could have made us imagine them.”

“Nonsense, Bickley! Those people were here right enough. Didn’t they eat our fruit and drink the water I brought them without ever saying thank you? Only, they are not human. They are evil spirits, and for my part I don’t want to see any more of them, though I have no doubt Arbuthnot does, as that Glittering Lady threw her arms round his neck when she woke up, and already he is calling her by her Christian name, if the word Christian can be used in connection with her. The old fellow had the impudence to tell us that he was a god, and it is remarkable that he should have called himself Oro, seeing that the devil they worship on the island is also called Oro and the place itself is named Orofena.”

“As to where they have gone,” continued Bickley, taking no notice of Bastin, “I really don’t know. My expectation is, however, that when we go to look tomorrow morning﻿—and I suggest that we should not do so before then in order that we may give our minds time to clear﻿—we shall find that sepulchre place quite empty, even perhaps without the crystal coffins we have imagined to stand there.”

===Chinese===

"这倒是真的，"他答。"某些微生物封在试管里、在特定条件下，确实显得拥有无限的生命力。镭的寿命也是无限的，可那是一种矿物。只是这些人既不是微生物，也不是矿物。况且经验告诉我们，在我们仿佛发现他们的那种境况里，他们至多活不过几个月。"

"那么你怎么解释？"

"我主张我们根本没有真的发现他们；我们全在做梦。你知道某些气体能制造幻象，笑气便是其中之一，这类气体在洞穴里有时会遇到。而那雕像底下的地方，气味非常特别，说不定就以这种方式作用于我们的想象。否则我们就是撞见了神迹，而你知道，我不信神迹。"

"*我*信，"巴斯廷平静地说。"你只消费心读一读圣经，里面样样都写着。你扯什么气体的胡话？"

"因为只有气体，或类似的东西，才能叫我们凭空想出他们来。"

"胡说，比克利！那些人确确实实在这里。他们吃我们的果子、喝我打给他们的水，可曾道过一声谢？只不过，他们不是人。他们是恶灵，就我而言我不想再见他们，虽然我不怀疑阿巴思诺特想——那位闪光夫人醒来时用胳膊搂了他的脖子，他已经在叫她的教名了，倘若"基督徒"这个词还用得上她的话。那老头子竟厚着脸皮说他自己是神；他自称奥罗，也真够巧，岛上那伙人敬拜的魔鬼也叫奥罗，那地方本身又名叫奥罗费纳。"

"至于他们去了哪里，"比克利接着说，对巴斯廷毫不理会，"我实在不知道。不过我的预料是：明早我们去看的时候——我建议今晚先不去，好让我们的脑子清一清——会发现那座墓室空空如也，说不定连我们想象中摆在那里的水晶棺材也没有。"

===Original===

“Perhaps we shall find that there isn’t a cave at all and that we are not sitting on a flat rock outside of it,” suggested Bastin with heavy sarcasm, adding, “You are clever in your way, Bickley, but you can talk more rubbish than any man I ever knew.”

“They told us they would come back tonight or tomorrow,” I said. “If they do, what will you say then, Bickley?”

“I will wait till they come to answer that question. Now let us go for a walk and try to change our thoughts. We are all overstrained and scarcely know what we are saying.”

“One more question,” I said as we rose to start. “Did Tommy suffer from hallucinations as well as ourselves?”

“Why not?” answered Bickley. “He is an animal just as we are, or perhaps we thought we saw Tommy do the things he did.”

“When you found that basket of fruit, Bastin, which the natives brought over in the canoe, was there a bough covered with red flowers lying on the top of it?”

“Yes, Arbuthnot, one bough only; I threw it down on the rock as it got in the way when I was carrying the basket.”

“Which flowering bough we all thought we saw the Sleeper Oro carry away after Tommy had brought it to him.”

“Yes; he made me pick it up and give it to him,” said Bastin.

“Well, if we did not see this it should still be lying on the rock, as there has been no wind and there are no animals here to carry it away. You will admit that, Bickley?”

He nodded.

“Then if it has gone you will admit also that the presumption is that we saw what we thought we did see?”

“I do not know how that conclusion can be avoided, at any rate so far as the incident of the bough is concerned,” replied Bickley with caution.

===Chinese===

"说不定我们会发现根本没有什么洞穴，我们也不是坐在洞外的平顶石上，"巴斯廷愚钝地讥讽道，又补一句："你自有你的聪明，比克利，可你说的废话比我认识的任何人都多。"

"他们说过今晚或明晚回来，"我说。"倘若真回来了，你到时又说什么呢，比克利？"

"等他们来了我再回答这个问题。现在我们散步去吧，换换脑子。我们全都绷得太紧，几乎不知道自己在说什么。"

"还有一个问题，"我们起身时我说。"汤米也害幻觉了吗，跟我们一样？"

"为什么不呢？"比克利答。"它同我们一样是动物；或者，也许我们只是以为看见汤米干了那些事。"

"巴斯廷，土人用独木舟送来的那篮果子，你找到时，篮子顶上可躺着一根开满红花的树枝？"

"有的，阿巴思诺特，只有一根；我把它扔在石头上了，搬篮子的时候碍事。"

"汤米把它衔给睡者奥罗之后，我们大家都以为看见他带着那根花枝走了。"

"是的；他叫我拾起来递给他，"巴斯廷说。

"好，倘若我们没看错，它此刻还该躺在石头上，因为没风，这里也没有会衔走它的活物。这一点你承认吧，比克利？"

他点头。

"那么，倘若它不见了，你也得承认：推论便是，我们看见的就是我们以为看见的东西？"

"我看不出这个结论怎么能避开，至少就花枝这一桩而言，"比克利谨慎地答道。

===Original===

Then, without more words, we started to look. At the spot where the bough should have been, there was no bough, but on the rock lay several of the red flowers, bitten off, I suppose, by Tommy while he was carrying it. Nor was this all. I think I have mentioned that the Glittering Lady wore sandals which were fastened with red studs that looked like rubies or carbuncles. On the rock lay one of these studs. I picked it up and we examined it. It had been sewn to the sandal-strap with golden thread or silk. Some of this substance hung from the hole drilled in the stone which served for an eye. It was as rotten as tinder, apparently with extreme age. Moreover, the hard gem itself was pitted as though the passage of time had taken effect upon it, though this may have been caused by other agencies, such as the action of the radium rays. I smiled at Bickley who looked disconcerted and even sad. In a way it is painful to see the effect upon an able and earnest man of the upsetting of his lifelong theories.

We went for our walk, keeping to the flat lands at the foot of the volcano cone, for we seemed to have had enough of wonders and to desire to reassure ourselves, as it were, by the study of natural and familiar things. As it chanced, too, we were rewarded by sundry useful discoveries. Thus we found a place where the bread-tree and other fruits, most of them now ripe, grew in abundance, as did the yam. Also, we came to an inlet that we noticed was crowded with large and beautiful fish from the lake, which seemed to find it a favourite spot. Perhaps this was because a little stream of excellent water ran in here, overflowing from the great pool or mere which filled the crater above.

At these finds we rejoiced greatly, for now we knew that we need not fear starvation even should our supply of food from the main island be cut off. Indeed, by help of some palm-leaf stalks which we wove together roughly, Bastin, who was rather clever at this kind of thing, managed to trap four fish weighing two or three pounds apiece, wading into the water to do so. It was curious to observe with what ease he adapted himself to the manners and customs of primeval man, so much so, indeed, that Bickley remarked that if he could believe in reincarnation, he would be absolutely certain that Bastin was a troglodyte in his last sojourn on the earth.

===Chinese===

于是，二话不说，我们动身去看。花枝本该在的地方没有花枝，石头上却躺着几朵红花——我想是汤米衔枝时咬落的。还不止此。我记得闪光夫人脚上的凉鞋缀着红石扣，看着像红宝石或红玉。石头上躺着一枚这样的石扣。我拾起来，我们细细验看。它是用金线或金丝缝在鞋带上的；一小截线还挂在石头的钻孔里——那孔便是扣眼。线已经糟朽如引火绒，显然因年代极久。而且，坚硬的宝石本身也起了麻点，仿佛时间的流逝在它身上生了效——虽然这也可能是别的因素所致，比如镭射线的作用。我朝比克利微笑，他看上去狼狈，甚至悲哀。眼见一个能干而认真的人毕生的理论被推翻，那种滋味，某种意义上是不好受的。

我们散步去了，沿着火山锥脚下的平地走，因为我们似乎已经受够了奇迹，巴不得借研究自然而熟悉的事物，可以说，给自己壮壮胆。凑巧，我们也得了几样有用的发现。比如，我们找到一处地方，面包树和其他果树——大多已经熟透——还有山药，长得分外繁茂。我们还遇到一个小水湾，里面挤满了湖中大而美丽的鱼，似乎那里是它们的乐土。也许因为一条极好的清水溪在这里注入，从上面灌满火山口的大水潭里漫溢下来。

得了这些收获，我们大大欢喜，因为纵使主岛的接济断了，我们也不必怕饿死了。巴斯廷干这类事相当灵巧，用几根棕榈叶茎粗粗编在一起，趟进水里，套住了四条鱼，每条两三磅重。看他如此轻易地适应原始人的风俗习惯，实在有趣，以致比克利说，倘若他能信轮回，他绝对可以断定，巴斯廷上一世驻留人间时是个穴居人。

===Original===

However this might be, Bastin’s primeval instincts and abilities were of the utmost service to us. Before we had been many days on that island he had built us a kind of native hut or house roofed with palm leaves in which, until provided with a better, as happened afterwards, we ate and he and Bickley slept, leaving the tent to me. Moreover, he wove a net of palm fibre with which he caught abundance of fish, and made fishing-lines of the same material (fortunately we had some hooks) which he baited with freshwater mussels and the insides of fish. By means of these he secured some veritable monsters of the carp species that proved most excellent eating. His greatest triumph, however, was a decoy which he constructed of boughs, wherein he trapped a number of waterfowl. So that soon we kept a very good table of a sort, especially after he had learned how to cook our food upon the native plan by means of hot stones. This suited us admirably, as it enabled Bickley and myself to devote all our time to archaeological and other studies which did not greatly interest Bastin.

By the time that we got back to camp it was drawing towards evening, so we cooked our food and ate, and then, thoroughly exhausted, made ourselves as comfortable as we could and went to sleep. Even our marvelous experiences could not keep Bickley and myself from sleeping, and on Bastin such things had no effect. He accepted them and that was all, much more readily than we did, indeed. Triple-armed as he was in the mail of a childlike faith, he snapped his fingers at evil spirits which he supposed the Sleepers to be, and at everything else that other men might dread.

===Chinese===

不管怎样，巴斯廷原始的本领对我们用处极大。上岛不出几天，他便给我们盖起一座土人的茅屋，苫着棕榈叶；直到后来有了更好的住处，我们就在那里吃饭，他和比克利睡在那里，帐篷留给我。此外，他用棕榈纤维编了一张网，捕到大量的鱼；又用同一种材料做了钓线（幸而我们有些鱼钩），以淡水蚌和鱼的内脏作饵。靠这些，他钓到几条鲤鱼属的十足巨物，其味极美。而他最大的得意之作，是一个用树枝搭成的诱捕棚，捉住了一批水禽。于是很快我们的餐桌便颇为可观，尤其在他学会用滚烫的石块按土法烹调之后。这法子对我们再合适不过，它让比克利和我得以把全部时间用于考古和其他研究——巴斯廷对那些不大感兴趣。

我们回到营地时，天已向晚，于是烧饭、吃饭，随后彻底力竭，尽量把自己安顿得舒服些，睡了。即便我们那些奇异的经历，也没能拦住比克利和我入睡；而这类事对巴斯廷更毫无作用。他接受它们，如此而已，实在说，比我们接受得痛快得多。他披着孩子般信心的锁子甲，三层护体，对恶灵——他认定那些睡者是恶灵——和其他一切旁人畏惧的东西，嗤之以鼻。

===Original===

Now, as I have mentioned, after our talk with Marama, although we did not think it wise to adventure ourselves among them again at present, we had lost all fear of the Orofenans. In this attitude, so far as Marama himself and the majority of his people were concerned, we were quite justified, for they were our warm friends. But in the case of the sorcerers, the priests and all their rascally and superstitious brotherhood, we were by no means justified. They had not forgiven Bastin his sacrilege or for his undermining of their authority by the preaching of new doctrines which, if adopted, would destroy them as a hierarchy. Nor had they forgiven Bickley for shooting one of their number, or any of us for our escape from the vengeance of their god.

So it came about that they made a plot to seize us all and hale us off to be sacrificed to a substituted image of Oro, which by now they had set up. They knew exactly where we slept upon the rock; indeed, our fire showed it to them and so far they were not afraid to venture, since here they had been accustomed for generations to lay their offerings to the god of the Mountain. Secretly on the previous night, without the knowledge of Marama, they had carried two more canoes to the borders of the lake. Now on this night, just as the moon was setting about three in the morning, they made their attack, twenty-one men in all, for the three canoes were large, relying on the following darkness to get us away and convey us to the place of sacrifice to be offered up at dawn and before Marama could interfere.

The first we knew of the matter, for most foolishly we had neglected to keep a watch, was the unpleasant sensation of brawny savages kneeling on us and trussing us up with palm-fibre ropes. Also they thrust handfuls of dry grass into our mouths to prevent us from calling out, although as air came through the interstices of the grass, we did not suffocate. The thing was so well done that we never struck a blow in self-defence, and although we had our pistols at hand, much less could we fire a shot. Of course, we struggled as well as we were able, but it was quite useless; in three minutes we were as helpless as calves in a net and like calves were being conveyed to the butcher. Bastin managed to get the gag out of his mouth for a few seconds, and I heard him say in his slow, heavy voice:

===Chinese===

现在，正如我提过的，同马拉玛谈过之后，虽然我们暂时不认为再冒险到他们中间去是明智的，我们对奥罗费纳人的恐惧已一扫而空。就马拉玛本人和他的大多数族人而言，这态度完全有理，因为他们是我们热忱的朋友。可是至于那些巫师、祭司和他们整个无赖而迷信的行会，我们毫无道理这样放心。他们没有饶恕巴斯廷的渎圣，也没有饶恕他以新教义的宣讲瓦解他们的权威——那教义一旦采行，便会把他们作为一个祭司阶层连根拔掉。他们也没有饶恕比克利开枪打死他们的同伙，没有饶恕我们任何人对他们的神的报复的逃遁。

于是他们定下毒计，要把我们一网擒住，拖去献给一尊新立的奥罗代用像——那像他们已经立起来了。我们睡在平顶石上的哪个位置，他们知道得分毫不差；我们的火堆便指给了他们；至今他们也敢于前来，因为多少代以来，他们惯于在此向山神奉献祭品。前一夜，他们瞒着马拉玛，偷偷又运了两只独木舟到湖边。就在这一夜，凌晨三点上下月亮落山之际，他们动手了，一共二十一人——三只独木舟都大——倚仗随后的黑暗把我们运走，送到献祭的地方，赶在黎明、赶在马拉玛能干涉之前把我们献上去。

我们对此事的第一点知觉——我们愚蠢之至，竟没有安排守望——是彪形野人跪在我们身上、用棕榈纤维绳捆我们的难受感觉。他们还往我们嘴里塞干草把，叫我们喊不出声；好在草把的缝隙透得进空气，我们没有窒息。这活做得干净利落，我们连一拳也没能回击；手枪虽在手边，更谈不上一枪。当然，我们尽力挣扎，却全然无用；三分钟内，我们已同网中的牛犊一样无助，也同牛犊一样正被送往屠夫那里。巴斯廷设法把嘴里的草把挤开几秒钟，我听见他用他那缓慢沉重的声调说：

===Original===

“This, Bickley, is what comes of trafficking with evil spirits in museum cases﻿—” There his speech stopped, for the grass wad was jammed down his throat again, but distinctly I heard the inarticulate Bickley snort as he conceived the repartee he was unable to utter. As for myself, I reflected that the business served us right for not keeping a watch, and abandoned the issue to fate.

Still, to confess the truth, I was infinitely more sorry to die than I should have been forty-eight hours earlier. This is a dull and in most ways a dreadful world, one, if we could only summon the courage, that some of us would be glad to leave in search of new adventures. But here a great and unprecedented adventure had begun to befall me, and before its mystery was solved, before even I could formulate a theory concerning it, my body must be destroyed, and my intelligence that was caged therein, sent far afield; or, if Bickley were right, eclipsed. It seemed so sad just when the impossible, like an unguessed wandering moon, had risen over the grey flats of the ascertained and made them shine with hope and wonder.

They carried us off to the canoes, not too gently; indeed, I heard the bony frame of Bastin bump into the bottom of one of them and reflected, not without venom, that it served him right as he was the fount and origin of our woes. Two stinking magicians, wearing on their heads undress editions of their court cages, since these were too cumbersome for active work of the sort, and painted all over with various pigments, were just about to swing me after him into the same, or another canoe, when something happened. I did not know what it was, but as a result, my captors left hold of me so that I fell to the rock, lying upon my back.

===Chinese===

"比克利，这就是同博物馆橱窗里的恶灵做买卖的下场——"他的话在这里断了，草把又塞回喉咙；我分明听见语不成声的比克利哼了一声，那是他想好了却说不出的回敬。至于我，心想这事全怪我们没守望，活该，便把结局交给命运。

尽管如此，说老实话，比起四十八小时之前，我此刻死要遗憾得多。这是个沉闷的、在大多数方面可怕的世界，只要鼓得起勇气，我们有些人乐意离开它去寻找新的冒险。可在这里，一场空前的伟大冒险已经开始落到我头上；而它的奥秘还未解开，我甚至还未定出一条关于它的理论，我的肉体便要被毁掉，我的困在其中的才智便要被遣往遥远的未知；或者——倘若比克利是对的——被抹去。恰恰在"不可能"像一轮未曾猜度的游移的月亮，升上已被查明之物的灰色平川，使它们闪耀着希望与惊奇的时候，这多么可悲。

他们把我们抬向独木舟，不怎么客气；实在说，我听见巴斯廷嶙峋的骨架咚地撞进某只舟的船底，便不无痛快地想：活该，他是我们祸事的源头。两个臭气熏天的巫师——头上戴着他们朝服宝筐的便装版，因为那玩意太笨重，不便干这类利落活——浑身涂着各色颜料，正要把我在他之后荡进同一只或另一只独木舟，忽然发生了一件事。我不知道是什么，但结果是，抓我的人撒了手，我摔在石头上，仰面躺着。

===Original===

Then, within my line of vision, which, it must be remembered, was limited because I could not lift my head, appeared the upper part of the tall person of the Ancient who said that he was named Oro. I could only see him down to his middle, but I noted vaguely that he seemed to be much changed. For instance, he wore a different coloured dress, or rather robe; this time it was dark blue, which caused me to wonder where on earth it came from. Also, his tremendous beard had been trimmed and dressed, and on his head there was a simple black cap, strangely quilted, which looked as though it were made of velvet. Moreover, his face had plumped out. He still looked ancient, it is true, and unutterably wise, but now he resembled an antique youth, so great were his energy and vigour. Also, his dark and glowing eyes shone with a fearful intensity. In short, he seemed impressive and terrible almost beyond imagining.

He looked about him slowly, then asked in a deep, cold voice, speaking in the Orofenan tongue:

“What do you, slaves?”

No one seemed able to answer, they were too horror-stricken at this sudden vision of their fabled god, whose fierce features of wood had become flesh; they only turned to fly. He waved his thin hand and they came to a standstill, like animals which have reached the end of their tether and are checked by the chains that bind them. There they stood in all sorts of postures, immovable and looking extremely ridiculous in their paint and feathers, with dread unutterable stamped upon their evil faces.

The Sleeper spoke again:

“You would murder as did your forefathers, O children of snakes and hogs fashioned in the shape of men. You would sacrifice those who dwell in my shadow to satisfy your hate because they are wiser than you. Come hither thou,” and he beckoned with a bony finger to the chief magician.

The man advanced towards him in short jumps, as a mechanical toy might do, and stood before him, his miniature crate and feathers all awry and the sweat of terror melting the paint in streaks upon his face.

“Look into the eyes of Oro, O worshipper of Oro,” said the Sleeper, and he obeyed, his own eyes starting out of his head.

===Chinese===

随后，进入我视野之内——记住，我的视野有限，因为头抬不起来——出现那位自称名叫奥罗的古代人的高大身形的一半。我只能看见他腰部以上，但隐约察觉他似乎大变样了。比如，他穿一件不同颜色的袍子；这回是深蓝色，我不禁纳闷它究竟从何而来。还有，他那惊人的长须修剪梳理过了；头上戴一顶朴素的黑色小帽，怪异地绗着缝，看着像天鹅绒做的。此外，他的脸丰润了。他看上去仍旧古老，这是真的，仍旧智慧得不可言说，可如今他更像一位古典的青年，精力与生气这样充沛。还有，他幽黑发光的双眼，闪耀着一种可怕的高度。总之，他显得威凛可畏，几乎超出想象。

他缓缓环视，随后用深冷的声调，以奥罗费纳语发问：

"你们在做什么，奴隶们？"

似乎没有人能答话，面对他们传说中的神突然显圣——那木头的凶相忽然成了血肉——他们吓得魂飞魄散；他们只想逃。他一挥瘦手，他们便钉在原地，像拽到绳头的牲口被锁链勒住，各色姿势站在那里，一动不动，涂彩插羽，丑态百出，凶恶的脸上铭刻着无法言说的恐惧。

那睡者又开口了：

"蛇与猪所生而披着人形的儿女啊，你们要像你们的祖先那样行凶。你们要把我荫庇下的人献上祭坛，只因为他们比你们聪明，以泄你们的仇恨。你，过来。"他用一根瘦指头朝首席巫师勾了勾。

那人像机械玩具似的一蹦一蹦挪到他跟前，站在他面前，小号的宝筐和羽毛全歪了，恐惧的汗水把脸上的颜料冲出一道道条纹。

"看看奥罗的眼睛，奥罗的敬拜者啊，"睡者说。他遵命了，自己的眼珠子从眼眶里突了出来。

===Original===

“Receive the curse of Oro,” said the Ancient again. Then followed a terrible spectacle. The man went raving mad. He bounded into the air to a height inconceivable. He threw himself upon the ground and rolled upon the rock. He rose again and staggered round and round, tearing pieces out of his arms with his teeth. He yelled hideously like one possessed. He grovelled, beating his forehead against the rock. Then he sat up, slowly choked and﻿—died.

His companions seemed to catch the infection of death as terrified savages often do. They too performed dreadful antics, all except three of them who stood paralysed. They rushed about battering each other with their fists and wooden weapons, looking like devils from hell in their hideous painted attire. They grappled and fought furiously. They separated and plunged into the lake, where with a last grimace they sank like stones.

It seemed to last a long while, but I think that as a matter of fact within five minutes it was over; they were all dead. Only the three paralysed ones remained standing and rolling their eyes.

The Sleeper beckoned to them with his thin finger, and they walked forward in step like soldiers.

“Lift that man from the boat,” he said, pointing to Bastin, “cut his bonds and those of the others.”

They obeyed with a wonderful alacrity. In a minute we stood at liberty and were pulling the grass gags from our mouths. The Ancient pointed to the head magician who lay dead upon the rock, his hideous, contorted countenance staring open-eyed at heaven.

“Take that sorcerer and show him to the other sorcerers yonder,” he said, “and tell them where your fellows are if they would find them. Know by these signs that the Oro, god of the Mountain, who has slept a while, is awake, and ill will it go with them who question his power or dare to try to harm those who dwell in his house. Bring food day by day and await commands. Begone!”

The dreadful-looking body was bundled into one of the canoes, that out of which Bastin had emerged. A rower sprang into each of them and presently was paddling as he had never done before. As the setting moon vanished, they vanished with it, and once more there was a great silence.

===Chinese===

"领受奥罗的咒诅吧，"古代人又说。随后是一幕可怕的景象。那人疯了，狂呼乱叫。他蹦到空中，高得难以想象。他扑在地上，在石头上打滚。他爬起来，一圈圈踉跄打转，用牙齿撕下自己臂上的肉。他像着了魔的人一样发出可怕的号叫。他匍匐在地，拿额头撞石头。随后他坐起身，慢慢窒息——死了。

他的同伙似乎染上了死亡的疯病，正如受惊的野人常有的那样。他们也做出种种可怕的花样，只有三个人除外，他们僵立着。他们四处奔窜，用拳头和木器互相殴打，浑身狰狞的彩饰，活像从地狱里逃出的魔鬼。他们扭打厮杀。他们散开，跳进湖里，做了最后一个鬼脸，像石头一样沉了下去。

这一切似乎持续了很久，但我想事实上不出五分钟便完了；他们全死了。只剩三个僵住的人还站着，眼珠乱转。

睡者用瘦指头朝他们一勾，他们便像士兵那样踏着一致的步伐走上前来。

"把那人从船里抬出来，"他指着巴斯廷说，"割断他的绳子，还有其余人的。"

他们以奇妙的神速遵命。一分钟内，我们自由了，正往外掏嘴里的草把。古代人指指躺在石头上的首席巫师——那张狰狞扭曲的脸还圆睁着眼瞪着天空。

"把这个巫师带走，给那边其余的巫师看看，"他说，"告诉他们，你们的人在哪里，倘若他们想去找的话。记住这些征兆：山神奥罗，小睡了一会儿的那位，已经醒来；谁质疑他的权力，谁胆敢伤害住在他屋檐下的人，谁就要遭殃。天天送食物来，听候吩咐。去吧！"

那具可怕的尸首被塞进一只独木舟——巴斯廷出来的那一只。每只舟里跳进一个划手，不久便拼命划起来，划得比他们此生任何时候都快。下落的月亮消失时，他们随它一同消失了，四周重归大寂静。

===Original===

“I am going to find my boots,” said Bastin. “This rock is hard and I hurt my feet kicking at those poor fellows who appear to have come to a bad end, how, I do not exactly understand. Personally, I think that more allowances should have been made for them, as I hope will be the case elsewhere, since after all they only acted according to their lights.”

“Curse their lights!” ejaculated Bickley, feeling his throat which was bruised. “I’m glad they are out.”

Bastin limped away in search of his boots, but Bickley and I stood where we were contemplating the awakened Sleeper. All recollection of the recent tumultuous scene seemed to have passed from his mind, for he was engaged in a study of the heavens. They were wonderfully brilliant now that the moon was down, brilliant as they only can be in the tropics when the sky is clear.

Something caused me to look round, and there, coming towards us, was she who said her name was Yva. Evidently all her weakness had departed also, for now she needed no support, but walked with a peculiar gliding motion that reminded me of a swan floating forward on the water. Well had we named her the Glittering Lady, for in the starlight literally she seemed to glitter. I suppose the effect came from her golden raiment, which, however, I noticed, as in her father’s case, was not the same that she had worn in the coffin; also from her hair that seemed to give out a light of its own. At least, she shimmered as she came, her tall shape swaying at every step like a willow in the wind. She drew near, and I saw that her face, too, had filled out and now was that of one in perfect health and vigour, while her eyes shone softly and seemed wondrous large.

===Chinese===

"我要去找我的靴子，"巴斯廷说。"这石头真硬，我踢那几个可怜家伙的时候伤了脚——他们看来下场不妙，怎么个不妙法，我不大明白。照我看，本该多体谅他们几分，就像我指望在别处也会得到体谅那样，毕竟他们只是照着自己的见识行事。"

"咒死他们的见识！"比克利嚷道，摸着他淤伤的喉咙。"他们完蛋了，我很高兴。"

巴斯廷一瘸一拐找靴子去了，比克利和我站在原地，端详这位苏醒的睡者。方才那场惊涛骇浪的记忆似乎全从他心里退去，因为他正仰观天象。月亮落下之后，星空灿烂极了，只有在热带、晴天里的星空才能灿烂到这个地步。

不知什么使我回过头去——是她，那个说她名叫伊娃的人，正朝我们走来。显然她的虚弱也全退了，因为她此刻无须搀扶，走着一种奇特的滑曳的步子，使我想起浮水而前的天鹅。我们叫她闪光夫人，叫得真好：星光之下，她当真仿佛在闪闪发光。我想那效果来自她金色的衣裳——我注意到，同她父亲一样，那已不是棺中的那一身——也来自她的头发，那头发仿佛自己放着光。至少，她走来时通体微光潋滟，高挑的身形一步一摇，像风中的柳。她走近了，我看见她的脸也丰润了，如今是一张十足健旺的脸，眼睛柔光闪闪，似乎大得出奇。

===Original===

In her hands she carried those two plates of metal which I had seen lying in the coffin of the Sleeper Oro. These she gave to him, then fell back out of his hearing﻿—if it were ever possible to do this, a point on which I am not sure﻿—and began to talk to me. I noted at once that in the few hours during which she was absent, her knowledge of the Orofenan tongue seemed to have improved greatly as though she had drunk deeply from some hidden fount of memory. Now she spoke it with readiness, as Oro had done when he addressed the sorcerers, although many of the words she used were not known to me, and the general form of her language appeared archaic, as for instance that of Spenser as compared with modern English. When she saw I did not comprehend her, however, she would stop and cast her sentences in a different shape, till at length I caught her meaning. Now I give the substance of what she said.

“You are safe,” she began, glancing first at the palm ropes that lay upon the rock and then at my wrists, one of which was cut.

“Yes, Lady Yva, thanks to your father.”

“You should say thanks to me. My father was thinking of other things, but I was thinking of you strangers, and from where I was I saw those wicked ones coming to kill you.”

“Oh! from the top of the mountain, I suppose.”

She shook her head and smiled but vouchsafed no further explanation, unless her following words can be so called. These were:

“I can see otherwise than with my eyes, if I choose.” A statement that caused Bickley, who was listening, to mutter:

“Impossible! What the deuce can she mean? Telepathy, perhaps.”

“I saw,” she continued, “and told the Lord, my father. He came forth. Did he kill them? I did not look to learn.”

“Yes. They lie in the lake, all except three whom he sent away as messengers.”

“I thought so. Death is terrible, O Humphrey, but it is a sword which those who rule must use to smite the wicked and the savage.”

Not wishing to pursue this subject, I asked her what her father was doing with the metal plates.

“He reads the stars,” she answered, “to learn how long we have been asleep. Before we went to sleep he made two pictures of them, as they were then and as they should be at the time he had set for our awakening.”

“We set that time,” interrupted Bickley.

===Chinese===

她手里捧着那两块金属板——我曾在睡者奥罗的棺里见过。她把它们交给他，然后退到他的听觉之外——倘若真有什么能逃出他的听觉的话，这一点我不敢断言——开始同我说话。我立刻注意到，在离去的这几个小时里，她对奥罗费纳语的掌握似乎大有长进，仿佛从某眼隐秘的记忆之泉痛饮了一番。此刻她说得流利，同奥罗训斥巫师时一样，虽然她用的许多词我不认识，她语言的总的格局也显得古奥，譬如像斯宾塞的英语之比于现代英语。不过，一旦见我不懂，她便停下，把句子换一种说法，直到我领会她的意思。下面是她所说的大意。

"你们平安了，"她开口，先瞥一眼躺在石头上的棕榈绳，再看看我的手腕——其中一只割破了。

"是的，伊娃夫人，多亏您的父亲。"

"你该说多亏我。我父亲想着别的事情，我却在想你们这些外来人；从我所在的地方，我看见那些恶人前来杀你们。"

"哦！我猜是在山顶上。"

她摇头微笑，却不肯再解释，除非她接着的话算解释。那话是：

"只要我愿意，我能不用眼睛看东西。"这句话叫在旁听的比克利嘀咕起来：

"不可能！她这话到底是什么意思？也许是心灵感应。"

"我看见了，"她接着说，"便告诉了主，我的父亲。他出来了。他把他们杀了吗？我没有看，不愿知道。"

"是的。他们躺在湖里，只有三个例外，他打发他们当传话人去了。"

"我想是这样。死是可怕的，哦，汉弗莱，但它是一柄剑，掌权的人必须用它去击打邪恶与野蛮。"

我不想深究这个题目，便问她她父亲拿那两块金属板做什么。

"他读星辰，"她答，"要弄清我们睡了多久。睡前他把星象绘了两张：一张是当时的样子，一张是到他为我们定下的觉醒之时的样子。"

"那个时刻是我们定的，"比克利插嘴。

===Original===

“Not so, O Bickley,” she answered, smiling again. “In the divine Oro’s head was the time set. You were the hand that executed his decree.”

When Bickley heard this I really thought he would have burst. However, he controlled himself nobly, being anxious to hear the end of this mysterious fib.

Oro’s chart of the stars made by him about 250,000 years ago.

Key to Oro’s chart of ancient times showing our names for the stars.

Oro’s chart of the stars as he calculated they would appear at his awakening.

Key to Oro’s chart showing our names and present-day positions for the stars.

“How long was the time that the lord Oro set apart for sleep?” I asked.

She paused as though puzzled to find words to express her meaning, then held up her hands and said:

“Ten,” nodding at her fingers. By second thoughts she took Bickley’s hands, not mine, and counted his ten fingers.

“Ten years,” said Bickley. “Well, of course, it is impossible, but perhaps﻿—” and he paused.

“Ten tens,” she went on with a deepening smile, “one hundred.”

“O!” said Bickley.

“Ten hundreds, one thousand.”

“I say!” said Bickley.

“Ten times ten thousand, one hundred thousand.”

Bickley became silent.

“Twice one hundred thousand and half a hundred thousand, two hundred and fifty thousand years. *That* was the space of time which the lord Oro, my father, set for our sleep. Whether it has been fulfilled he will know presently when he has read the book of the stars and made comparison of it with what he wrote before we laid us down to rest,” and she pointed to the metal plates which the Ancient was studying.

Bickley walked away, making sounds as though he were going to be ill and looking so absurd in his indignation that I nearly laughed. The Lady Yva actually did laugh, and very musical was that laugh.

“He does not believe,” she said. “He is so clever he knows everything. But two hundred and fifty thousand years ago we should have thought him quite stupid. Then we could read the stars and calculate their movements forever.”

“So can we,” I answered, rather nettled.

“I am glad, O Humphrey, since you will be able to show my father if in one of them he is wrong.”

===Chinese===

"不是的，哦，比克利，"她答，又微微一笑。"神圣的奥罗的头脑里定下那个时刻。你们不过是执行祂旨意的手。"

比克利听了这话，我真以为他要气炸了。然而他高贵地克制了自己，急于听这场神秘大谎的下文。

奥罗约二十五万年前所绘的星图。

奥罗古代星图对照表，标注今日我们对各星的命名。

奥罗据其推算所绘的、他苏醒之时的星象图。

奥罗星图对照表，标注今日各星之名与位置。

"奥罗主人为睡眠定下的时间是多久？"我问。

她停了停，仿佛苦于找不到表达意思的词，随后举起双手说：

"十，"朝着自己的手指点头。想了想，她拿起比克利的手——不是我的——数他的十根手指。

"十年，"比克利说。"嗯，当然，这不可能，但也许——"他顿住了。

"十的十倍，"她笑意渐深地接着说，"一百。"

"哦！"比克利说。

"十的百倍，一千。"

"我说！"比克利说。

"十乘一万，十万。"

比克利哑了。

"两个十万，再加半个十万，二十五万年。*那*就是奥罗主人为我们的睡眠定下的时限。它是否已满，他读完星辰之书、拿它同我们安睡之前所记下的对照，立刻便知。"她指着古代人正在研读的金属板。

比克利走开了，嘴里发出仿佛要呕吐的声音，那副愤懑的模样滑稽得很，我几乎笑出来。伊娃夫人倒真的笑了，笑声极是悦耳。

"他不信，"她说。"他太聪明，什么都知道。可是在二十五万年前，我们会觉得他相当蠢。那时我们能读星辰，能算它们的运行，直到永远。"

"我们也能，"我答，颇有些被刺痛。

"我很高兴，哦，汉弗莱，这样你便能在某一处他错了的时候指给他看。"

===Original===

Secretly I hoped that this task would not be laid on me. Indeed, I thought it well to change the subject for the edification of Bickley who had recovered and was drawn back by his eager curiosity. Just then, too, Bastin joined us, happy in his regained boots.

“You tell us, Lady Yva,” I said, “that you slept, or should have slept for two hundred and fifty thousand years.” Here Bastin opened his eyes. “If that was so, where was your mind all this time?”

“If by my mind you mean spirit, O Humphrey, I have to answer that at present I do not know for certain. I think, however, that it dwelt elsewhere, perhaps in other bodies on the earth, or some different earth. At least, I know that my heart is very full of memories which as yet I cannot unroll and read.”

“Great heavens, this is madness!” said Bickley.

“In the great heavens,” she answered slowly, “there are many things which you, poor man, would think to be madness, but yet are truth and perfect wisdom. These things, or some of them, soon I shall hope to show you.”

“Do if you can,” said Bickley.

“Why not?” interrupted Bastin. “I think the lady’s remarks quite reasonable. It seems to me highly improbable if really she has slept for two hundred and fifty thousand years, which, of course, I can’t decide, that an immortal spirit would be allowed to remain idle for so long. That would be wallowing in a bed of idleness and shirking its duty which is to do its work. Also, as she tells you, Bickley, you are not half so clever as you think you are in your silly scepticism, and I have no doubt that there are many things in other worlds which would expose your ignorance, if only you could see them.”

At this moment Oro turned and called his daughter. She went at once, saying:

“Come, strangers, and you shall learn.”

So we followed her.

“Daughter,” he said, speaking in Orofenan, I think that we might understand, “ask these strangers to bring one of those lamps of theirs that by the light of it I may study these writings.”

===Chinese===

我心里暗暗盼望这差事别落到我头上。的确，我觉得最好换个题目，也好开导开导比克利——他已经缓过劲来，叫强烈的好奇心又拉回来了。恰在此时，巴斯廷也凑了过来，为失而复得的靴子欢喜。

"伊娃夫人，您告诉我们，"我说，"你们睡了，或者说本该睡上二十五万年。"巴斯廷听到这里睁大了眼睛。"倘若果真如此，这么久的时间里，你们的心智在哪里？"

"倘若您说的心智是指灵，哦，汉弗莱，我只好答道：眼下我不能确知。不过我想，它寓居在别处，也许在世上别的身体里，或在某个不同的世界里。至少，我知道我的心盛满了记忆，只是还展不开、读不成。"

"天哪，这是疯话！"比克利说。

"在那诸天之上，"她缓缓答道，"有许多东西，您这可怜的人会以为是疯话，却偏偏是真理和圆满的智慧。这些东西，或其中一些，我很快希望能指给您看。"

"能指就指，"比克利说。

"为什么不能？"巴斯廷插话。"我以为夫人的话入情入理。依我看，倘若她真的睡了二十五万年——这我当然无从裁断——一个不朽的灵竟被容许闲置这么久，倒是极不可能的。那等于在怠惰的床上打滚，逃避它做工的本分。而且，正如她告诉你的，比克利，你那愚蠢的怀疑主义远没有你自己想的那么聪明；别的世界里有许多东西，单看你能不能看见，就足以暴露你的无知。"

这时奥罗转过身，唤他的女儿。她立刻过去，说：

"来吧，外来人，你们便可以知道了。"

我们跟着她走。

"女儿，"他用奥罗费纳语说——我想他是说给我们懂的，"请这些外来人取一盏他们的灯来，好借着灯光研读这些文字。"

===Original===

“Perhaps this may serve,” said Bickley, suddenly producing an electric torch from his pocket and flashing it into his face. It was his form of repartee for all he had suffered at the hands of this incomprehensible pair. Let me say at once that it was singularly successful. Perhaps the wisdom of the ages in which Oro flourished had overlooked so small a matter as electric torches, or perhaps he did not expect to meet with them in these degenerate days. At any rate for the first and last time in my intercourse with him I saw the god, or lord﻿—the native word bears either meaning﻿—Oro genuinely astonished. He started and stepped back, and for a moment or two seemed a little frightened. Then muttering something as to the cleverness of this light-producing instrument, he motioned to his daughter to take it from Bickley and hold it in a certain position. She obeyed, and in its illumination he began to study the engraved plates, holding one of them in either hand.

After a while he gave me one of the plates to hold, and with his disengaged hand pointed successively to the constellation of Orion, to the stars Castor, Pollux, Aldebaran, Rigel, the Pleiades, Sirius and others which with my very limited knowledge I could not recognise offhand. Then on the plate which I held, he showed us those same stars and constellations, checking them one by one.

Then he remarked very quietly that all was in order, and handing the plate he held to Yva, said:

===Chinese===

"也许这个顶用，"比克利说着，突然从口袋里掏出一支手电筒，往自己脸上晃。这是他吃了这对莫名其妙的人的亏之后的一种回敬。我不妨立刻说：这回敬妙不可言。也许奥罗盛行的那个伟大时代的智慧，漏掉了手电筒这样一桩小事；也许他不曾料到在这个堕落的年代里会同它相逢。总之，在同他交往的始终，我有幸头一回也是末一回看见这位神或主——土语的词两义兼收——奥罗真正地吃了一惊。他一震，退了一步，有一两分钟似乎有点害怕。随后他咕哝了一句这发光的器具如何聪明之类的话，示意女儿从比克利手里接过它，举到一个特定的位置。她遵命，他便在它的光照下开始研读那些镌刻的板子，一手执一块。

过了一阵，他把其中一块递给我举着，用空出来的那只手，依次指向猎户座，指向北河二、北河三、毕宿五、参宿七、昴星团、天狼星和别的几处星——凭我那点可怜的知识，我一时认不出。随后，在我举着的那块板上，他把同样的星和星座一一指给我们，逐一核对。

然后他极为平静地宣称：一切无误；随后把他手里那块递给伊娃，说道：

===Original===

“The calculations made so long ago are correct, nor have the stars varied in their proper motions during what is after all but an hour of time. If you, Stranger, who, I understand, are named Humphrey, should be, as I gather, a heaven-master, naturally you will ask me how I could fix an exact date by the stars without an error of, let us say, from five to ten thousand years. I answer you that by the proper motion of the stars alone it would have been difficult. Therefore I remember that in order to be exact, I calculated the future conjunctions of those two planets,” and he pointed to Saturn and Jupiter. “Finding that one of these occurred near yonder star,” and he indicated the bright orb, Spica, “at a certain time, I determined that then I would awake. Behold! There are the stars as I engraved them from my foreknowledge, upon this chart, and there those two great planets hang in conjunction. Daughter Yva, my wisdom has not failed me. This world of ours has travelled round the sun neither less nor more than two hundred and fifty thousand times since we laid ourselves down to sleep. It is written here, and yonder,” and he pointed, first to the engraved plates and then to the vast expanse of the starlit heavens.

Awe fell on me; I think that even Bickley and Bastin were awed, at any rate for the moment. It was a terrible thing to look on a being, to all appearance more or less human, who alleged that he had been asleep for two hundred and fifty thousand years, and proceeded to prove it by certain ancient star charts. Of course at the time I could not check those charts, lacking the necessary knowledge, but I have done so since and found that they are quite accurate. However this made no difference, since the circumstances and something in his manner convinced me that he spoke the absolute truth.

He and his daughter had been asleep for two hundred and fifty thousand years. Oh! Heavens, *for two hundred and fifty thousand years*!

===Chinese===

"远古所作的推算是正确的，而在那不过是时间里的一个钟头的历程中，星辰在其自行中也未曾偏移。倘若您，外来人——我听说您名叫汉弗莱——正如我领会的，是一位通晓上天的人，您自然会问我：单凭星辰，怎能定下一个准确的日子，而不出譬如五千到一万年的误差。我回答您：单凭星辰的自行，确乎困难。所以我记得，为了精确，我推算了那两颗行星未来的会合，"他指向土星和木星。"查出其中一次会合将在某个时刻临近那颗星，"他指了指那颗明亮的星宿，角宿一，"我便定下届时醒来。看哪！板上刻着的，是我凭先见镌下的星辰；而天上悬着的，是那两颗巨行星的会合。女儿伊娃，我的智慧不曾骗我。自我们安睡以来，我们这个世界绕着太阳转的圈数，不多不少，正是二十五万次。这里写着它，那边也写着它，"他先指镌刻的板子，再指繁星满布的浩渺天穹。

敬畏落在我身上；我想，连比克利和巴斯廷也敬畏了，至少在那一刻。看着一个外表多少同我们相似的存在者，自称沉睡了二十五万年，并拿几张古老的星图加以证明，这是一件可怕的事。当然，当时我无法核验那些星图，缺少必要的知识；但后来我核验过，发现它们相当准确。然而这已无关紧要，因为种种情状，加上他言谈中的某种气质，已使我确信他说的绝对是真话。

他同他的女儿沉睡了二十五万年。哦！天哪，*沉睡了二十五万年*！

## XIII / 第十三章
===Original===

Oro Speaks and Bastin Argues

The reader of what I have written, should there ever be such a person, may find the record marvelous, and therefore rashly conclude that because it is beyond experience, it could not be. It is not a wise deduction, as I think Bickley would admit today, because without doubt many things are which surpass our extremely limited experience. However, those who draw the veil from the Unknown and reveal the New, must expect incredulity, and accept it without grumbling. Was that not the fate, for instance, of those who in the Middle Ages, a few hundred years ago, discovered, or rather rediscovered the mighty movements of those constellations which served Oro for an almanac?

But the point I want to make is that if the sceptic plays a Bickleyan part as regards what has been written, it seems probable that his attitude will be accentuated as regards that which it still remains for me to write. If so, I cannot help it, and must decline entirely to water down or doctor facts and thus pander to his prejudice and ignorance. For my part I cannot attempt to explain these occurrences; I only know that they happened and that I set down what I saw, heard and felt, neither more nor less.

Immediately after Oro had triumphantly vindicated his stellar calculations he turned and departed into the cave, followed by his daughter, waving to us to remain where we were. As she passed us, however, the Glittering Lady whispered﻿—this time to Bastin﻿—that he would see them again in a few hours, adding:

“We have much to learn and I hope that then you who, I understand, are a priest, will begin to teach us of your religion and other matters.”

Bastin was so astonished that he could make no reply, but when they had gone he said:

“Which of you told her that I was a priest?”

We shook our heads for neither of us could remember having done so.

“Well, I did not,” continued Bastin, “since at present I have found no opportunity of saying a word in season. So I suppose she must have gathered it from my attire, though as a matter of fact I haven’t been wearing a collar, and those men who wanted to cook me, pulled off my white tie and I didn’t think it worth while dirtying a clean one.”

===Chinese===

奥罗开言，巴斯廷辩理

读到我这篇文字的人——倘若真会有这样一个人——或许会觉得记录神奇，从而轻率地下结论说：既然超出经验，它便不可能。这不是一个明智的推论——我想比克利今天也会承认——因为毫无疑问，许多事物确乎存在，只是超出我们那极其有限的经验罢了。然而，揭开"未知"之幕、昭示"新事物"的人，必须料到不信，并且不抱怨地领受它。譬如，中世纪——几百年前——那些发现，或不如说重新发现供奥罗作历书之用的那些星座的宏大运行的人，岂不正是这个命运？

但我要提的一点是：倘若怀疑派对我已写下的部分摆出一副比克利式的姿态，那么，对我尚待写下的部分，他的态度多半还会变本加厉。倘若如此，我无能为力，只能断然拒绝稀释或篡改事实，从而迁就他的偏见与无知。至于我自己，我无意解释这些事；我只知道它们发生了，我只写下我看见、听见、感到的，不多也不少。

奥罗刚凯旋似地证实了他的星辰推算，便转身进洞，他女儿随后，挥手叫我们留在原地。然而闪光夫人经过我们身边时，低声——这一回是对巴斯廷——说几小时后他还会见到他们，又补一句：

"我们有许多要学的；我希望到那时，您——我听说您是一位祭司——开始教导我们你们的宗教和其他事情。"

巴斯廷惊得说不出话；等他们走远了，他说：

"你们谁告诉她我是祭司的？"

我们摇头，谁也不记得做过。

"嗯，我没有说过，"巴斯廷接着说，"因为眼下我还没找到机会在合宜的时候说上一句话。所以我想她必定是从我的衣着看出来的；虽然事实上我没戴领子，那些要煮我的人扯走了我的白领带，我也犯不着弄脏一条干净的。"

===Original===

“If,” said Bickley, “you imagine that you look like the minister of any religion ancient or modern in a grubby flannel shirt, a battered sun-helmet, a torn green and white umbrella and a pair of ragged duck trousers, you are mistaken, Bastin, that is all.”

“I admit that the costume is not appropriate, Bickley, but how otherwise could she have learned the truth?”

“These people seem to have ways of learning a good many things. But in your case, Bastin, the cause is clear enough. You have been walking about with the head of that idol and always keep it close to you. No doubt they believe that you are a priest of the worship of the god of the Grove﻿—Baal, you know, or something of that sort.”

When he heard this Bastin’s face became a perfect picture. Never before did I see it so full of horror struggling with indignation.

“I must undeceive them without a moment’s delay,” he said, and was starting for the cave when we caught his arms and held him.

“Better wait till they come back, old fellow,” I said, laughing. “If you disobey that Lord Oro you may meet with another experience in the sacrifice line.”

“Perhaps you are right, Arbuthnot. I will occupy the interval in preparing a suitable address.”

“Much better occupy it in preparing breakfast,” said Bickley. “I have always noticed that you are at your best extempore.”

In the end he did prepare breakfast though in a distrait fashion; indeed I found him beginning to make tea in the frying-pan. Bastin felt that his opportunity had arrived, and was making ready to rise to the occasion.

===Chinese===

"倘若你以为，"比克利说，"穿着一件脏法兰西绒衬衫、一顶破遮阳盔、一把撕烂的绿白伞、一条破帆布裤，看上去就像古今任何宗教的牧师，那你错了，巴斯廷，如此而已。"

"我承认这身行头不合适，比克利，可她还能从哪里学到真相呢？"

"这些人似乎有许多学事情的门道。至于你，巴斯廷，缘由再明白不过。你一直捧着那颗神像的头走来走去，总把它带在身边。毫无疑问，他们认定你是林中之神那一门敬拜的祭司——你知道，巴力神，或类似的那一套。"

听到这话，巴斯廷的脸成了一幅绝妙的图画。我从没见过它那样满盈着恐怖与义愤的交战。

"我必须毫不迟延地纠正他们的误会，"他说着就要往洞穴去，我们拉住他的胳膊。

"最好等他们回来，老伙计，"我笑着说。"你若违了那位主奥罗的命，恐怕又要在祭祀那一行里领受一回新的经历。"

"也许你说得对，阿巴思诺特。这一段时间，我就用来准备一篇合宜的讲词。"

"用来准备早饭好得多，"比克利说。"我一向注意到，你脱稿发挥时最出色。"

末了他果然去准备了早饭，虽然神思恍惚；我发现他开始在煎锅里煮茶。巴斯廷觉得他的时机到了，正在准备大显身手。

===Original===

Also we felt, all three of us, that we were extremely shabby-looking objects, and though none of us said so, each did his best to improve his personal appearance. First of all Bickley cut Bastin’s and my hair, after which I did him the same service. Then Bickley who was normally clean shaven, set to work to remove a beard of about a week’s growth, and I who wore one of the pointed variety, trimmed up mine as best I could with the help of a hand-glass. Bastin, too, performed on his which was of the square and rather ragged type, wisely rejecting Bickley’s advice to shave it off altogether, offered, I felt convinced, because he felt that the result on Bastin would be too hideous for words. After this we cut our nails, cleaned our teeth and bathed; I even caught Bickley applying hair tonic from his dressing case in secret, behind a projecting rock, and borrowed some myself. He gave it me on condition that I did not mention its existence to Bastin who, he remarked, would certainly use the lot and make himself smell horrible.

Next we found clean ducks among our store of spare clothes, for the Orofenans had brought these with our other possessions, and put them on, even adding silk cumberbunds and neckties. My tie I fastened with a pin that I had obtained in Egypt. It was a tiny gold statuette of very fine and early workmanship, of the god Osiris, wearing the crown of the Upper Land with the uraeus crest, and holding in his hands, which projected from the mummy wrappings, the emblems of the crook, the scourge and the *crux ansata*, or Sign of Life.

Bastin, for his part, arrayed himself in full clerical costume, black coat and trousers, white tie and stickup clergyman’s collar which, as he remarked, made him feel extremely hot in that climate, and were unsuitable to domestic duties, such as washing-up. I offered to hold his coat while he did this office and told him he looked very nice indeed.

“Beautiful!” remarked Bickley, “but why don’t you put on your surplice and biretta?” (Being very High-Church Bastin did wear a biretta on festival Sundays at home.) “There would be no mistake about you then.”

“I do not think it would be suitable,” replied Bastin whose sense of humour was undeveloped. “There is no service to be performed at present and no church, though perhaps that cave﻿—” and he stopped.

===Chinese===

我们三个也都觉得自己这副样子寒酸之极，虽然谁也没说出口，各自都尽力整饰仪表。首先，比克利给巴斯廷和我理了发，随后我替他如法炮制。然后，一向刮得干干净净的比克利动手刮掉约莫一星期份的胡须；蓄着尖式胡子的我，借一面手镜尽力修整；巴斯廷那把方而蓬乱的，也修剪了一通，聪明地拒绝了比克利叫他全部剃光的建议——我确信那建议的用意，是料定剃光之后巴斯廷的模样会丑得没法说。这之后，我们剪了指甲，刷了牙，洗了澡；我甚至逮住比克利在一块凸岩后面偷偷施用发油——从他的梳妆箱里——自己也借了些。他给了我，条件是我不得向巴斯廷提它的存在；他说，巴斯廷必定会把一瓶用个精光，把自己熏得臭不可闻。

接着，我们在备用的衣物堆里找到干净的帆布裤——奥罗费纳人连同其余物品一并送来的——穿上，甚至添上绸腰巾和领带。我的领带用一枚在埃及得来的别针别住。那是一尊极小极精、工艺古老的金质小像，是神奥西里斯，戴着上埃及的王冠和圣蛇冠饰，从木乃伊裹布里伸出的双手握着曲杖、连枷和"安卡"——生命之符——的形物。

巴斯廷则把自己装扮齐全套的教士行头：黑外套黑裤，白领带和硬立领——照他的说法，在这气候里热得要命，而且不适合洗碗这类家务。我主动在他行礼时替他拿着外套，还告诉他他当真漂亮极了。

"漂亮！"比克利评道，"可你为什么不穿上你的白罩袍和四角帽？"（巴斯廷教派极高，在家逢节期的礼拜日是戴四角帽的。）"那就断不会有人认错了。"

"我想那不合适，"幽默感发育不良的巴斯廷答道。"眼下没有仪式要行，也没有教堂，虽然也许那个洞——"他停住了。

===Original===

When we had finished these vain adornments and Bastin had put away the things and tidied up, we sat down, rather at a loose end. We should have liked to walk but refrained from doing so for fear lest we might dirty our clean clothes. So we just sat and thought. At least Bickley thought, and so did I for a while until I gave it up. What was the use of thinking, seeing that we were face to face with circumstances which baffled reason and beggared all recorded human experience? What Bastin did I am sure I do not know, but I think from the expression of his countenance that he was engaged in composing sermons for the benefit of Oro and the Glittering Lady.

One diversion we did have. About eleven o’clock a canoe came from the main island laden with provisions and paddled by Marama and two of his people. We seized our weapons, remembering our experiences of the night, but Marama waved a bough in token of peace. So, carrying our revolvers, we went to the rock edge to meet him. He crept ashore and, chief though he was, prostrated himself upon his face before us, which told me that he had heard of the fate of the sorcerers. His apologies were abject. He explained that he had no part in the outrage of the attack, and besought us to intercede on behalf of him and his people with the awakened god of the Mountain whom he looked for with a terrified air.

We consoled him as well as we could, and told him that he had best be gone before the god of the Mountain appeared, and perhaps treated him as he had done the sorcerers. In his name, however, we commanded Marama to bring materials and build us a proper house upon the rock, also to be sure to keep up a regular and ample supply of provisions. If he did these things, and anything else we might from time to time command, we said that perhaps his life and those of his people would be spared. This, however, after the evil behaviour of some of them of course we could not guarantee.

===Chinese===

这些虚饰的打扮做完，巴斯廷把东西收好、收拾停当，我们便坐下来，颇有些无所事事。我们本想散步，又忍住了，怕弄脏干净的衣裳。于是我们只是坐着想。至少比克利在想，我也想了一阵，随后放弃了。想有什么用？既然我们面对的是挫败理性、穷尽人类一切记载的经验的境况？巴斯廷在做什么我不知道，但从他的神色看，我想他正为开导奥罗和闪光夫人拟着布道词。

有一桩消遣。十一点左右，一只独木舟从主岛来，载着给养，划手是马拉玛和他的两个族人。我们抓起武器——记着昨夜的经历——马拉玛却挥着树枝表示和平。于是我们带着左轮走到石边迎他。他爬上岸，虽是酋长，却在我们面前五体投地，这告诉我他已听说了巫师们的下场。他的道歉卑屈之至。他解释说，那次袭击的暴行他没有份，并恳求我们替他和他的族人，向那位苏醒的山神说情——他提起山神时一脸惊惶。

我们尽力安慰他，告诉他最好趁山神还没露面赶紧走，免得山神拿他像处置巫师那样处置他。不过我们以山神的名义命令马拉玛：备料，在石上给我们盖一座像样的房子；还要保证给养按时、充足。倘若这些——以及我们不时吩咐的别的——都照办，我们说，他和他族人的性命也许可得保全。至于他们中某些人的恶行，自然我们不作担保。

===Original===

Marama departed so thoroughly frightened that he even forgot to make any inquiries as to who this god of the Mountain might be, or where he came from, or whither he was going. Of course, the place had been sacred among his people from the beginning, whenever that may have been, but that its sacredness should materialise into an active god who brought sorcerers of the highest reputation to a most unpleasant end, just because they wished to translate their preaching into practice, was another matter. It was not to be explained even by the fact of which he himself had informed me, that during the dreadful storm of some months before, the cave mouth which previously was not visible on the volcano, had suddenly been lifted up above the level of the Rock of Offerings, although, of course, all religious and instructed persons would have expected something peculiar to happen after this event.

Such I knew were his thoughts, but, as I have said, he was too frightened and too hurried to express them in questions that I should have found it extremely difficult to answer. As it was he departed quite uncertain as to whether one of us was not the real “god of the Mountain,” who had power to bring hideous death upon his molesters. After all, what had he to go on to the contrary, except the word of three priests who were so terrified that they could give no coherent account of what had happened? Of these events, it was true, there was evidence in the twisted carcass of their lamented high sorcerer, and, for the matter of that, of certain corpses which he had seen, that lay in shallow water at the bottom of the lake. Beyond all was vague, and in his heart I am sure that Marama believed that Bastin was the real “god of the Mountain.” Naturally, he would desire to work vengeance on those who tried to sacrifice and eat him. Moreover, had he not destroyed the image of the god of the Grove and borne away its head whence he had sucked magic and power?

Thus argued Marama, disbelieving the tale of the frightened sorcerers, for he admitted as much to me in after days.

===Chinese===

马拉玛走了，怕得厉害，甚至忘了打听这山神究竟是谁、从何而来、往何处去。当然，这个地方打从起初——无论那起初是几时——便在他的族人中是神圣的；但它的神圣竟会化作一位活动的神，把声名最隆的巫师送到极不愉快的下场，只因他们想把讲道变成行动，那便是另一回事了。这甚至不能用他自己告诉过我的那件事解释：几个月前那场可怕的风暴里，火山上原先看不见的洞口，忽然升到了献祭石的水面之上——虽然，一切虔敬而有见识的人自然早该料到，这桩事之后必有蹊跷。

我知道他心里这样想；但如我所说，他又怕又急，来不及把这些想法化成问题——那些问题我本会极难作答。事实是，他走时全然拿不准：我们中间是否就有一位是真"山神"，有权叫搅扰他的人死得惨不可言。毕竟，反驳这一点的证据有什么呢？除了三个吓破了胆、语无伦次的祭司的话？这些事倒是真有物证：他们哀悼的大巫师扭曲的尸身；还有，他亲眼见过的某些尸体，躺在湖底的浅水里。除此之外一切都是模糊的；我确信，在心底里马拉玛相信巴斯廷就是真"山神"。他要向那些想献祭吃他的人复仇，顺理成章。况且，他岂不毁了林中之神的像，还捧走了它的头——他从那颗头里吸足了法力和神通？

马拉玛是这样想的，他不信那三个吓坏的祭司的话；这一点他后来对我亲口承认。

===Original===

Marama departed in a great hurry, fearing lest the “god of the Mountain,” or Bastin, whose new and splendid garb he regarded with much suspicion, might develop some evil energy against him. Then we went back to our camp, leaving the industrious Bastin, animated by a suggestion from Bickley that the fruit and food might spoil if left in the sun, to carry it into the shade of the cave. Owing to the terrors of the Orofenans the supply was so large that to do this he must make no fewer than seven journeys, which he did with great good will since Bastin loved physical exercise. The result on his clerical garments, however, was disastrous. His white tie went awry, squashed fruit and roast pig gravy ran down his waistcoat and trousers, and his high collar melted into limp crinkles in the moisture engendered by the tropical heat. Only his long coat escaped, since that Bickley kindly carried for him.

It was just as he arrived with the seventh load in this extremely dishevelled condition that Oro and his daughter emerged from the cave. Indeed Bastin, who, being shortsighted, always wore spectacles that, owing to his heated state were covered with mist, not seeing that dignitary, dumped down the last basket on to his toes, exclaiming:

“There, you lazy beggar, I told you I would bring it all, and I have.”

In fact he thought he was addressing Bickley and playing off on him a troglodytic practical joke.

Oro, however, who at his age did not appreciate jokes, resented it and was about to do something unpleasant when with extraordinary tact his daughter remarked:

“Bastin the priest makes you offerings. Thank him, O Lord my father.”

So Oro thanked him, not too cordially for evidently he still had feeling in his toes, and once more Bastin escaped. Becoming aware of his error, he began to apologise profusely in English, while the lady Yva studied him carefully.

“Is that the costume of the priests of your religion, O Bastin?” she asked, surveying his dishevelled form. “If so, you were better without it.”

Then Bastin retired to straighten his tie, and grabbing his coat from Bickley, who handed it to him with a malicious smile, forced his perspiring arms into it in a peculiarly awkward and elephantine fashion.

Meanwhile Bickley and I produced two camp chairs which we had made ready, and on these the wondrous pair seated themselves side by side.

===Chinese===

马拉玛匆匆走了，生怕"山神"——或者巴斯廷，他那身崭新华服很叫马拉玛起疑——突然对他放出什么邪能。随后我们回到营地，把勤快的巴斯廷留下——比克利一句果子食品晒在太阳里要坏的话鼓动了他——让他把东西搬进洞穴的荫里。奥罗费纳人吓破了胆，给养多得他足足跑了七趟才搬完；他干得满心欢喜，因为巴斯廷酷爱体力运动。然而他的教士衣裳遭了殃。白领带歪了，压烂的果子汁和烤猪油顺着马甲和裤子往下淌，高温蒸出的湿气把硬立领泡成软塌塌的皱褶。只有长外套幸免，因为好心肠的比克利替他拿着。

第七趟东西搬完，他这副极其狼狈的样子刚到，奥罗和他的女儿恰恰从洞里出来。巴斯廷近视，一向戴眼镜，此刻眼镜上蒙着雾，没看清那位大人物，把最后一篮东西咚地卸在那位的脚趾上，嚷道：

"喏，你这懒骨头，我说过全给你搬来，我真的搬来了。"

事实上他以为自己在跟比克利说话，跟他开一个穴居人式的玩笑。

然而奥罗这把年纪不欣赏玩笑，动了气，正要发作点不愉快的事，他的女儿以非凡的圆滑插话：

"祭司巴斯廷向您献祭。谢他吧，哦，主，我的父亲。"

于是奥罗谢了他，不过谢得不算热络，因为他的脚趾显然还在疼。巴斯廷又逃过一劫。觉察到自己的错误，他开始用英语滔滔地道歉，伊娃夫人却把他细细端详。

"这就是你们宗教的祭司的服装吗，哦，巴斯廷？"她打量着他那副乱糟糟的模样问。"若是这样，您不如不穿。"

随后巴斯廷退下去整理领带，从比克利手里一把抓过外套——比克利微笑着递上，笑里带着恶意——以一种格外笨拙、大象式的姿态，把冒汗的胳膊塞进袖子。

同时比克利和我搬出两把早备好的野营椅，这对奇异的佳偶便并肩坐下了。

===Original===

“We have come to learn,” said Oro. “Teach!”

“Not so, Father,” interrupted Yva, who, I noted, was clothed in yet a third costume, though whence these came I could not imagine. “First I would ask a question. Whence are you, Strangers, and how came you here?”

“We are from the country called England and a great storm shipwrecked us here; that, I think, which raised the mouth of the cave above the level of this rock,” I answered.

“The time appointed having come when it should be raised,” said Oro as though to himself.

“Where is England?” asked Yva.

Now among the books we had with us was a pocket atlas, quite a good one of its sort. By way of answer I opened it at the map of the world and showed her England. Also I showed, to within a thousand miles or so, that spot on the earth’s surface where we spoke together.

The sight of this atlas excited the pair greatly. They had not the slightest difficulty in understanding everything about it and the shape of the world with its division into hemispheres seemed to be quite familiar to them. What appeared chiefly to interest them, and especially Oro, were the relative areas and positions of land and sea.

“Of this, Strangers,” he said, pointing to the map, “I shall have much to say to you when I have studied the pictures of your book and compared them with others of my own.”

“So he has got maps,” said Bickley in English, “as well as star charts. I wonder where he keeps them.”

“With his clothes, I expect,” suggested Bastin.

Meanwhile Oro had hidden the atlas in his ample robe and motioned to his daughter to proceed.

“Why do you come here from England so far away?” the Lady Yva asked, a question to which each of us had an answer.

“To see new countries,” I said.

“Because the cyclone brought us,” said Bickley.

“To convert the heathen to my own Christian religion,” said Bastin, which was not strictly true.

It was on this last reply that she fixed.

“What does your religion teach?” she asked.

“It teaches that those who accept it and obey its commands will live again after death forever in a better world where is neither sorrow nor sin,” he answered.

When he heard this saying I saw Oro start as though struck by a new thought and look at Bastin with a curious intentness.

“Who are the heathen?” Yva asked again after a pause, for she also seemed to be impressed.

===Chinese===

"我们是来求教的，"奥罗说。"教吧！"

"别急，父亲，"伊娃插话——我注意到她又换了第三套衣裳，至于这些衣裳从何而来，我想象不出。"我先问一句。外来人，你们从哪里来，怎样来的？"

"我们来自一个叫英格兰的国度，一场大风暴把我们抛在这里；就是那场风暴——我想——把洞口抬过了这块石头的水面，"我答。

"所定的时刻到了，它便该升起，"奥罗仿佛自语。

"英格兰在哪里？"伊娃问。

我们的书里有一本袖珍地图册，就其品类而言相当不错。作为回答，我翻开世界地图，指给她英格兰；又指了指——误差千把英里之内——我们此刻对话的地球上的这一点。

这本地图册叫他们大为兴奋。理解其中的种种、理解世界的形状和两半球的划分，他们毫无困难，仿佛早就烂熟。最叫他们发生兴趣的——尤其是奥罗——是陆地与海洋的相对面积与位置。

"关于这个，外来人，"他指着地图说，"等我研究了你们这本书的图画、把它们同我自己的另一些图对照之后，我要跟你们谈许多。"

"原来他藏了地图，"比克利用英语说，"除了星图还有地图。我倒想知道他放在哪里。"

"多半同他的衣裳放在一起，"巴斯廷猜道。

同时奥罗已把地图册藏进宽大的袍子，示意女儿继续。

"你们为什么大老远地从英格兰跑到这里来？"伊娃夫人问。这个问题我们各有一答。

"想看看新的国度，"我说。

"气旋把我们卷来的，"比克利说。

"为叫异教徒皈依我自己的基督教，"巴斯廷说——这话不完全属实。

她盯住了最后这一句。

"你们的宗教教什么？"她问。

"它教：凡接受它、遵行它诫命的人，死后将在一个更好的世界里永远复活，那里没有忧伤，也没有罪，"他答。

听见这话，我看见奥罗一震，仿佛被一个新念头击中，以一种奇异专注的目光望着巴斯廷。

"异教徒是谁？"伊娃又问——停了片刻，因为她似乎也受了触动。

===Original===

“All who do not agree with Bastin’s spiritual views,” answered Bickley.
