At the same moment, with a swift spring, Yva leapt immediately in front of the lens or window, so that the metallic shield with which she covered herself pressed against its substance.

Simultaneously Oro flung up his arms as though in horror.

Too late! The shutter fell and from behind it there sprang out a rush of living flame. It struck on Yva’s shield and expanded to right and left. The insulated shield and garments that she wore seemed to resist it. For a fraction of time she stood there like a glowing angel, wrapped in fire.

Then she was swept outwards and upwards and at a little distance dissolved like a ghost and vanished from our sight.

Yva was ashes! Yva was gone! The sacrifice was consummated!

---

===Chinese===

奥罗瞪着眼，瘦指头在杆上收紧，他的头发和长须仿佛在狂喜的兴奋里根根竖起。那魔物的紫穗轮缘早已罩过那片涂白的岩石；它碾磨的脚不过十码之遥。奥罗向伊娃又打了几个手势，伊娃在盾的荫蔽下又俯身做了点我看不清的事。随后，仿佛她的部分已经演完，她立起，把斗篷的灰兜帽拢住全脸，只留眼睛可见，朝我迈了一步，用我们教她的那种破碎的英语，向着我耳边唤道：

"汉弗莱，上帝祝福你！汉弗莱，我们很快再见。别忘了我！"

我还没来得及作答，她又退回去；下一瞬，奥罗把身子弯成两折，用一种狰狞的、倾尽全力的挣扎，向那杆子上压——从他张开的嘴我看得出——一面压一面喊。

同一瞬间，伊娃一个迅疾的飞跃，跳到透镜或窗的正前方，于是她用来掩护自己的那面金属盾，抵住了它的实体。

同时奥罗举起双臂，仿佛恐怖。

太迟了！闸落下来，闸后迸出一股活的火焰。它打在伊娃的盾上，向左右扩张。她那绝缘的盾和衣仿佛抵挡住了它。一瞬之间，她立在那里，像一位发光的天使，裹在火里。

随后她被荡出去、荡上去，在不远处像一缕幽灵般消散，从我们的视野里灭去。

伊娃成了灰！伊娃去了！牺牲成全了！

---

===Original===

And not in vain! Not in vain! On her poor breast she had received the full blast of that hellish lightning flash. Yet whilst destroying, it turned away from her, seeking the free paths of the air. So it came about that its obstructed strength struck the foot of the travelling gyroscope, diffused and did not suffice to thrust it that one necessary inch on which depended the fate of half the world, or missing it altogether, passed away on either side. Even so the huge, gleaming mountain rocked and trembled. Once, twice, thrice, it bowed itself towards us as though in majestic homage to greatness passed away. For a second, too, its course was checked, and at the check the earth quaked and trembled. Yes, then the world shook, and the blue globes of fire went out, while I was thrown to the ground.

When they returned again, the flaming monster was once more sailing majestically upon its way and *down the accustomed left-hand path*!

---

Indeed the sacrifice was not in vain. The world shook﻿—but Yva had saved the world!

===Chinese===

而且不是徒然！不是徒然！她可怜的胸口承受了那道地狱闪电的全力一击。然而它在毁灭她的同时折转了方向，去寻空气的自由之路。于是事情便这样成了：它被拦住的力量打在行进的回旋器的脚上，扩散开来，不足以把它推过那决定半个世界命运的一英寸，或者完全落空，从两边擦过去。即便这样，那座巨大而闪耀的山也摇晃、震颤起来。一次，两次，三次，它向我们这边俯下身去，仿佛对逝去的伟大行庄严的敬礼。有一秒钟，它的进程也被阻住了；在那一阻之下，大地震颤动摇。是的，那时世界摇晃了，蓝色的火球熄灭，我被摔在地上。

等它们重新亮起，那燃烧的魔物再一次庄严地航行在它的路上——*沿着惯行的左边的路*！

---

实在说，牺牲并非徒然。世界震动了——但伊娃救了世界！

## XXVI / 第二十六章
===Original===

Tommy

I lay still a while, on my back as I had fallen, and beneath the shield-like defence which Yva had given to me. Notwithstanding the fire-resisting, metalised stuff of which it was made, I noted that it was twisted and almost burnt through. Doubtless the stored-up electricity or earth magnetism, or whatever it may have been that had leapt out of that hole, being diffused by the resistance with which it was met, had grazed me with its outer edge, and had it not been for the shield and cloak, I also should have been burned up. I wished, oh! how I wished that it had been so. Then, by now all must have finished and I should have known the truth as to what awaits us beyond the change: sleep, or dreams, or perchance the fullest life. Also I should not have learned alone.

Lying there thus, idly, as though in a half-sleep, I felt Tommy licking my face, and throwing my arm about the poor little frightened beast, I watched the great world-balance as it retreated on its eternal journey. At one time its vast projecting rim had overshadowed us and almost seemed to touch the cliff of rock against which we leant. I remember that the effect of that shining arch a thousand feet or so above our heads was wonderful. It reminded me of a canopy of blackest thunder clouds supported upon a framework of wheeling rainbows, while beneath it all the children of the devil shouted together in joy. I noted this effect only a few seconds before Yva spoke to me and leapt into the path of the flash.

Now, however, it was far away, a mere flaming wheel that became gradually smaller, and its Satanic voices were growing faint. As I have said, I watched its disappearance idly, reflecting that I should never look upon its like again; also that it was something well worth going forth to see. Then I became aware that the humming, howling din had decreased sufficiently to enable me to hear human voices without effort. Bastin was addressing Bickley﻿—like myself they were both upon the ground.

“Her translation, as you may have noticed, Bickley, if you were not too frightened, was really very remarkable. No doubt it will have reminded you, as it did me, of that of Elijah. She had exactly the appearance of a person going up to Heaven in a vehicle of fire. The destination was certainly the same, and even the cloak she wore added a familiar touch and increased the similarity.”

===Chinese===

汤米

我仰面静静躺了一会儿——照我倒下的样子——在伊娃给我的那面盾似的护具底下。虽然它是防火的、金属化的料子，我注意到它已扭曲，几乎烧穿。无疑，从那洞里跃出的积储的电或地磁，或随便什么，被它遭遇的阻力扩散开，以外缘擦过了我；倘若没有盾和斗篷，我也被烧尽了。我但愿，哦！我多么但愿真是这样。那么，如今一切必已完成，我也该知道变化的彼岸等着我们的是什么真相：睡眠，或梦，或也许最完满的生命。我也不会独自知道。

这样躺在那里，懒懒地，仿佛半睡，我觉得汤米在舔我的脸；我伸出臂搂着那可怜的小小受惊的兽，望着那巨大的世界天平沿它永恒的旅程退去。有一时，它那突出的巨大轮缘罩在我们头上，几乎像要碰到我们倚着的石崖。我记得那道闪烁的拱——在我们头顶一千英尺上下——给我们的印象何等奇妙。它叫我想起一片最黑的雷雨云的天篷，架在旋转的彩虹的骨架上；而在这一切底下，魔鬼的儿女们齐声欢呼。我记得这印象，不过是在伊娃对我说话、跃入闪电的路径之前几秒钟。

可是此刻它已远去，一朵渐渐变小的燃烧的轮，它那撒旦似的声浪也渐渐微弱。我说过，我懒懒地望着它消失，想着我再不会看见它的同类；也想着那毕竟是一件值得专程一看的东西。随后我觉出那嗡嗡嚎叫的喧嚣已减到足以让我不费力地听见人的嗓音。巴斯廷在对比克利说话——他们同我一样都躺在地上。

"她的升天，照你也许注意到的，比克利——倘若你没吓得太厉害——实在非常非凡。无疑它会使你像我一样，想起以利亚。她的模样活脱是一个乘着火的车辇升天的人。目的地当然相同，连她披的斗篷也添了一笔熟悉的色彩，加深了那相似。

===Original===

“At any rate it did not fall upon you,” answered Bickley with something like a sob, in a voice of mingled awe and exasperation. “For goodness’ sake! Bastin, stop your Biblical parallels and let us adore, yes, let us adore the divinest creature that the earth has borne!”

Never have I loved Bickley more than when I heard him utter those words.

“ ‘Divinest’ is a large term, Bickley, and one to which I hesitate to subscribe, remembering as I do certain of the prophets and the Early Fathers with all their faults, not of course to mention the Apostles. But﻿—” here he paused, for suddenly all three of us became aware of Oro.

He also has been thrown to the ground by the strength of the prisoned forces which he gathered and loosed upon their unholy errand, but, as I rejoiced to observe, had suffered from them much more than ourselves. Doubtless this was owing to the fact that he had sprung forward in a last wild effort to save his daughter, or to prevent her from interfering with his experiment, I know not which. As a result his right cheek was much scorched, his right arm was withered and helpless, and his magnificent beard was half burnt off him. Further, very evidently he was suffering from severe shock, for he rocked upon his feet and shook like an aspen leaf. All this, however, did not interfere with the liveliness of his grief and rage.

There he stood, a towering shape, like a lightning-smitten statue, and cursed us, especially Bastin.

“My daughter has gone!” he cried, “burned up by the fiery power that is my servant. Nothing remains of her but dust, and, Priest, this is your doing. You poisoned her heart with your childish doctrines of mercy and sacrifice, and the rest, so that she threw herself into the path of the flash to save some miserable races that she had never even known.”

He paused exhausted, whereon Bastin answered him with spirit:

“Yes, Oro, she being a holy woman, has gone where *you* will never follow her. Also it is your own fault since you should have listened to her entreaties instead of boxing her ears like the brute you are.”

“My daughter is gone,” went on Oro, recovering his strength, “and my great designs are ruined. Yet only for a while,” he added, “for the world-balance will return again, if not till long after your lifespans are done.”

===Chinese===

"至少那火没落在你头上，"比克利答，声里像带着呜咽，是敬畏与恼怒的混合。"看在老天分上！巴斯廷，住口，别再引你的圣经类比，让我们敬拜吧，是的，让我们敬拜大地生过的最神圣的造物吧！"

我从不曾比听见比克利说出这些话时更爱他。

"'最神圣'是个大词，比克利，我不便贸然签署，记得某些先知和早期教父连同他们的种种过错——当然不必提使徒们。可是——"说到这里他停住，因为我们三个忽然都觉出了奥罗。

他也叫那被囚的力——他聚集又释放出去干那不圣洁差事的力——的冲击摔在地上，可叫我欢喜的是，他受的伤比我们重得多。无疑这缘于他在最后一阵狂乱里扑上前去，或想救他的女儿，或想拦她搅扰他的试验——哪一样我不知道。结果是他的右颊烧得厉害，右臂枯废无力，漂亮的胡须烧去了一半。再者，分明他受着剧烈的震撼，因为他在脚上摇晃，像一片杨树叶抖。然而这一切并不妨碍他的悲与怒的炽烈。

他站在那里，一个高耸的形体，像一座被雷击的石像，咒骂我们，尤其是巴斯廷。

"我的女儿去了！"他喊，"叫那做我仆役的火的权力烧尽了。剩下的只有灰，祭司，这是你干的。你拿你幼稚的仁慈与牺牲之类的教条毒了她的心，她便扑进闪电的路径里，去救几个她连认识也不认识的可怜种族。"

他力竭停住，巴斯廷便鼓起精神答他：

"是的，奥罗，她既然是个圣洁的女人，已去了*你*永远跟随不到的地方。这也是你自己的错，因为你本该听她的恳求，不该像你这蛮子那样打她的耳光。"

"我的女儿去了，"奥罗接着说，气力恢复着，"我的大计毁了。不过只是暂时的，"他补一句，"因为世界的天平还要回来，即便要等到你们的寿数用尽之后很久。

===Original===

“If you don’t doctor yourself, Lord Oro,” said Bickley, also rising, “I may tell you as one who understands such things, that most likely it will be after *your* lifespan is done also. Although their effect may be delayed, severe shocks from burns and over-excitement are apt to prove fatal to the aged.”

Oro snarled at him; no other word describes it.

“And there are other things, Physician,” he said, “which are apt to prove fatal to the young. At least now you will no longer deny my power.”

“I am not so sure,” answered Bickley, “since it seems that there is a greater Power, namely that of a woman’s love and sacrifice.”

“And a greater still,” interrupted Bastin, “which put those ideas into her head.”

“As for you, Humphrey,” went on Oro, “I rejoice to think that you at least have lost two things that man desires above all other things﻿—the woman you sought and the future kingship of the world.”

I stood up and faced him.

“The first I have gained, although how, you do not understand, Oro,” I answered. “And of the second, seeing that it would have come through you, on your conditions, I am indeed glad to be rid. I wish no power that springs from murder, and no gifts from one who answered his daughter’s prayer with blows.”

For a moment he seemed remorseful.

“She vexed me with her foolishness,” he said. Then his rage blazed up again:

“And it was you who taught it to her,” he went on. “You are guilty, all three of you, and therefore I am left with none to serve me in my age; therefore also my mighty schemes are overthrown.”

“Also, Oro, if you speak truth, therefore half the world is saved,” I added quietly, “and one has left it of whom it was unworthy.”

===Chinese===

"你若不给自己医治，奥罗主上，"比克利也站起来说，"我可以以懂这一行的人的身份告诉你：多半要等到*你*的寿数也用尽。虽然后果可以延迟，烧灼与过度激动引起的剧烈震撼，对老年人往往致命。"

奥罗朝他咆哮；再没有别的字形容得更贴切。

"还有些东西，医师，"他说，"往往证明对年轻人致命。至少，如今你不会再否认我的权力了。"

"我可不一定，"比克利答，"因为看来还有一股更大的权力，即一个女人的爱与牺牲。"

"还有更大的，"巴斯廷插话，"把那些念头放进她脑袋的。"

"至于你，汉弗莱，"奥罗接着说，"想到你至少失去了两样人最渴想的东西——你追寻的女人和将来大地的王位——我很欢喜。"

我立起身面对他。

"头一样我已经得着了，虽然以什么方式，你不懂，奥罗，"我答。"至于第二样，既然它要通过你、按你的条件来，我实在庆幸摆脱了它。我不愿要任何从谋杀里生出的权力，不愿要任何用一个巴掌回答他女儿祈祷的人的赏赐。"

有一刻他仿佛悔恨。

"她拿她的愚蠢烦我，"他说。随后他的怒火又蹿起来：

"教她的又是你，"他接着说。"你们有罪，三个都有；所以我老了，没有人侍奉；所以我的大计也被推翻了。"

"而且，奥罗，照你说的，半个世界得救了，"我平静地补一句，"还有一个人离开了它，它原配不上她。

===Original===

“You think that these civilisations of yours, as you are pleased to call them, are saved, do you?” he sneered. “Yet, even if Bickley were right and I should die and become powerless, I tell you that they are already damned. I have studied them in your books and seen them with my eyes, and I say that they are rotten before ever they are ripe, and that their end shall be the end of the Sons of Wisdom, to die for lack of increase. That is why I would have saved the East, because in it alone there is increase, and thence alone can rise the great last race of man which I would have given to your children for an heritage. Moreover, think not that you Westerners have done with wars. I tell you that they are but begun and that the sword shall eat you up, and what the sword spares class shall snatch from class in the struggle for supremacy and ease.”

Thus he spoke with extraordinary and concentrated bitterness that I confess would have frightened me, had I been capable of fear, which at the moment I was not. Who is afraid when he has lost all?

Nor was Bastin alarmed, if for other reasons.

“I think it right to tell you, Oro,” he said, “that the only future you need trouble about is your own. God Almighty will look after the western civilisations in whatever way He may think best, as you may remember He did just now. Only I am sure you won’t be here to see how it is done.”

Again fury blazed in Oro’s eyes.

“At least I will look after you, you half-bred dogs, who yap out ill-omened prophecies of death into my face. Since the three of you loved my daughter whom you brought to her doom, and were by her beloved, if differently, I think it best that you should follow on her road. How? That is the question? Shall I leave you to starve in these great caves?﻿—Nay, look not towards the road of escape which doubtless she pointed out to you, for, as Humphrey knows, I can travel swiftly and I will make sure that you find it blocked. Or shall I﻿—” and he glanced upwards at the great globes of wandering fire, as though he purposed to summon them to be our death, as doubtless he could have done.

===Chinese===

"你以为你们这些文明——随你们高兴这样称呼——得救了，是不是？"他讥道。"可是，即便比克利是对的、我会死而失去权力，我告诉你们：它们已经定了罪。我在你们的书里研究过它们，也亲眼看过它们；我说，它们未熟先烂，它们的结局便是智慧之子的结局：因不再生养而死。这就是为什么我本要救东方，因为唯独它还有生养，唯独从它能兴起人类最后的伟大种族——我本要把它赐给你的儿女作产业。况且，别以为你们西方人同战争已经两清。我告诉你们：战争不过才开始，刀剑要把你们吃尽，刀剑饶过的，阶级又要从阶级手里抢——在争夺统治与安逸的搏斗里。

他这样说，带着一种非常集中、非常沉痛的苦毒；我承认，倘若我还能害怕，这会叫我害怕，只是那一刻我不能。失去了全部的人，还怕什么？

巴斯廷也不惊惶，虽然缘由不同。

"我认为应该告诉你，奥罗，"他说，"你须费心的唯一将来是你自己的将来。全能的上帝会照祂以为最好的方式照管西方的文明，你也许记得祂方才就照管了一回。只是我确信你不会在这里看见它怎样照管。"

奥罗的眼里怒火又蹿。

"至少我要照管你们，你们这些杂种的狗，当着我的脸吠出不祥的死讯的预言。你们三个既爱我的女儿——你们把她送进她的毁灭——又蒙她所爱，虽然爱的不一样，我看你们最好顺着她的路跟了去。怎样跟？问题在这里。要不要我任你们在这大洞穴里饿死？——不，别朝逃走的路望，无疑是她指点你们的；照汉弗莱所知，我走得快，我必叫你们发现它被封死。要不要我——"他朝上瞥了一眼那几个游荡的火球，仿佛打算召它们来做我们的死，无疑他做得到。

===Original===

“I do not care what you do,” I answered wearily. “Only I would beg you to strike quickly. Yet for my friends I am sorry, since it was I who led them on this quest, and for you, too, Tommy,” I added, looking at the poor little hound. “You were foolish, Tommy,” I went on, “when you scented out that old tyrant in his coffin, at least for our own sake.”

Indeed the dog was terribly scared. He whined continually and from time to time ran a little way and then returned to us, suggesting that we should go from this horror-haunted spot. Lastly, as though he understood that it was Oro who kept us there, he went to him and jumping up, licked his hand in a beseeching fashion.

The superman looked at the dog and as he looked the rage went out of his face and was replaced by something resembling pity.

“I do not wish the beast to die,” he muttered to himself in low reflective tones, as though he thought aloud, “for of them all it alone liked and did not fear me. I might take it with me but still it would perish of grief in the loneliness of the caves. Moreover, she loved it whom I shall see no more; yes, Yva﻿—” as he spoke the name his voice broke a little. “Yet if I suffer them to escape they will tell my story to the world and make me a laughingstock. Well, if they do, what does it matter? None of those Western fools would believe it; thinking that they knew all; like Bickley they would mock and say that they were mad, or liars.”

Again Tommy licked his hand, but more confidently, as though instinct told him something of what was passing in Oro’s mind. I watched with an idle wonder, marvelling whether it were possible that this merciless being would after all spare us for the sake of the dog.

So, strange to say, it came about, for suddenly Oro looked up and said:

===Chinese===

"你做什么我都不在乎，"我疲惫地答。"只求你下手快些。只是我为我的朋友们难过，因为是我领他们走上这趟探寻的；也为你难过，汤米，"我望着那可怜的小狗补一句。"你糊涂，汤米，"我接着说，"你在棺材里嗅出那个老暴君的时候——至少为我们自己打算。"

实在说狗怕极了。它不停地呜咽，时时跑开一小段，又跑回我们这里，示意我们离开这闹鬼的可怕地方。末了，仿佛它明白是奥罗把我们扣在这里，它走到他跟前去，跳起来，哀求似的舔他的手。

超人望着狗；望着望着，他脸上的怒气退了，换上近似怜惜的东西。

"我不愿这畜生死，"他低声自语，声调低回而若有所思，仿佛自言自语，"因为它们中间唯独它喜欢我、不怕我。我可以带它走，可它在洞穴的寂寞里仍要悲伤而死。况且，她爱它，我再不能见的那一位；是的，伊娃——"说出这个名字，他的嗓音微微断了。然而倘若我容他们逃走，他们会把我的故事讲给全世界，把我做成笑柄。好吧，倘若他们讲，又有什么要紧？那些西方的蠢货没有一个会信；自以为无所不知；像比克利一样讥诮，说我们是疯子或骗子。"

汤米又舔他的手，这回更有信心，仿佛本能告诉它奥罗心里正在经过的某样东西。我懒懒地惊奇地望着，纳闷这位无情的存在者会不会毕竟为了狗的缘故饶我们。

果然，说来奇怪，事情这样来了，因为奥罗忽然抬头说：

===Original===

“Get you gone, and quickly, before my mood changes. The hound has saved you. For its sake I give you your lives, who otherwise should certainly have died. She who has gone pointed out to you, I doubt not, a road that runs to the upper air. I think that it is still open. Indeed,” he added, closing his eyes for a moment, “I see that it is still open, if long and difficult. Follow it, and should you win through, take your boat and sail away as swiftly as you can. Whether you die or live I care nothing, but my hands will be clean of your blood, although yours are stained with Yva’s. Begone! and my curse go with you.”

Without waiting for further words we went to fetch our lanterns, water-bottles and bag of food which we had laid down at a little distance. As we approached them I looked up and saw Oro standing some way off. The light from one of the blue globes of fire which passed close above his head, shone upon him and made him ghastly. Moreover, it seemed to me as though approaching death had written its name upon his malevolent countenance.

I turned my head away, for about his aspect in those sinister surroundings there was something horrible, something menacing and repellent to man and of him I wished to see no more. Nor indeed did I, for when I glanced in that direction again Oro was gone. I suppose that he had retreated into the shadows where no light played.

---

===Chinese===

"去，快去，趁我的心绪未变。这狗救了你们。为它的缘故，我把你们的性命给你们，不然你们必定死。去的那位，想必已指点你们一条通往上界空气的路。我想它还开着。实在说，"他闭眼片刻，补一句，"我看见它还开着，虽然又长又难。顺着它走；你们若走得出去，便乘你们的船，尽快离开。你们死也好活也好，我一概不问；只是你们血债的账上，我的手是干净的，虽然你们的手染着伊娃的血。去吧！我的咒诅随你们去。"

不等更多的话，我们便去取我们的灯笼、水壶和那一袋食物——我们在不远处放下的。走近它们时，我抬头，看见奥罗立在几步之外。一颗蓝火球从接近他头顶的地方经过，光照在他身上，叫他狰狞。而且，仿佛逼近的死已把它的名字写在他那副恶相上。

我扭过头去，因为在那阴森的四围里，他的仪表有种可怕的东西，有种威胁人、拒人于千里之外的东西；他的模样我一眼也不想再看。实在说我也不曾再看，因为我朝那方向再瞥时，奥罗已不见了。想必他退进了没有光玩耍的暗影里。

---

===Original===

We gathered up our gear, and while the others were relighting the lanterns, I walked a few paces forward to the spot where Yva had been dissolved in the devouring fire. Something caught my eye upon the rocky floor. I picked it up. It was the ring, or rather the remains of the ring that I had given her on that night when we declared our love amidst the ruins by the crater lake. She had never worn it on her hand but for her own reasons, as she told me, suspended it upon her breast beneath her robe. It was an ancient ring that I had bought in Egypt, fashioned of gold in which was set a very hard basalt or other black stone. On this was engraved the ankh or looped cross, which was the Egyptian symbol of Life, and round it a snake, the symbol of Eternity. The gold was for the most part melted, but the stone, being so hard and protected by the shield and asbestos cloak, for such I suppose it was, had resisted the fury of the flash. Only now it was white instead of black, like a burnt onyx that had known the funeral pyre. Indeed, perhaps it was an onyx. I kissed it and hid it away, for it seemed to me to convey a greeting and with it a promise.

Then we started, a very sad and dejected trio. Leaving with a shudder that vast place where the blue lights played eternally, we came to the shaft up and down which the travelling stone pursued its endless path, and saw it arrive and depart again.

“I wonder he did not send us that way,” said Bickley, pointing to it.

“I am sure I am very glad it never occurred to him,” answered Bastin, “for I am certain that we could not have made the journey again without our guide, Yva.”

I looked at him and he ceased. Somehow I could not bear, as yet, to hear her beloved name spoken by other lips.

Then we entered the passage that she pointed out to us, and began a most terrible journey which, so far as we could judge, for we lost any exact count of time, took us about sixty hours. The road, it is true, was smooth and unblocked, but the ascent was fearfully steep and slippery; so much so that often we were obliged to pull each other up it and lie down to rest.

===Chinese===

我们收拾好行装；别人重新点灯的时候，我向前走了几步，到伊娃在吞噬的火里消散的地方。石地上有什么东西映入我眼。我拾起来。是那枚戒指，或不如说那戒指的残骸——我们在火山湖畔的废墟里互诉爱情的那一夜给她的。她从不曾把它戴在手上，却出于她自己的缘由——照她告诉我的——把它悬在胸口、藏在袍内。那是一枚古戒指，我在埃及买的，金托上嵌着极硬的玄武岩或别的黑石。石上镌着安卡，即环形的十字，埃及的生命之符；四周是一条蛇，永恒之符。金子大半熔了，可那块石头，因为那样硬，又受着盾与石棉斗篷——我想它是石棉——的保护，顶住了闪电的狂怒。只是它如今是白的，不是黑的，像一块经过了火葬堆的烧过的缟玛瑙。实在说，它或许本就是缟玛瑙。我吻了它，把它藏起，因为我觉得它传达着一声问候，连同问候里的一句许诺。

随后我们出发了，一支非常悲哀沮丧的三人队伍。带着一阵战栗离开那蓝光永在玩耍的浩渺之地，我们来到大石上下行走的井道，望见它到了又去了。

"奇怪他没叫我们从那条路走，"比克利指着它说。

"我确信我非常庆幸他没想到，"巴斯廷答，"因为我确信，没有向导伊娃，我们再走不成那趟旅程。"

我望着他，他住了口。不知怎的，我此刻还受不了听别的唇说出她亲爱的名字。

随后我们进了她指给我们的通道，开始一段极可怕的旅程，照我们所能判断——我们已失去时间的准确计数——它费了我们六十个钟头上下。路倒是平滑无阻，可上坡陡得吓人、滑得吓人；以致我们常常不得不互相拉拽着往上爬，躺下来歇。

===Original===

Had it not been for those large, felt-covered bottles of Life-water, I am sure we should never have won through. But this marvelous elixir, drunk a little at a time, always reinvigorated us and gave us strength to push on. Also we had some food, and fortunately our spare oil held out, for the darkness in that tunnel was complete. Tommy became so exhausted that at length we must carry him by turns. He would have died had it not been for the water; indeed I thought that he was going to die.

After our last rest and a short sleep, however, he seemed to begin to recover, and generally there was something in his manner which suggested to us that he knew himself to be not far from the surface of the earth towards which we had crawled upwards for thousands upon thousands of feet, fortunately without meeting with any zone of heat which was not bearable.

We were right, for when we had staggered forward a little further, suddenly Tommy ran ahead of us and vanished. Then we heard him barking but where we could not see, since the tunnel appeared to take a turn and continue, but this time on a downward course, while the sound of the barks came from our right. We searched with the lanterns which were now beginning to die and found a little hole almost filled with fallen pieces of rock. We scooped these away with our hands, making an aperture large enough to creep through. A few more yards and we saw light, the blessed light of the moon, and in it stood Tommy barking hoarsely. Next we heard the sound of the sea. We struggled on desperately and presently pushed our way through bushes and vegetation on to a steep declivity. Down this we rolled and scrambled, to find ourselves at last lying upon a sandy beach, whilst above us the full moon shone in the heavens.

Here, with a prayer of thankfulness, we flung ourselves down and slept.

If it had not been for Tommy and we had gone further along the tunnel, which I have little doubt stretched on beneath the sea, where, I wonder, should we have slept that night?

When we woke the sun was shining high in the heavens. Evidently there had been rain towards the dawn, though as we were lying beneath the shelter of some broad-leaved tree, from it we had suffered little inconvenience. Oh! how beautiful, after our sojourn in those unholy caves, were the sun and the sea and the sweet air and the raindrops hanging on the leaves.

===Chinese===

倘若没有那几只毛毡包着的大瓶生命之水，我确信我们永远走不出去。可这奇妙的甘露，每次饮一点，总重新振作我们，给我们往前推的力气。我们还有些食物；幸而我们备用的油也撑住了，因为那隧道里的黑暗是十足的。汤米衰竭得厉害，末了我们只得轮流抱着它走。倘若没有那水，它必死；实在说，我以为它要死了。

然而末一次歇息、小睡片刻之后，它仿佛开始复原，而且一般说来，它的举止里有种东西向我们暗示：它知道自己离地面不远——我们朝上爬了几千英尺又几千英尺，幸而没有遇见任何熬不住的热区。

我们没有猜错，因为我们再踉跄着往前走了一点，汤米忽然跑到我们前头，不见了。随后我们听见它叫，却看不见在哪里，因为隧道仿佛一转弯又继续，这回却转为下坡，而犬吠声来自我们右侧。我们打着开始转暗的灯笼搜索，找到一个小洞，几乎被坠落的碎石塞满。我们用手把碎石扒开，扒出一个爬得过去的口子。再走几码，我们看见了光，蒙福的月光，月光里立着汤米，嘶哑地叫着。随后我们听见海的声音。我们拼命挣扎着往前，不久拨开灌木和草木，到了一道陡坡上。顺着坡我们连滚带爬，末了发觉自己躺在一片沙的海滩上，头顶上满月在天上照着。

在这里，我们带着感恩的祷告扑倒在地，睡了。

倘若不是为了汤米，倘若我们顺着隧道再往前——我毫不怀疑那隧道在海底之下延伸——我纳闷，那一夜我们该睡在哪里？

醒来时太阳已高照中天。分明黎明前后下过雨，不过我们躺在一片阔叶树的荫下，没受多少罪。哦！在那些不洁的洞穴里驻留之后，太阳、大海、甜美的空气、挂在叶子上的雨珠，多么美啊！

===Original===

We did not wake of ourselves; indeed if we had been left alone I am sure that we should have slept the clock round, for we were terribly exhausted. What woke us was the chatter of a crowd of Orofenans who were gathered at a distance from the tree and engaged in staring at us in a frightened way, also the barks of Tommy who objected to their intrusion. Among the people I recognised our old friend the chief Marama by his feather cloak, and sitting up, beckoned to him to approach. After a good deal of hesitation he came, walking delicately like Agag, and stopping from time to time to study us, as though he were not sure that we were real.

“What frightens you, Marama?” I asked him.

“You frighten us, O Friend-from-the-Sea. Whence did you and the Healer and the Bellower come and why do your faces look like those of ghosts and why is the little black beast so large-eyed and so thin? Over the lake we know you did not come, for we have watched day and night; moreover there is no canoe upon the shore. Also it would not have been possible.”

“Why not?” I asked idly.

“Come and see,” he answered.

Rising stiffly we emerged from beneath the tree and perceived that we were at the foot of the cliff against which the remains of the yacht had been borne by the great tempest. Indeed there it was within a couple of hundred yards of us.

Following Marama we climbed the sloping path which ran up the cliff and ascended a knoll whence we could see the lake and the cone of the volcano in its centre. At least we used to be able to see this cone, but now, at any rate with the naked eye, we could make out nothing, except a small brown spot in the midst of the waters of the lake.

“The mountain which rose up many feet in that storm which brought you to Orofena, Friend-from-the-Sea, has now sunk till only the very top of it is to be seen,” said Marama solemnly. “Even the Rock of Offerings has vanished beneath the water, and with it the house that we built for you.”

“Yes,” I said, affecting no surprise. “But when did that happen?”

“Five nights ago the world shook, Friend-from-the-Sea, and when the sun rose we saw that the mouth of the cave which appeared on the day of your coming, had vanished, and that the holy mountain itself had sunk deep, so that now only the crest of it is left above the water.”

“Such things happen,” I replied carelessly.

===Chinese===

我们不是自己醒来的；实在说倘若没人搅扰，我确信我们会睡满一整天，因为我们耗竭得厉害。惊醒我们的是一群奥罗费纳人的嘁喳——他们聚在离那棵树一段距离的地方，惊恐地瞪着我们——还有汤米的吠叫，它抗议他们闯进来。人群里我认出我们的老朋友酋长马拉玛，凭他那件羽毛斗篷；我坐起来，招手叫他过来。他踌躇了好一阵才来，走得斯斯文文，像亚甲，不时停步端详我们，仿佛拿不准我们是真是假。

"什么吓着你，马拉玛？"我问。

"是你们吓着我们，哦，海上来的朋友。您和大医师和大嗓门从哪里来？为什么你们的脸像鬼？为什么那小黑畜生眼睛这样大、瘦成这样？湖上我们知道你们没有来，因为我们日夜守望；况且岸上没有独木舟。再者，那也不可能。"

"为什么不可能？"我随口问。

"来看，"他答。

我们僵硬地立起身，从树下出来，发觉我们正在崖脚——游艇的残骸被那场大风送到崖边的地方。实在说，它离我们不过两百码。

跟着马拉玛，我们爬上崖壁上那条斜路，登上一座小丘，从那里望得见湖和湖心的火山锥。至少从前望得见；如今，至少肉眼望去，我们什么也认不出，只除了湖水当中一个小小的褐点。

"那座山——在那场把你带到奥罗费纳的风暴里升高了许多英尺的那座——海上来的朋友，如今沉下去了，只剩山顶露出一点，"马拉玛肃然说。"连献祭石也沉到水底下去了，连同我们给你们盖的房子。"

"是的，"我说，不露惊讶。"可那是什么时候发生的？"

"五夜之前，世界震动了，海上来的朋友；日头升起时，我们看见你来的那天出现的洞口不见了，圣山自己也沉得深了，如今只有山脊露在水上。"

"这类事是有的，"我漫不经心地答。

===Original===

“Yes, Friend-from-the-Sea. Like many other marvels they happen where you and your companions are. Therefore we beg you who can arise out of the earth like spirits, to leave us at once before our island and all of us who dwell thereon are drowned beneath the ocean. Leave us before we kill you, if indeed you be men, or die at your hands if, as we think, you be evil spirits who can throw up mountains and drag them down, and create gods that slay, and move about in the bowels of the world.”

“That is our intention, for our business here is done,” I answered calmly. “Come now and help us to depart. But first bring us food. Bring it in plenty, for we must victual our boat.”

Marama bowed and issued the necessary orders. Indeed food sufficient for our immediate needs was already there as an offering, and of it we ate with thankfulness.

Then we boarded the ship and examined the lifeboat. Thanks to our precautions it was still in very fair order and only needed some little caulking which we did with grass fibre and pitch from the stores. After this with the help of the Orofenans who worked hard in their desperate desire to be rid of us, we drew the boat into the sea, and provisioned her with stores from the ship, and with an ample supply of water. Everything being ready at last, we waited for the evening wind which always blew off shore, to start. As it was not due for half an hour or more, I walked back to the tree under which we had slept and tried to find the hole whence we had emerged from the tunnel on to the face of the cliff.

My hurried search proved useless. The declivity of the cliff was covered with tropical growth, and the heavy rain had washed away every trace of our descent, and very likely filled the hole itself with earth. At any rate, of it I could discover nothing. Then as the breeze began to blow I returned to the boat and here bade adieu to Marama, who gave me his feather cloak as a farewell gift.

“Goodbye, Friend-from-the-Sea,” he said to me. “We are glad to have seen you and thank you for many things. But we do not wish to see you any more.”

“Goodbye, Marama,” I answered. “What you say, we echo. At least you have now no great lump upon your neck and we have rid you of your wizards. But beware of the god Oro who dwells in the mountain, for if you anger him he will sink your island beneath the sea.”

===Chinese===

"是的，海上来的朋友。像别的许多奇迹一样，它们专在您和您的同伴所在的地方发生。所以我们求你们——能像精灵一样从地里钻出来的人——趁我们的岛、和我们岛上住的一切还没沉到大洋底下，立刻离开我们。趁我们还没杀你们——倘若你们真是人的话——离开我们；不然就死在你们手里，倘若照我们的猜想，你们是能把山掀起来又拖下去、造出杀人的神、在大地脏腑里来去的恶灵。"

"这原是我们的打算，因为我们在这里的事已完，"我平静地答。"来吧，帮我们离开。不过先给我们食物。多备些，我们要给船装给养。"

马拉玛行礼，发出必要的命令。实在说，够我们眼前吃的食物已经放在那里，是作献礼的；我们感恩地吃了。

随后我们上了船，察看救生艇。托我们的防备，它仍非常完好，只需一点小小的捻缝；我们用草纤维和库房里的沥青做了。这之后，在奥罗费纳人的帮助下——他们怀着摆脱我们的绝望的热望，干得非常卖力——我们把船拖下水，从船上取给养装了船，又装了充足的水。一切终于备齐，我们等傍晚的离岸风起航。离起风还有半个多钟头，我回到我们睡过的树下，试图找那个洞——我们从隧道里钻出来、上到崖面的那个洞。

我草草的搜索证明无用。崖坡覆着热带的草木，大雨把下降的痕迹冲得干干净净，多半把洞本身也灌了土。无论如何，它我一点也找不着。随后微风起了，我回到船上，在那里向马拉玛告别，他把他的羽毛斗篷作临别的礼物送了我。

"再会，海上来的朋友，"他对我说。"见了你们我们欢喜，又为许多事谢你们。可我们不愿意再见你们了。"

"再会，马拉玛，"我答。"你说的话，我们原样奉还。至少你脖子上如今没有大瘤子了，我们还替你们除了巫师。但要提防住在山里的神奥罗，倘若你们惹恼他，他会把你们的岛沉到海底。

===Original===

“And remember all that I have taught you,” shouted Bastin.

Marama shivered, though whether at the mention of the god Oro, of whose powers the Orofenans had so painful a recollection, or at the result of Bastin’s teachings, I do not know. And that was the last we shall ever see of each other in this world.

The island faded behind us and, sore at heart because of all that we had found and lost again, for three days we sailed northward with a fair and steady wind. On the fourth evening by an extraordinary stroke of fortune, we fell in with an American tramp steamer, trading from the South Sea Islands to San Francisco. To the captain, who treated us very kindly, we said simply that we were a party of Englishmen whose yacht had been wrecked on a small island several hundreds of miles away, of which we knew neither the name, if it had one, nor the position.

This story was accepted without question, for such things often happen in those latitudes, and in due course we were landed at San Francisco, where we made certain depositions before the British Consul as to the loss of the yacht *Star of the South*. Then we crossed America, having obtained funds by cable, and sailed for England in a steamer flying the flag of the United States.

Of the great war which made this desirable I do not speak since it has nothing, or rather little, to do with this history. In the end we arrived safely at Liverpool, and thence travelled to our homes in Devonshire.

---

Thus ended the history of our dealings with Oro, the superman who began his life more than two hundred and fifty thousand years ago, and with his daughter, Yva, whom Bastin still often calls the Glittering Lady.

===Chinese===

"还要记着我教过你们的一切，"巴斯廷喊道。

马拉玛打了个寒噤——是因为提起奥罗神呢，他的权力奥罗费纳人记得痛切；还是因为巴斯廷的教导的结果呢——我不知道。而那是我们在这世上彼此相见的最后一次。

岛在我们背后淡去；因为所寻见的、又失去的一切，我们心痛，乘着一阵顺而稳的风，向北航了三天。第四天傍晚，凭着非常的运气，我们遇上一艘美国不定期货船，从南海诸岛做买卖到旧金山去。我们只对那位待我们非常和气的船长说：我们是一伙英国人，游艇在几百英里外一座小岛上失事，那岛叫什么名字——倘若有名字——位于何处，我们一概不知道。

这个故事没经过盘问便被接受了，因为那几个纬度里这类事常有；到时候我们在旧金山上岸，就"南方之星"号游艇的损失向英国领事作了某些声明。随后我们横过美洲——电汇得来的款项——乘一艘挂美国旗的轮船回英国。

那场叫这一切成为必要的战争我不谈了，因为它同这段历史毫无关系，或不如说相干很少。末了我们平安到了利物浦，从那里回到我们德文郡的家。

---

我们同奥罗——那个在二十五万多年前开始他的生命的超人——的一切交涉，同他的女儿伊娃——巴斯廷至今还常叫她闪光夫人——的历史，就这样结束了。

## XXVII / 第二十七章
===Original===

Bastin Discovers a Resemblance

There is little more to tell.

Shortly after our return Bickley, like a patriotic Englishman, volunteered for service at the front and departed in the uniform of the R.A.M.C. Before he left he took the opportunity of explaining to Bastin how much better it was in such a national emergency as existed, to belong to a profession in which a man could do something to help the bodies of his countrymen that had been broken in the common cause, than to one like his in which it was only possible to pelt them with vain words.

“You think that, do you, Bickley?” answered Bastin. “Well, I hold that it is better to heal souls than bodies, because, as even you will have learned out there in Orofena, they last so much longer.”

“I am not certain that I learned anything of the sort,” said Bickley, “or even that Oro was more than an ordinary old man. He said that he had lived a thousand years, but what was there to prove this except his word, which is worth nothing?”

“There was the Lady Yva’s word also, which is worth a great deal, Bickley.”

“Yes, but she may have meant a thousand moons. Further, as according to her own showing she was still quite young, how could she know her father’s age?”

“Quite so, Bickley. But all she actually said was that she was of the same age as one of our women of twenty-seven, which may have meant two hundred and seventy for all I know. However, putting that aside you will admit that they had both slept for two hundred and fifty thousand years.”

“I admit that they slept, Bastin, because I helped to awaken them, but for how long there is nothing to show, except those star maps which are probably quite inaccurate.”

“They are not inaccurate,” I broke in, “for I have had them checked by leading astronomers who say that they show a marvelous knowledge of the heavens as these were two hundred and fifty thousand years ago, and are today.”

===Chinese===

巴斯廷发现一种相似

要讲的已经不多了。

我们回来后不久，比克利像一个爱国的英国人那样，志愿到前线服务，穿着皇家陆军军医队的军服走了。临走他抓住机会向巴斯廷解释：像眼前这样的国家危急关头，投身于一种能出力医治同胞的身体——他们在共同的事业里被打断了骨头——的行业，比投身于他那一行好得多，他那一行只能朝他们扔些空洞的字眼。

"你这样想，是不是，比克利？"巴斯廷答。"嗯，我认为医灵魂比医身体好，因为，连你在奥罗费纳也该学会了，灵魂比身体长久得多。"

"我不确信我学过这一类的任何东西，"比克利说，"甚至不确信奥罗比一个寻常老头多些什么。他说他活过一千年，可除了他的一句话——那话一文不值——有什么可作证？"

"还有伊娃夫人的话作证，那可值大钱，比克利。"

"是的，可她也许指的是一千个月。再说，照她自己招认的，她仍旧相当年轻，她怎么会知道她父亲的年纪？"

"很对，比克利。可她实际说的只是：她的年纪同我们二十七岁的女人相同；照我看，那也许指二百七十年。不过，把这撇开，你会承认他们两位都睡过二十五万年。"

"我承认他们睡过，巴斯廷，因为唤醒他们的也有我一份；至于睡了多久，除了那些星图——它们多半相当不准——没有凭据。"

"它们并不不准，"我插嘴，"因为我请过第一流的星象家核对过，他们说，那些图显示出对二十五千年前、也即今日的天象的惊人知识。

===Original===

Here I should state that those two metal maps and the ring which I gave to Yva and found again after the catastrophe, were absolutely the only things connected with her or with Oro that we brought away with us. The former I would never part with, feeling their value as evidence. Therefore, when we descended to the city Nyo and the depths beneath, I took them with me wrapped in cloth in my pocket. Thus they were preserved. Everything else went when the Rock of Offerings and the cave mouth sank beneath the waters of the lake.

This may have happened either in the earth tremor, which no doubt was caused by the advance of the terrific world-balance, or when the electric power, though diffused and turned by Yva’s insulated body, struck the great gyroscope’s travelling foot with sufficient strength, not to shift it indeed on to the right-hand path as Oro had designed, but still to cause it to stagger and even perhaps to halt for the fraction of a second. Even this pause may have been enough to cause convulsions of the earth above; indeed, I gathered from Marama and other Orofenans that such convulsions had occurred on and around the island at what must have corresponded with that moment of the loosing of the force.

This loss of our belongings in the house of the Rock of Offerings was the more grievous because among them were some Kodak photographs which I had taken, including portraits of Oro and one of Yva that was really excellent, to say nothing of pictures of the mouth of the cave and of the ruins and crater lake above. How bitterly I regret that I did not keep these photographs in my pocket with the map-plates.

“Even if the star-maps are correct, still it proves nothing,” said Bickley, “since possibly Oro’s astronomical skill might have enabled him to draw that of the sky at any period, though I allow this is impossible.”

“I doubt his taking so much trouble merely to deceive three wanderers who lacked the knowledge even to check them,” I said. “But all this misses the point, Bickley. However long they had slept, that man and woman did arise from seeming death. They did dwell in those marvelous caves with their evidences of departed civilisations, and they did show us that fearful, world-wandering gyroscope. These things we saw.”

===Chinese===

这里我该说明：那两幅金属星图，和那枚我给伊娃、又在灾变之后寻回的戒指，是我们带走的、与她或与奥罗相关的仅有的东西。前者我从不肯放手，因为觉出它们作为证据的价值。所以，我们下到尼俄城、下到更深的地方时，我把它们用布包着揣在口袋里。这样它们才保存下来。其余的一切，都随着献祭石和洞口沉入湖底而去了。

那沉没也许发生在地颤里——那颤无疑是那可怕的世界的天平行进而引起的；或者发生在电的伟力——它虽被伊娃绝缘的身体扩散、折转——以足够的力量打在那回旋器行进的脚上之时：那力量确实没能把它推上右边的路，如奥罗所谋，却仍足以叫它踉跄，甚至叫它停了一瞬的一分。连这一停也许已足以叫上面的地球痉挛；实在说，我从马拉玛和别的奥罗费纳人口中得知：就在该与力量释放的那一瞬相应的时刻，岛上和岛的四周发生过这样的痉挛。

我们在献祭石之屋里财物的丧失，尤其痛心，因为其中有几张我拍的柯达照片，包括奥罗的像，还有一张伊娃的，实在出色，更不必说洞口、废墟和上面那座火山湖的照片。我多么痛悔没把这些照片同星图板一起揣在口袋里。

"即便星图是真的，仍旧证明不了什么，"比克利说，"因为奥罗的天文本领兴许叫他画得出任何时代的星象，虽然我承认这不可能。"

"我疑心他不至于只为骗三个连核验都办不到的流浪者，费这么大的工夫，"我说。"可这一切都没打中要害，比克利。无论睡了多久，那一男一女的确从貌似死亡里起来了。他们确实住在那些奇妙洞穴里——那里留着湮灭文明的证据。他们确实把那台可怕的、行遍世界的回旋器放给我们看了。这些我们亲眼见过。

===Original===

“I admit that we saw them, Arbuthnot, and I admit that they are one and all beyond human comprehension. To that extent I am converted, and, I may add, humbled,” said Bickley.

“So you ought to be,” exclaimed Bastin, “seeing that you always swore that there was nothing in the world that is not capable of a perfectly natural explanation.”

“Of which all these things may be capable, Bastin, if only we held the key.”

“Very well, Bickley, but how do you explain what the Lady Yva did? I may tell you now what she commanded me to conceal at the time, namely, that she became a Christian; so much so that by her own will, I baptised and confirmed her on the very morning of her sacrifice. Doubtless it was this that changed her heart so much that she became willing, of course without my knowledge, to leave everything she cared for,” here he looked hard at me, “and lay down her life to save the world, half of which she believed was about to be drowned by Oro. Now, considering her history and upbringing, I call this a spiritual marvel, much greater than any you now admit, and one you can’t explain, Bickley.”

“No, I cannot explain, or, at any rate, I will not try,” he answered, also staring hard at me. “Whatever she believed, or did not believe, and whatever would or would not have happened, she was a great and wonderful woman whose memory I worship.”

“Quite so, Bickley, and now perhaps you see my point, that what you describe as mere vain words may also be helpful to mankind; more so, indeed, than your surgical instruments and pills.”

“You couldn’t convert Oro, anyway,” exclaimed Bickley, with irritation.

“No, Bickley; but then I have always understood that the devil is beyond conversion because he is beyond repentance. You see, I think that if that old scoundrel was not the devil himself, at any rate he was a bit of him, and, if I am right, I am not ashamed to have failed in his case.”

“Even Oro was not utterly bad, Bastin,” I said, reflecting on certain traits of mercy that he had shown, or that I dreamed him to have shown in the course of our mysterious midnight journeys to various parts of the earth. Also I remembered that he had loved Tommy and for his sake had spared our lives. Lastly, I do not altogether wonder that he came to certain hasty conclusions as to the value of our modern civilisations.

===Chinese===

"我承认我们见过，阿巴思诺特，我承认它们桩桩件件都超出人类的理解。在这个限度上我被转变了，我可以补一句，也被压服了，"比克利说。

"你也该被压服，"巴斯廷嚷道，"要知道你向来赌咒说，世上没有一样东西不能有一个十足自然的解释。"

"这一切也许都可能有自然的解释，巴斯廷，只消我们握着钥匙。"

"很好，比克利，可你怎么解释伊娃夫人做的事？我可以现在告诉你她当时吩咐我隐瞒的事，即：她成了基督徒；到了这样地步，出于她自己的意愿，我在她牺牲的那个早晨为她行了洗礼和坚振礼。无疑正是这改变了她的心，叫她甘愿——自然背着我的知情——舍掉她所珍爱的一切，"他重重地看了我一眼，"舍掉性命去救世界——她相信半个世界就要叫奥罗淹掉。现在，考虑到她的历史与教养，我称这是属灵的奇迹，大过你如今承认的任何一件，而且是解释不了的，比克利。"

"不，我解释不了，或说无论如何不愿尝试，"他答，也重重地盯着我。"无论她信还是不信，无论会发生还是不会发生，她是一位伟大而奇妙的女人，她的记忆是我所敬拜的。"

"正是，比克利，如今也许你明白我的意思了：你描写作空洞字眼的东西，也许同样有益于人类；实在说，比你的手术刀和药丸更有益。"

"反正你转变不了奥罗，"比克利恼火地嚷道。

"不，比克利；不过我一向明白，魔鬼是转变不了的，因为他不肯悔改。你瞧，我想那个老恶棍倘若不是魔鬼本尊，至少也算他的一小片；倘若我看得不错，我在他身上失败，并不引以为耻。"

"连奥罗也不是全然的坏，巴斯廷，"我说，回想起他露出的某些仁慈的性情，或我在我们那些神秘的午夜旅程里梦见他露出的。我也记得他爱汤米，为它的缘故饶了我们的命。末了，我并不全然奇怪，他对我们现代文明的价值会得出某些仓促的结论。

===Original===

“I am very glad to hear it, Humphrey, since while there is a spark left the whole fire may burn up again, and I believe that to the Divine mercy there are no limits, though Oro will have a long road to travel before he finds it. And now I have something to say. It has troubled me very much that I was obliged to leave those Orofenans wandering in a kind of religious twilight.”

“You couldn’t help that,” said Bickley, “seeing that if you had stopped, by now you would have been wandering in religious light.”

“Still, I am not sure that I ought not to have stopped. I seem to have deserted a field that was open to me. However, it can’t be helped, since it is certain that we could never find that island again, even if Oro has not sunk it beneath the sea, as he is quite capable of doing, to cover his tracks, so to speak. So I mean to do my best in another field by way of atonement.”

“You are not going to become a missionary?” I said.

“No, but with the consent of the Bishop, who, I think, believes that my locum got on better in the parish than I do, as no doubt was the case, I, too, have volunteered for the Front, and been accepted as a chaplain of the 201st Division.”

“Why, that’s mine!” said Bickley.

“Is it? I am very glad, since now we shall be able to pursue our pleasant arguments and to do our best to open each other’s minds.”

“You fellows are more fortunate than I am,” I remarked. “I also volunteered, but they wouldn’t take me, even as a Tommy, although I misstated my age. They told me, or at least a specialist whom I saw did afterwards, that the blow I got on the head from that sorcerer’s boy﻿—”

“I know, I know!” broke in Bickley almost roughly. “Of course, things might go wrong at any time. But with care you may live to old age.”

“I am sorry to hear it,” I said with a sigh, “at least I think I am. Meanwhile, fortunately there is much that I can do at home; indeed a course of action has been suggested to me by an old friend who is now in authority.”

---

===Chinese===

"听见这话我很欢喜，汉弗莱，因为只要还剩一粒火星，整堆火还可以重新烧起来；我相信神的怜悯没有限量，虽然奥罗找到它之前还有长路要走。好，现在我有一句话。我心里一直很难受，因为我不得不丢下那些奥罗费纳人，让他们在一种宗教的蒙昧里游荡。"

"你也没法子，"比克利说，"要知道你倘若留下，如今游荡在宗教的光里的便是你。"

"虽然如此，我不拿准我是不是不该留下。我仿佛抛弃了一片向我敞开的园地。然而没法子，因为我们永远找不着那个岛了，即便奥罗没有把它沉到海底——那是他做得到的，可以说，为了掩他的足迹。所以我打算在另一片园地尽力，作为补偿。"

"你不是要去当传教士吧？"我说。

"不，不过得了主教的同意——我想他也觉得我的代理人在教区比我干得好，无疑实情如此——我也志愿到前线去了，并被接纳为第201师的随军牧师。"

"嗳，那正是我的师！"比克利说。

"是吗？我很欢喜，如今我们可以继续我们愉快的争论，尽力互相开导了。"

"你们两位都比我幸运，"我说。"我也志愿过，可他们不肯收，连当一名小兵也不收，虽然我把年纪报小了。他们告诉我——或者至少后来替我看的一位专家告诉我——我的头挨过那个巫师的孩子一棒——"

"我知道，我知道！"比克利几乎粗声地插进来。"自然，任何时候都可能出岔子。可是当心些，你可以活到老。"

"听见这话我难过，"我叹口气说，"至少我想我是难过。同时，幸好在家里我还有很多可做的事；实在说，有一位如今掌权的故交，向我建议了一条行事的路径。"

---

===Original===

Once more Bickley and Bastin in their war-stained uniforms were dining at my table and on the very night of their return from the Front, which was unexpected. Indeed Tommy nearly died of joy on hearing their voices in the hall. They, who played a worthy part in the great struggle, had much to tell me, and naturally their more recent experiences had overlaid to some extent those which we shared in the mysterious island of Orofena. Indeed we did not speak of these until, just as they were going away, Bastin paused beneath a very beautiful portrait of my late wife, the work of an artist famous for his power of bringing out the inner character, or what some might call the soul, of the sitter. He stared at it for a while in his shortsighted way, then said: “Do you know, Arbuthnot, it has sometimes occurred to me, and never more than at this moment, that although they were different in height and so on, there was a really curious physical resemblance between your late wife and the Lady Yva.”

“Yes,” I answered. “I think so too.”

Bickley also examined the portrait very carefully, and as he did so I saw him start. Then he turned away, saying nothing.

---

Such is the summary of all that has been important in my life. It is, I admit, an odd story and one which suggests problems that I cannot solve. Bastin deals with such things by that acceptance which is the privilege and hallmark of faith; Bickley disposes, or used to dispose, of them by a blank denial which carries no conviction, and least of all to himself.

What is life to most of us who, like Bickley, think ourselves learned? A round, short but still with time and to spare wherein to be dull and lonesome; a fateful treadmill to which we were condemned we know not how, but apparently through the casual passions of those who went before us and are now forgotten, causing us, as the Bible says, to be born in sin; up which we walk wearily we know not why, seeming never to make progress; off which we fall outworn we know not when or whither.

===Chinese===

比克利和巴斯廷又穿着染上战痕的军服在我的桌上用饭了，而且恰在他们从前线回来的那一夜——出人意外。实在说，汤米听见门厅里他们的嗓音，欢喜得差点儿死掉。他们在这场大战里担当过一份配得的角色，有许多话对我讲；自然，他们较近的经历，多少盖过了我们在奥罗费纳神秘岛上共享的种种。实在说，直到他们将要告辞的时候，我们才提起这些；那时巴斯廷在我亡妻的一幅非常美的画像下面停了步——那幅画出自一位名家之手，以能显出坐者的内在性格、或有人所谓的灵魂著称。他照他近视的样子端详了一会儿，说："你知道吗，阿巴思诺特，有时——从来没有比此刻更甚——我想起，虽然她们的高矮等等不同，你的亡妻同伊娃夫人之间，有一种当真离奇的相貌相似。"

"是的，"我答。"我也这样想。"

比克利也把那幅画像细细察看，看的时候我见他一震。随后他转过身去，一言不发。

---

这便是我的平生一切大事的梗概。我承认，这是一个古怪的故事，一个引出我无法解答的问题的故事。巴斯廷应付这类事，用的是那种领受——那是信仰的特权和印记；比克利处理它们——或者说从前处理——用的是一概否认，那否认不能说服谁，最说服不了的是他自己。

生命，对我们这些自以为博学的人——像比克利——是什么？一个圆环，短促，却仍有余暇叫人沉闷孤寂；一个命定的踏车，我们不知怎样被判上去踩——分明是经我们前人的随意情欲，那些人如今已被遗忘——照圣经的说法，叫我们生在罪里；我们在上面疲惫地走，不知为什么，仿佛永无寸进；我们力竭跌落，不知何时、也不知跌向哪里。

===Original===

Such upon the surface it appears to be, nor in fact does our ascertained knowledge, as Bickley would sum it up, take us much further. No prophet has yet arisen who attempted to define either the origin or the reasons of life. Even the very Greatest of them Himself is quite silent on this matter. We are tempted to wonder why. Is it because life as expressed in the higher of human beings, is, or will be too vast, too multiform and too glorious for any definition which we could understand? Is it because in the end it will involve for some, if not for all, majesty on unfathomed majesty, and glory upon unimaginable glory such as at present far outpass the limits of our thought?

The experiences which I have recorded in these pages awake in my heart a hope that this may be so. Bastin is wont, like many others, to talk in a light fashion of Eternity without in the least comprehending what he means by that gigantic term. It is not too much to say that Eternity, something without beginning and without end, and involving, it would appear, an everlasting changelessness, is a state beyond human comprehension. As a matter of fact we mortals do not think in constellations, so to speak, or in aeons, but by the measures of our own small earth and of our few days thereon. We cannot really conceive of an existence stretching over even one thousand years, such as that which Oro claimed and the Bible accords to a certain early race of men, omitting of course his two thousand five hundred centuries of sleep. And yet what is this but one grain in the hourglass of time, one day in the lost record of our earth, of its sisters the planets and its father the sun, to say nothing of the universes beyond?

It is because I have come in touch with a prolonged though perfectly finite existence of the sort, that I try to pass on the reflections which the fact of it awoke in me. There are other reflections connected with Yva and the marvel of her love and its various manifestations which arise also. But these I keep to myself. They concern the wonder of woman’s heart, which is a microcosm of the hopes and fears and desires and despairs of this humanity of ours whereof from age to age she is the mother.

Humphrey Arbuthnot.

===Chinese===

从表面看它便是这样；而照比克利会作的总结，我们查明的知识，其实也带我们走不了多远。还没有一位先知起来，试图解释生命的起源或缘由。连他们中间至大者，祂自己也在这件事上全然缄默。我们禁不住要问为什么。是不是因为生命——照它在人类中较高者身上的表达——是、或将是一个太浩瀚、太多样、太荣耀的东西，不是我们所能理解的定义装得下的？是不是因为到末了，它要为一些人——即便不为全体——带来威严之上的威严、荣耀之上的荣耀，那些荣耀远超我们此刻思想的边界，无以测度？

我在这几页里记下的经历，在我心里唤起一种希望：也许正是如此。巴斯廷向来同许多人一样，轻飘飘地谈永恒，对那个巨大的词他是什么意思，根本不懂。说永恒——一件无始无终的东西，它看来还牵涉一种万古不易——是超出人类理解的一种境界，并不算过分。实在说，我们这些凡人思想的尺度，可以说，不是星座，不是万古，而是我们的小小地球，和我们在它上面的短短的几天。我们其实想象不出甚至一千年的存在——奥罗自称的那种，圣经记给某支早期人类的——姑且不提他那两千五百个世纪的睡眠。然而这算什么？不过是时间沙漏里的一粒沙，不过是我们的地球的湮灭了的记载里的一天，是它的姊妹行星们和它的父太阳的记载里的一天，更不必说外面的诸宇宙了。

正因为我自己接触过这样一段绵长、却彻底有限的存在，我才试着把我因这事实而起的感想传下去。还有别的感想，关乎伊娃，关乎她的爱的奇迹和它的种种显现。可这些我留着不说。它们关乎女人之心的奇迹；那心是这一切人类的希望与恐惧、欲望与绝望的小宇宙——而一代又一代，她是这一切的母亲。

汉弗莱·阿巴思诺特

## Epilogue / 尾声
===Original===

Note by J. R. Bickley, M.R.C.S.

Within about six months of the date on which he wrote the last words of this history of our joint adventures, my dear friend, Humphrey Arbuthnot, died suddenly, as I had foreseen that probably he would do, from the results of the injury he received in the island of Orofena.

He left me the sole executor to his will, under which he divided his property into three parts. One third he bequeathed to me, one third (which is strictly tied up) to Bastin, and one third to be devoted, under my direction, to the advancement of Science.

His end appears to have been instantaneous, resulting from an effusion of blood upon the brain. When I was summoned I found him lying dead by the writing desk in his library at Fulcombe Priory. He had been writing at the desk, for on it was a piece of paper on which appear these words: “*I have seen her. I*﻿—” There the writing ends, not stating whom he thought he had seen in the moments of mental disturbance or delusion which preceded his decease.

Save for certain verbal corrections, I publish this manuscript without comment as the will directs, only adding that it sets out our mutual experiences very faithfully, though Arbuthnot’s deductions from them are not always my own.

I would say also that I am contemplating another visit to the South Sea Islands, where I wish to make some further investigations. I dare say, however, that these will be barren of results, as the fountain of Life-water is buried forever, nor, as I think, will any human being stand again in the Hades-like halls of Nyo. It is probable also that it would prove impossible to rediscover the island of Orofena, if indeed that volcanic land still remains above the waters of the deep.

Now that he is a very wealthy man, Bastin talks of accompanying me for purposes quite different from my own, but on the whole I hope he will abandon this idea. I may add that when he learned of his unexpected inheritance he talked much of the “deceitfulness of riches,” but that he has not as yet taken any steps to escape their golden snare. Indeed he now converses of his added “opportunities of usefulness,” I gather in connection with missionary enterprise.

J. R. Bickley.

===Chinese===

J. R. 比克利（皇家外科医师公会会员）附记

这部我们共同冒险的历史的末一句写定之后，不出六个月，我的挚友汉弗莱·阿巴思诺特突然去世——照我预料的可能——死于他在奥罗费纳岛上所受的伤的后患。

他立我为遗嘱的唯一执行人；照遗嘱，他把产业分作三份。一份赠我，一份（严格地锁定了的）赠巴斯廷，一份在我的指导下用于推进科学。

他的结局仿佛是刹那间的，起于脑溢血。我被召去时，发现他倒在富尔科姆修道院旧宅藏书室的写字台旁，已经死去。他方才正在写字，因为桌上有一页纸，上面写着这几个字："*我看见她了。我——*"文字便到此为止，没有说明他在去世之前那阵心智的纷乱或错觉的时刻里，以为自己看见了谁。

除若干文字上的订正外，我照遗嘱的指示发表这部手稿，不加评论，只补一句：它极其忠实地记述了我们共同的经历，虽然阿巴思诺特从中引出的推论，未必尽同于我。

我还要说，我正打算再到南海诸岛去一趟，想作一些进一步的考察。不过我敢说，这些考察不会有什么结果，因为生命之水的泉源已经永远埋葬，而照我看，也再不会有任何人重进尼俄那冥府般的殿堂。多半也不可能再寻见奥罗费纳岛，倘若那片火山的土地当真还浮在深海的水面上的话。

如今他已是很富的人，巴斯廷谈着要陪我同去，目的却同我的全然两样；总的说，我希望他打消这个主意。我可以补一句：他得知这笔意外的遗产时，谈了许多"钱财的迷惑"，可他至今没有采取任何步骤，逃出那黄金的罗网。实在说他如今谈的是他添了的"行善的机会"，我领会，那是同传教事业连在一起说的。

J. R. 比克利

===Original===

P.S.﻿—I forgot to state that the spaniel Tommy died within three days of his owner. The poor little beast was present in the room at the time of Arbuthnot’s passing away, and when found seemed to be suffering from shock. From that moment Tommy refused food and finally was discovered quite dead and lying by the body on Marama’s feather cloak, which Arbuthnot often used as a dressing-gown. As Bastin raised some religious objections, I arranged without his knowledge that the dog’s ashes should rest not far from those of the master and mistress whom it loved so well.

J. R. B.

===Chinese===

又及——我忘了交代：那只小獚犬汤米死在它主人之后三天。阿巴思诺特咽气的时候，这可怜的小畜生正在屋里，被发现时仿佛受了惊。从那一刻起汤米拒绝进食，末了人们发觉它已经死了，躺在遗体旁边，躺在马拉玛那件羽毛斗篷上——阿巴思诺特常拿它当晨衣用。因为巴斯廷提出若干宗教上的异议，我便瞒着他安排了：把这狗的骨灰安放在它深爱的主人夫妇的骨灰不远处。

J. R. B.

-

It is fortunate that we did not accept Yva’s offer. Had we done so we should have found ourselves shut in, and perished, as shall be told. —﻿H. A. ↩︎

===Chinese===

-

幸而我们没有接受伊娃的提议。倘若接受了，我们便会发现自己被封在里面，因而丧命，后文自有交代。——H. A. ↩︎
